# Adolescent Idiopathic Scoliosis

*Spine · 1 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — coronal deformity, **Cobb > 10°**, age **10–18 yrs**; **commonest type of scoliosis**

**Incidence** — **3%** for 10–20° curves · **0.3%** for curves > 30°

**Sex ratio** — **1:1** small curves · **10:1 female** for curves > 30°

**Commonest pattern** — **right thoracic** curve + **thoracic hypokyphosis** (T5–T12 ≈ 8°; normal 20–50°)\
✗ Wrong options: right thoracic + hyperkyphosis · left thoracic

**Family history** — most patients **positive**

### Aetiology

**Pathophysiology** — **unknown / multifactorial**: hormonal (**melatonin**), brain stem, proprioception, platelet, **calmodulin**

**Neurocentral synchondrosis (NCS)** — cartilaginous plate between **centrum + posterior neural arches**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior arches

| NCS closure order | Age |
|---|---|
| **Cervical** | 5–6 yrs |
| **Lumbar** | 11–12 yrs (\~100% closed at age 10) |
| **Thoracic** | **14–17 yrs** (\~50% closed at age 10) |

**NCS order trap** — **cervical → lumbar → thoracic**\
✗ Wrong options: cervical → thoracic → lumbar

### Skeletal maturity & progression risk

**Best predictor of progression** — curve magnitude at **peak height velocity (PHV)**; **> 30° at PHV** → **> 70%** reach surgical range\
✗ Wrong options: > 20° at menarche · > 30° at Risser 2 · skeletal age 12

**PHV timing** — **before menarche** and **before Risser 1** (occurs during **Risser 0**) · menarche ≈ 1 yr after PHV · girls mature **\~1.5 yrs (18 months) after menarche**

**Skeletal method best matching curve acceleration / PHV** — **Tanner-Whitehouse III RUS** (radius, ulna, metacarpals + phalanges of digits 1, 3, 5) (✗ Wrong options: Greulich-Pyle · Oxford · Risser · Lenke) · TW **stage III** (✗ Wrong options: stage 2)

**PHV correlates closest with (TO)** — **olecranon apophysis fusion**; olecranon **stage 5 = PHV** (stage 1 = 3.0 yrs before, stage 7 = 3.4 yrs after)\
✗ Wrong options: triradiate closure · Risser 1 · menarche

**Other PHV surrogates (TO)** — modified Oxford score **19–21** · calcaneal apophysis **stage 3** · humeral head ossification between **stages 2–3** · Sauvegrain elbow score · TW2 = most accurate/reproducible, GP = simpler, less reliable in Asians

| Risk factor for progression | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Curve magnitude (immature)** | **> 25°** before maturity → continues to progress |
| **Curve magnitude (mature)** | Thoracic **> 50°** · lumbar **> 40°** → progress **1–2°/yr** |
| **Remaining growth** | Age **< 12 yrs** · Tanner **< 3** (F) · **Risser 0–1** · **open triradiate cartilage** · PHV |
| **Curve type** | Thoracic > lumbar · double > single |

| Risser | Iliac apophysis | Growth |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | No ossification | Covers **first 2/3 of pubertal spurt** → **greatest velocity of linear growth**; highest progression risk with open triradiate |
| **I** | 25% | Velocity descending |
| **II** | 50% | GT apophysis unites; **\~3 cm sitting growth** left, no more LL growth |
| **III** | 75% | \~2 cm sitting growth |
| **IV** | 100% ossified, not fused | \~1 cm sitting growth; = **skeletal maturity** for bracing |
| **V** | Fused | Complete maturity |

![Risser staging of the iliac apophysis](../../MCQ/attachments/q4696-illa-ossification-of-the-iliac-apophysis-creates-risser.jpg)

**Open triradiate + < 18 h brace wear** — factor **most correlated with progression** (Karol) → Risser 0 needs **≥ 18 h/day**\
✗ Wrong options: curves < 25° · Risser 1 · Sanders 6

**Karol – progression to ≥ 50° in brace** — Risser 0 **44.2%** · Risser 1 **6.9%** · Risser 2 **0%**

### Natural history into adulthood

**Untreated AIS (Weinstein 50-yr)** — ↑ **chronic back pain** (✓)\
✗ Wrong options: difficult vaginal delivery / ↑ caesarean · pulmonary dysfunction at 38°

**Cobb > 60° at maturity** — ↑ **acute + chronic back pain** vs controls\
✗ Wrong options: premature death · disability · depression · ADL limitation

**Curves < 30° at maturity** — rarely progress · thoracic **> 50°** most likely to progress

**Curves > 90°** — cardiopulmonary dysfunction, early death, pain, ↓ self-image

## 3 Presentation

**School screening** — **7° on scoliometer** (Adams forward bend) abnormal → correlates with **\~20° Cobb**

**Adams forward bending** — axial rotation (rib prominence) = **structural** curve · **sitting** forward bend eliminates **leg-length inequality**

**Back pain** — 25–30% of all adolescents; mild, intermittent, non-limiting + typical right curve → **stretching + lighter backpack** · atypical pain → MRI / bone scan\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · bone scan · TLSO for 20° · pain clinic

### Atypical features requiring MRI

| Exam finding (non-idiopathic) | Suggests |
|---|---|
| **Hairy patch** | Spina bifida / **diastematomyelia** |
| Subcutaneous tumours · pigmented lesions · **café-au-lait** | **Neurofibromatosis** |
| Excessive flexibility | **Ehlers-Danlos / Marfan** |
| Joint contractures | CP / arthrogryposis |
| Craniofacial anomalies | Syndromic scoliosis |
| Limb weakness | Neuromuscular scoliosis |
| **Cavovarus foot** | Neural axis anomaly → MRI |
| **Asymmetric abdominal reflexes** | **Syringomyelia** (curve with little rotation) |

**Truncal shift (TO)** — loss of coronal balance in **large idiopathic** curves → NOT a sign of non-idiopathic scoliosis (EXCEPT answer)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**First films (TO)** — **standing PA + lateral whole spine**; PA = ↓ breast radiation (AP gives better quality)\
✗ Wrong options: standing AP · supine · segment only

**Cobb angle** — lines along the **most tilted** end vertebrae · **> 10° = scoliosis** · error **3–5°**

| Radiographic term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Coronal balance** | **C7 plumb line** vs **central sacral vertical line (CSVL)** |
| **Sagittal balance** | C7 plumb vs posterosuperior corner of S1 |
| **Stable zone** | Between vertical lines from lumbosacral facets |
| **Stable vertebra** | Most proximal vertebra most closely **bisected by CSVL** |
| **Neutral vertebra** | **Rotationally neutral** (spinous process equidistant from pedicles) |
| **End vertebra** | **Most tilted** from horizontal apical vertebra |
| **Apical vertebra** | Disc / vertebra **deviated farthest** from centre |
| **Touched vertebra (TV)** | **Last cephalad vertebra touched by CSVL** |
| **Clavicle angle** | **Best predictor of post-op shoulder balance** |

**MRI** — **posterior fossa → conus**; rules out Chiari / syrinx / cord cyst / tether

| MRI indicated | NOT an MRI indication |
|---|---|
| **Left thoracic curve** | **Left thoracolumbar / lumbar curve** (TO – common pattern) |
| Short angular curve · **apical kyphosis** / excessive kyphosis | Age 10–18 |
| **Rapid progression** | Risser stage |
| Neurologic signs, pain (atypical) | Curve magnitude alone (without rapid progression) |
| **Asymmetric abdominal reflexes** · foot deformity (cavovarus) | Mild activity-related back pain |
| **Male**, structural abnormality | Routine initial work-up |

**Left thoracic curve in a neurologically normal child** — **total spine MRI** = next step\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · PT · bending films · CT

![Syringomyelia on sagittal MRI — reason for MRI in a left thoracic curve](../../MCQ/attachments/q740-illa-mri-cervical-sagital-shows-syringomyelia.jpg)

### Lenke classification

**Components (TO)** — **curve type 1–6** + **lumbar modifier A/B/C** + **sagittal (thoracic) modifier −/N/+**\
✗ Wrong options: "thoracic modifier A/B/C"

**Regions** — proximal thoracic: apex **T3–T5** · main thoracic: apex **T6 → T11–12 disc** · thoracolumbar: apex **T12–L1** · lumbar: apex **L1–2 disc → L4**

**Major curve** — largest Cobb, always structural

**Structural minor curve** — residual **≥ 25° on supine side-bending** OR kyphosis **≥ 20°** (T2–T5 for PT; **T10–L2** for TL/L)

| Lenke type | Name | Structural curves |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Main thoracic | MT only |
| **2** | **Double thoracic** (TO) | **MT (major) + PT** structural; TL/L non-structural |
| **3** | Double major | MT (major) + TL/L structural; PT non-structural |
| **4** | Triple major | All three structural |
| **5** | TL/L | TL/L only (major) |
| **6** | TL/L–MT | TL/L major (≥ 5° > MT) + MT structural |

| Lumbar modifier | CSVL at lumbar apex |
|---|---|
| **A** | Between pedicles |
| **B** | Touches apical pedicle |
| **C** | Completely medial to apical body/pedicle |

| Sagittal modifier (T5–T12) | Kyphosis |
|---|---|
| **−** | **< 10°** (hypokyphosis) |
| **N** | **10–40°** |
| **+** | **> 40°** (hyperkyphosis) |

![Lenke classification summary table](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356190-8.png)

**King-Moe** — 5 thoracic curve types for **Harrington** instrumentation; coronal only, no sagittal plane → replaced by Lenke

## 5 Management

```
AIS by Cobb angle + maturity
├─ < 25° (any maturity) → OBSERVE, serial X-rays
├─ 25–45°, Risser 0–2 (flexible, immature) → BRACE
│      (goal = stop progression, not correct)
├─ 25–45°, Risser 4–5 / mature → OBSERVE
└─ > 45–50° → POSTERIOR SPINAL FUSION
       └─ Very young (Risser 0, girls < 10, boys < 13) / > 75° / stiff → ANTERIOR + POSTERIOR
```

### Bracing – indications, wear time & efficacy

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| **Cobb range** | **25–45°** (Orthobullets) · **20–40°** (OB) · **20–25° with documented progression** → brace — read the options |
| **Maturity** | **Risser 0, 1, 2** · Sanders 1–3 · age 10–15 |
| **Prescribed** | **16–23 h/day** until maturity or surgery |
| **Minimum effective (actual)** | **> 12 h/day** (12.9 h → **90–93% success**, BrAIST) (✗ Wrong options: 6–8 h · 9–10 h) |
| **Dose-response** | **17.7 h → 93%**; > 12 h → 82% no progression; < 7 h → 69% progressed; prescribed 16 vs 23 h had no effect |
| **Effect** | **50% ↓ need for surgery** · **NNT = 4** (compliant) |
| **Best predictor of brace success** | **Time in brace** (✗ Wrong options: in-brace correction · PT first) |

**Brace success / failure (TO)** — success = **< 5°** progression · failure = **≥ 6°** progression at discontinuation, **> 45°** before/at maturity, or switch to surgery\
✗ Wrong options: "success = correction > 5°"

**Skeletal maturity = stop brace** — **Risser 4** · **< 1 cm height change over 2 visits 6 months apart** · **2 yrs post-menarche**

**Poor brace prognosis** — poor in-brace correction · **hypokyphosis** (relative CI) · **male** · **obese** · non-compliant

| Brace (TO) | Curve apex |
|---|---|
| **Milwaukee (CTLSO)** | **Above T7** (TO, Orthobullets) · at/proximal to **T6** (OB) · double thoracic / apex above T8 do not respond to TLSO |
| **TLSO / Boston (underarm)** | **T7 or below** (e.g. apex T12) |
| **Charleston bending** | **Night-time** only |

**Sanders staging** — **AP hand X-ray** + curve magnitude → risk of progression **> 50° despite bracing** in **Lenke 1 + 3** curves · stage 3 = **complete capping of phalangeal epiphyses**

| 25° curve + Sanders stage | Risk > 50° despite brace |
|---|---|
| **1** | **99%** |
| **3** | **29%** → brace most likely to change natural history (✓) |
| **4–8** | **0%** |
| 45° curve | \~100% |

![Sanders skeletal maturity staging (hand radiograph)](../../MCQ/attachments/q8775-illa-sanders-classification.jpg)

**Bracing traps** — **scoliosis-specific exercises / PT** not effective · spinal manipulation unsupported · 12° curve with 6° progression → observe · Risser 4 / Sanders 7 + 30° → observe · boy with 50° → surgery · bracing large (> 45°) curves can still **delay** surgery to maturity · congenital curve → no bracing

### Fusion level selection

**PSF indications** — Cobb **> 45°** (Orthobullets) / **> 50°** (OB) · **gold standard** for thoracic + double major curves · usable for all idiopathic types

**ASF** — **thoracolumbar / lumbar** curves with **normal sagittal profile**; saves lumbar levels; fuse **end → end vertebra**; not if **thoracic hyperkyphosis > 40°** (↑ pseudarthrosis)

| Technique | Fusion levels |
|---|---|
| **Typical** | Proximal end vertebra → 1–2 levels cephalad to **stable** vertebra; double/triple major → **distal end vertebra** |
| **Harrington** | 1 above + 2 below end vertebrae if within **stable zone** |
| **Moe** | To **neutral** vertebra |
| **Lenke** | All major curves + minor curves that are **stiff or kyphotic** |
| **Touched vertebra** | Fuse **to or caudad to TV** → best correction; cephalad to TV → **truncal shift**, poor long-term outcome |

![Touched vertebra: last cephalad vertebra touched by CSVL](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2053-determination-of-tv.jpg)

**Avoid L4 / L5** — Cochran: ↑ **low back pain** with fusion to **L5** (less with L4) · pelvis almost **never** needed in AIS

**Lenke 1B (65° MT, flexible PT + lumbar)** — **PSF of thoracic curve only**\
✗ Wrong options: T2–L4 · brace · stapling · A/P

**Selective thoracic fusion** — **AVT ratio** = thoracic AVT (C7 plumb → thoracic apex) ÷ TL/L AVT (CSVL → lumbar apex); **> 1.2** → selective thoracic OK (e.g. 45/15 = **3.0** ✓)

**LIV to prevent DJK / adding-on** — include **stable sagittal vertebra** (≥ 50% of body anterior to **posterior sacral vertical line**) · **Lenke 1A-R (L4 tilted right)** → ↑ adding-on · e.g. TL kyphosis T8–L2 with L3 the first SSV → **LIV = L3**\
✗ Wrong options: T12 · L2 · L5 · pelvis

**Isolated long PSF indicated** — **13-yr Risser 3 with 55°**\
✗ Wrong options: JIS → needs anterior too · IIS → Mehta cast · hemivertebra + bar → bar excision · 27° → brace

**Vertebral body stapling** — small thoracic curves **< 35°**, immature

### Surgical technique

**Pedicle screws** — insertional torque ∝ **pullout resistance**; ↑ by **undertapping 1 mm** + **straightforward trajectory**; not tapping may be stronger still · segmental screws → ↑ coronal correction, ↓ need for anterior release\
✗ Wrong options: anatomic trajectory ↓ torque

**Pedicle subtraction osteotomy (PSO)** — posterior closing wedge hinged on **anterior column**, usually **L2 / L3** · commonest complication **pseudarthrosis 25–30%** · 1 PSO ≈ **3 Smith-Petersen** osteotomies, more blood loss\
✗ Wrong options: cord ischaemia · aorta < 1–2% · neuro deficit < 10%

**Opioid-sparing** — **short-course post-op steroids** ↓ opioid use\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · avoiding drain (drains ↓ opioids) · delaying PT · pre-op opioids ↑ use

**ED visits after PSF** — \~10%, mostly minor → **older age + more fusion levels**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI · Lenke type · ERAS ↓ visits · pelvic fixation/Ponte

### Neuromonitoring

**Standard of care** — **SSEP + MEP**; MEPs warn earliest of cord dysfunction, change faster than SSEPs

**Alert thresholds** — amplitude drop **> 50%** (Orthobullets) · critical: **MEP 80%**, **SSEP 50% / latency 10%** (OB)

```
Neuromonitoring signal drop
├─ Technical / anaesthetic cause? → CHECK leads + IV agents
├─ Not yet critical (e.g. MEP 60%, SSEP 30%) → REPOSITION, raise BP, check Hb
│      (do not deepen anaesthesia — suppresses MEPs)
└─ Persistent / critical → REVERSE CORRECTION
       └─ Still lost → STAGNARA WAKE-UP TEST → remove implants if spine stable
```

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Neurologic injury** | Paraplegia **1:1000**; ↑ with **kyphosis**, excessive correction, **sublaminar wires** |
| **Pseudarthrosis** (1–2%) | Late pain, progression, **rod breakage**; asymptomatic + no loss of correction → observe |
| **Infection** (1–2%) | **Late pain**, clean wound; **P. acnes** commonest delayed organism (**2-week** culture); I&D + retain hardware if not loose and < 6 months |
| **Flat back** | Loss of lumbar lordosis → fatigue + back pain; rare with sagittal rod contouring; Rx **posterior closing wedge osteotomies** (± anterior release) |
| **Crankshaft** | Continued **anterior growth** after **PSF alone** in very young → rotation; prevent with **anterior discectomy + fusion** |
| **SMA syndrome** | **3rd part of duodenum** compressed between SMA (arises **L1**) and aorta → bilious vomiting in **1st post-op week**, electrolyte disturbance; risk: height **< 50th**, weight **< 25th** centile, kyphosis; Rx **NG tube + IV fluids** |

## X vs Y

| | Typical AIS | Needs MRI |
|---|---|---|
| Curve | **Right thoracic**, left TL/L | **Left thoracic**, short angular |
| Sagittal | **Hypokyphosis** | **Apical kyphosis** / hyperkyphosis |
| Progression | Slow | **Rapid** |
| Exam | Normal neuro, symmetric reflexes | Asymmetric abdominal reflexes, cavovarus, skin signs |

| | Milwaukee (CTLSO) | Boston TLSO | Charleston |
|---|---|---|---|
| Apex | Above T7 | T7 or below | Any (night) |
| Wear | Full time | Full time | **Night only** |

| | Risser | Sanders | TW-III RUS |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | **Iliac apophysis** | **Hand** | Radius, ulna, MC/phalanges 1, 3, 5 |
| Use | Common; PHV during Risser 0 | Predicts > 50° despite brace | **Best correlate of curve acceleration** |

## How it's asked

**"Which is NOT an MRI indication in AIS?"** → **left thoracolumbar / lumbar curve** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: left thoracic · rapid progression · neuro signs

**"Exam finding NOT suggesting non-idiopathic scoliosis?"** → **truncal shift** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: hairy patch · pigmented lesions · hyperflexibility

**"12-yr girl, left thoracic curve, absent left abdominal reflexes — next step?"** → **MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · TLSO · PSF

**"First-visit evaluation EXCEPT?"** → **MRI** (✓ leg lengths, abdominal reflexes, waist asymmetry, café-au-lait)

**"Curve magnitude at which time point best predicts progression?"** → **peak height velocity**\
✗ Wrong options: menarche · Risser 3 · TW stage 2

**"PHV correlates closest with?"** → **olecranon apophysis fusion** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: triradiate closure · Risser 1 · menarche

**"Which Risser grade = greatest velocity of linear growth?"** → **Risser 0**

**"Which X-ray shows highest progression risk?"** → **Risser 0 with open triradiate cartilage**\
✗ Wrong options: late Risser 0 with fusing triradiate · Risser 3–5

**"Which statement on curve progression is WRONG?"** → **rib phase 1 worse than rib phase 2** (TO — phase 2 progresses rapidly; RVAD > 20° → progression)

**"NCS lies between and closes in which order?"** → **centrum + posterior arches; cervical, lumbar, thoracic**

**"Brace indicated?"** → **premenarchal girl, 30° curve** · **28°, Risser 1** · **30° MT, Risser 1**\
✗ Wrong options: 38° Risser 4 · 52° · 19°

**"Minimum wear for 90–93% success?"** → **12–13 h actual wear**\
✗ Wrong options: 6–8 · 15–16 · 19–20

**"Correct statement on bracing (TO)?"** → **Milwaukee for apex above T7**\
✗ Wrong options: Boston only below T12 · 8 h minimum · success = > 5° correction

**"Expected effect of bracing a 32° immature curve?"** → **rigid TLSO 13 h/day → 50% ↓ need for surgery**

**"Bracing true statement (Sanders 2, 32°)?"** → **NNT = 4**

**"Full-time TLSO most likely to improve natural history?"** → **26° MT, Sanders 3**

**"Braced 28° → 11° curve, now Risser 4 — next?"** → **discontinue brace**\
✗ Wrong options: continue full-time/night · PSF · MRI

**"Which curve suits a TLSO (Risser 0)?"** → **30°, apex T12**\
✗ Wrong options: apex T6 → Milwaukee · 15° · 53°

**"Lenke classification includes all EXCEPT?"** → **thoracic modifier (A, B, C)** (TO)

**"Double thoracic curve = ?"** → **Lenke type 2**: structural MT (major) + PT, non-structural TL (TO)

**"Lenke 1B, 65° — management?"** → **PSF of thoracic curve only**

**"AVT 45 mm / 15 mm?"** → **ratio 3.0 → selective thoracic fusion**

**"LIV relative to touched vertebra?"** → **to or caudad to TV**\
✗ Wrong options: cephalad → truncal shift

**"Anterior single-rod + cages — pseudarthrosis risk?"** → **thoracic hyperkyphosis > 40°**

**"Commonest complication of PSO?"** → **pseudarthrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: neuro deficit · aortic injury

**"Undertapping 1 mm + straightforward trajectory?"** → **↑ maximal insertional torque**

**"MEP ↓ 60%, SSEP ↓ 30%, anaesthesia unchanged — immediate step?"** → **reposition patient**\
✗ Wrong options: deepen anaesthesia · remove screw · wake-up test · abort

**"Reduces post-op opioid use?"** → **short-course adjunctive steroids**

**"Untreated AIS, 38° at maturity — counsel?"** → **↑ lifetime chronic back pain**\
✗ Wrong options: caesarean · pulmonary decline

**"Bilious vomiting day 5 after PSF in a thin girl?"** → **SMA syndrome → NG tube + IV fluids**

**"Late pain, clean wound, months after PSF?"** → **P. acnes infection** (hold cultures 2 weeks)

# Adult Isthmic Spondylolisthesis

*Spine · 2 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Pathogenesis, prevalence & classification

**Definition** — forward slip of a vertebra on the one below due to a **pars interarticularis defect** (spondylolysis)

**Pars** — thin bicortical bridge where **lamina + inferior articular process** meet **pedicle + superior articular process**; defect = fibrocartilaginous cleft

**Prevalence** — spondylolysis **4–6%** of North American adults · 4.4% at age 6 → 6% adult (Beutler)\
✗ Wrong options: < 2% · 8–10%

**Sex** — **male** (2/3 of defects); **slip progression** commoner in **females**

**Level** — **L5-S1** **82%** (Orthobullets) · 86–90% (OB) · L4-5 11%\
✗ Wrong options: thoracolumbar junction

**Why L5-S1** — highest lumbar forces + facet orientation: "**more sagittal**" (Orthobullets) · "**more coronal**" (OB) — read the options

**Sports** — **repetitive hyperextension**: gymnasts, weightlifters, football linemen · high incidence in **Inuits** (genetic)\
✗ Wrong options: hyperflexion

**Natural history** — only **5%** of spondylolysis → spondylolisthesis · progression mostly in **adolescence**, rare after maturity\
✗ Wrong options: adults progress more

### Wiltse-Newman classification

| Type | Name | Lesion |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Dysplastic** | Congenital L5 arch / upper sacrum anomaly |
| **IIA** | **Isthmic – lytic** | **Pars fatigue (stress) fracture** — commonest adult |
| **IIB** | Isthmic | **Elongated intact pars** (healed stress fractures) |
| **IIC** | Isthmic | **Acute pars fracture** |
| **III** | **Degenerative** | Facet instability, **pars intact** (L4-5) |
| **IV** | **Traumatic** | Acute fracture of arch **other than pars** (e.g. pedicle) |
| **V** | **Pathologic** | Local / generalised bone disease |

![Isthmic spondylolisthesis: pars defect (Wiltse II)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2038-wiltse-isthmic3.jpg)

### Meyerding grade

| Grade | Slip | Group |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | < 25% | Low grade |
| **II** | 25–50% | Low grade |
| **III** | 50–75% | **High grade** |
| **IV** | 75–100% | High grade |
| **V** | > 100% | **Spondyloptosis** |

![Meyerding grading](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2038-myeding-grade.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Foraminal stenosis (main cause of leg pain)** — **exiting L5 root** in L5-S1 foramen compressed by **hypertrophic fibrocartilage of pars defect** · **uncinate spur** of posteroinferior L5 body · bulging L5-S1 disc · facet hypertrophy

**Lateral recess stenosis** — facet arthrosis + hypertrophic ligamentum flavum

**Central stenosis = rare** — posterior arch stays behind as body slips → canal **enlarges**; only in spondyloptosis\
✗ Wrong options: "central stenosis compresses traversing root"

**Disc** — bilateral pars defect → ↑ shear across disc → early degeneration → pain

**Nutcracker vs shear (TO)** — **low PI + low SS** → L5 posterior elements **clamped between L4 and S1** in extension = **nutcracker** (low progression risk) · **high PI + high SS** → ↑ **shear** on L5 pars

## 3 Presentation

**Axial back pain** — **commonest**; long history, episodic

**Leg pain** — **L5 radiculopathy** (foraminal) → **EHL + ankle dorsiflexion weakness**, dorsum-of-foot dysaesthesia, hip abductor weakness\
✗ Wrong options: FHL · quadriceps · gastrocsoleus · iliopsoas

**Tight hamstrings** — common accompanying sign

**Neurogenic claudication / cauda equina** — **rare** (slips rarely exceed grade II)

**Root logic traps** — central L3-4 / far-lateral L4-5 HNP → **L4** · central L5-S1 HNP → **S1** (plantar flexion, lateral foot) · isthmic L3-4 → not L5

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Lateral X-ray** | Slip + pars defect (diagnosis) |
| **Oblique X-ray** | Pars defect ("Scottie dog collar") |
| **Flexion-extension** | **Instability = 4 mm translation or 10° angulation** vs adjacent segment |
| **AP X-ray** | Coronal deformity · **inverted Napoleon hat sign** = **marked anterolisthesis L5 on S1** (TO) (✗ Wrong options: retrolisthesis · facet dislocation · sacralisation) |
| **CT** | Best bony pars defect |
| **MRI – T2 parasagittal** | **Best for foraminal stenosis** / neural compression |

### Pelvic incidence & spinopelvic parameters

**Pelvic incidence (PI)** — angle between **perpendicular to S1 endplate at its midpoint** and line from **S1 endplate midpoint → femoral head centre**

**PI = PT + SS** — PT = PI − SS · SS = PI − PT

| Parameter | Measured | Type |
|---|---|---|
| **Pelvic incidence** | Perpendicular to S1 endplate vs line to hip axis | **Morphologic – fixed**, same standing/sitting/lying (TO) |
| **Pelvic tilt** | **Vertical** through hip axis vs line to S1 midpoint | Positional |
| **Sacral slope** | S1 endplate vs **horizontal** | Positional |
| **Slip angle** | L5 **inferior** endplate vs perpendicular to posterior sacral cortex | Local L5-S1 kyphosis; **> 30°** predicts progression |
| **Lumbosacral angle** | L5 **upper** endplate vs perpendicular to upper sacrum | **> 10°** predicts progression |
| Lumbar index | Anterior ÷ posterior L5 height | L5 wedging |

![PI = PT + SS](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2038-shows-pi-pt-ss.jpg)

**PI values** — normal **55° ± 10°** (TO) · \~47° childhood → ↑ in adolescence → \~57° adult, then constant\
✗ Wrong options: "PI greater sitting than standing"

**PI correlates with** — **severity / Meyerding grade** of slip (direct linear) · determines SS, PT, **lumbar lordosis** · correlates with **sacral slope** (TO)

**Pelvic tilt (TO)** — varies with position · opens at the back · mean **13° ± 6°** standing · ↑ PT → sacral plateau becomes more **horizontal**\
✗ Wrong options: "more vertical"

**Balanced pelvis (TO)** — **high SS + low PT** · unbalanced = **low SS + high PT** (retroverted pelvis, vertical sacrum) · PT is the better indicator (SS changes secondary to PT)

**Slip progression predictor** — **slip angle** (Orthobullets, OB — most predictive of progression + surgical outcome) · **pelvic incidence** (TO answer) — read the options

**Global balance** — SVA (C7 plumb vs posterosuperior S1); **SVA > 50 mm** → worse outcome after decompression-fusion

### SDSG classification (L5-S1)

| Grade | Type | Defining feature |
|---|---|---|
| Low (< 50%) | **1 – Nutcracker** | **PI < 45°** |
| Low | 2 | PI 45–60° |
| Low | **3 – Shear** | **PI > 60°** → prone to progress |
| High (> 50%) | 4 | **Balanced pelvis** (high SS, low PT) |
| High | 5 | Retroverted pelvis, **balanced spine** (C7 plumb on/behind hips) |
| High | 6 | Retroverted pelvis, **unbalanced spine** |

**High-grade slips** — mean **PI > 60°**

## 5 Management

**First line** — activity restriction, **NSAIDs**, PT; bracing in acute phase; injections unclear · acute pars defect after fall in adult → **observe + mobilise**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · TLSO 6–8 wks · ORIF · fusion

### Operative criteria

| Indication for decompression + fusion |
|---|
| **Incapacitating pain failing 6 months** non-op (commonest) |
| **Progressive neurologic deficit** |
| Slip progression |
| Cauda equina syndrome |

```
Isthmic spondylolisthesis failing 6 months non-op
├─ Low grade (I–II) → L5-S1 DECOMPRESSION + INSTRUMENTED FUSION
│      (Gill laminectomy + foraminal decompression must be fused)
└─ High grade (III–V) → L4-S1 DECOMPRESSION + FUSION + ANTERIOR COLUMN SUPPORT
       └─ Reduction? → PARTIAL (correct slip angle)
```

### Surgery – decompression & fusion; ALIF vs TLIF

**Gill laminectomy** — removes loose lamina + scarred pars → decompresses root; **destabilising** → always fuse\
✗ Wrong options: laminectomy alone → further slip · decompression alone in high grade = poor

**Pars repair** — not if > grade I slip

**Contraindicated** — **stand-alone ALIF / stand-alone PLIF** (high failure) · **total disc replacement** (not for isthmic slip)

**Recurrent L5 radiculopathy after L5-S1 discectomy in isthmic slip** — **posterior foraminal decompression + instrumented fusion + graft**\
✗ Wrong options: revision hemilaminotomy · TDA · Gill alone

**Best technique** — **surgeon preference; outcomes similar**\
✗ Wrong options: PSF highest fusion · reduction lowest L5 injury · TLIF lowest dural tear (TLIF ↑ root injury + dural tears)

| | ALIF (+ posterior fixation) | TLIF |
|---|---|---|
| Approach | Retroperitoneal; ALL released | Posterior; facetectomy, lateral to dura |
| Decompression | **Indirect** (disc height → foraminal distraction) | **Direct** visualisation of roots |
| Disc height / segmental lordosis | **Greater** (✓) | Less |
| Fusion rate | Similar (some ↑) | Similar |
| 1-yr pain / overall complications | Similar | Similar |
| Specific risks | **Retrograde ejaculation** (superior hypogastric plexus), retroperitoneal haematoma, persistent radiculopathy, pars-defect pain fibres left behind | Dural tear, root injury |
| Use | **Low-grade** slips, even with radicular pain (87–94% good at 2 yrs) · not high grade | Any |

**PLIF vs TLIF** — PLIF cage **medial to facets** · TLIF = facetectomy, more lateral · interbody ↑ time, blood loss, stay

### Reduction – L5 nerve root injury

**Root at risk with reduction** — **L5** (exits under L5 pedicle; S1 traverses) → post-op **EHL / great-toe extension weakness**\
✗ Wrong options: S1 plantar flexion · L2–3 hip flexion · L4 patellar reflex · genitofemoral (anterior approach) · cauda equina

**Strain biomechanics** — **71% of L5 strain in the second half** of complete reduction (non-linear after > 50%) → **partial reduction** · ↑ disc height and ↑ lordosis protect L5

**Reduction goal** — correct **slip angle / lumbosacral kyphosis** first; slip % second

```
High-grade slip (SDSG)
├─ Balanced pelvis (type 4) → IN-SITU FUSION
├─ Retroverted pelvis, spine clinically balanced (type 5) → PARTIAL REDUCTION + FUSION
└─ Retroverted pelvis + sagittal imbalance / stooping (type 6) → REDUCTION MANDATORY (360°)
```

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Note |
|---|---|
| **L5 root stretch** | Reduction |
| **Pseudarthrosis** | — |
| **Dural tear** | ↑ with TLIF |
| **Sacral insufficiency fracture** | Constructs **ending at S1** without pelvic fixation |
| Retrograde ejaculation | ALIF |

## X vs Y

| | Isthmic | Degenerative |
|---|---|---|
| Level | **L5-S1** | **L4-5** |
| Pars | **Defect** | Intact (facet arthrosis) |
| Age / sex | Young, **male** | Older, **female** |
| Root | **Exiting L5** (foraminal) | Traversing L5 (lateral recess / central) |
| Grade | Can be high | Rarely > grade I |
| Canal | **Enlarged** | Stenotic |

| | Nutcracker | Shear |
|---|---|---|
| PI / SS | **Low / low** | **High / high** |
| SDSG | Type 1 | Type 3 |
| Progression | Low | High |

## How it's asked

**"Inverted Napoleon hat sign on AP indicates?"** → **anterolisthesis of L5 on S1** (TO)

**"Cause of this spondylolisthesis (pars fracture on CT)?"** → **fatigue fracture of pars**\
✗ Wrong options: congenital arch · degenerative · traumatic non-pars

**"Commonest level + sex for pars defect?"** → **L5-S1, male**

**"Prevalence of spondylolysis in adults?"** → **4–6%**

**"Lacrosse player, bilateral pars defect — true?"** → **foraminal stenosis from fibrocartilage**\
✗ Wrong options: central stenosis · TL junction · adults progress more · fuse first

**"Grade I isthmic L5-S1 — weakness of?"** → **EHL**

**"Great-toe extension weakness + tight hamstrings in a 33-yr basketballer?"** → **grade 1 isthmic L5-S1**

**"True about isthmic spondylolisthesis?"** → **caused by pars defect**\
✗ Wrong options: hyperflexion · L4-5 · no PI correlation · adults progress more

**"Balanced pelvis correlates with?"** → **low pelvic tilt** (TO)

**"Pelvic incidence correlates with?"** → **sacral slope** (TO)

**"Progression in spondylolisthesis correlates with?"** → **pelvic incidence** (TO) · **slip angle** (OB image question) — read the options

**"Pelvic tilt — all true EXCEPT?"** → **"↑ PT makes sacral plateau more vertical"** (TO)

**"Pelvic incidence — NOT true?"** → **"greater sitting than standing"** (TO)

**"Nutcracker type = ?"** → **low PI + low SS** (TO)

**"Parameter correlating with severity?"** → **PT + SS (= PI)**

**"Which angle predicts slip progression and surgical outcome?"** → **slip angle** (L5 inferior endplate vs perpendicular to posterior sacrum)

**"Grade 3, retroverted pelvis, clinically balanced spine — treatment?"** → **partial reduction + fusion** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: complete reduction · in-situ fusion · conservative

**"Failed non-op high-grade slip — surgery?"** → **decompression + L4-S1 fusion + anterior column support**

**"Grade 2 isthmic with 3/5 dorsiflexion — surgery?"** → **L5 laminectomy + fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: pars repair · laminectomy alone · stand-alone ALIF · TDR

**"Best technique for high-grade isthmic slip in gymnast?"** → **surgeon preference; similar outcomes**

**"Advantage of ALIF + PF over TLIF?"** → **greater sagittal (segmental lordosis) correction** · **greater disc-height restoration**\
✗ Wrong options: lower sexual dysfunction · better fusion · direct root view

**"Deficit after reduction + fusion of L5-S1 slip?"** → **L5 root → great-toe extension weakness**

**"Most L5 strain during reduction occurs?"** → **second half of complete reduction (71%)**

# Adult Pyogenic Vertebral Osteomyelitis

*Spine · 3 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology – lumbar spine most common

**Spectrum (spondylodiscitis)** — discitis · vertebral osteomyelitis · epidural abscess

**Age** — median **50–60 yrs**

| Region | Share |
|---|---|
| **Lumbar** | **50–60%** |
| Thoracic | 30–40% |
| Cervical | **\~10%** (Orthobullets) · 20% (OB) |
| Sacrum | Rare |

**Order** — **lumbar > thoracic > cervical > sacrum**\
✗ Wrong options: lumbar = thoracic · cervical first

**Risk factors** — **IV drug use** · **diabetes** · **recent systemic infection (UTI, pneumonia)** · malignancy · immunosuppression · obesity · **malnutrition (albumin < 3 g/dL)** · trauma · smoking

### Organisms

| Organism | Setting |
|---|---|
| **Staph aureus** | **Commonest (50–65%)** |
| Staph epidermidis | 2nd commonest |
| Gram-negatives | ↑ last decade; GU + respiratory sources |
| **Pseudomonas** | **IV drug users** |
| **Salmonella** | **Sickle cell disease** |
| Fungal · TB | Immunocompromised |

**Commonest fungal infection of spine (TO)** — **Aspergillus**; Candida + Aspergillus = commensals entering via IV lines/implants/surgery · ↑ during COVID (steroid overuse) · Coccidioides + Blastomyces = geographic\
✗ Wrong options: Candida · Cryptococcus · Blastomyces

## 2 Mechanism

**Haematogenous seeding (commonest)** — arterial or venous → **endplate** (low-flow vascular anastomosis) → **disc** → adjacent endplate

**Direct inoculation** — penetrating trauma, open fracture, surgery

**Contiguous spread** — retropharyngeal + retroperitoneal abscess

**Neurologic deficit (10–20%)** — direct neural infection · **epidural abscess** compression · **instability**

**Epidural abscess** — pus/granulation between dura and epidural fat · in **\~18%** of spondylodiscitis · **50%** of abscess patients have neuro symptoms

**Psoas abscess** — associated

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — severe, **insidious**, unrelenting, worse with activity; **night pain** → infection / malignancy

**Fever** — **only 33%**

**History** — UTI, pneumonia, skin infection, transplant

**Neuro** — radiculopathy / myelopathy in 10–20% → careful neuro exam

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| **WBC** | ↑ in only **\~50%** → insensitive |
| **ESR** | ↑ in **90%**; serial monitoring, less reliable than CRP |
| **CRP** | ↑ in **90%**; **best for tracking treatment response** |
| **Blood cultures** | **All patients before antibiotics**; least invasive; positive **\~33% (25–66%)**; **85% accurate** when positive; ↑ yield off antibiotics + during fever |
| **CT-guided biopsy** | If blood cultures negative + no urgent surgery; diagnostic **68–86%** (Orthobullets) · needle \~70%, open > 80% (OB); send **aerobic, anaerobic, fungal, AFB** |
| **Open biopsy** | Non-invasive fails; anterior, costotransversectomy, transpedicular |

**Post-discectomy: severe pain + spasm, no fever, normal TLC + ESR, CRP very high (TO)** — **infective discitis**\
✗ Wrong options: instability · pseudomeningocele · residual disc

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Findings |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | Delayed by weeks · **loss of psoas shadow** (first days) · **disc space narrowing at 7–10 days** · **endplate erosion/sclerosis at 10–21 days** · local osteopenia |
| **CT** | Bony destruction, abscess extent |
| **MRI with gadolinium** | **Gold standard**; **most sensitive (96%)** + specific (93%); **best to separate infection from tumour**; early scan may mimic degeneration → repeat |
| **Tc-99m bone scan** | If MRI impossible; **90% sensitive, not specific**; + gallium-67 → ↑ sensitivity + specificity |
| **Indium-111** | Poor sensitivity (**17%**) |

![Disc space narrowing with endplate erosion on lateral X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2025-case-a-xray-lumbar-lat-shows-disc-space-narrowing-mgh.jpg)

**Classic discitis MRI triad** — **T2 bright disc** (fluid/pus) · **T1 dark**, disc–body margin lost · **T1 fat-sat + gad: rim enhancement** of abscess + adjacent bodies → pathognomonic\
✗ Wrong options: lymphoma · primary epidural abscess · DDD · early AS

![Spondylodiscitis on T2 and T1 MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2025-case-a-mri-t2-and-t1.jpg)

**MRI limit** — less useful than **labs** for treatment response

**Infection vs tumour** — infection = **disc involved**, endplate erosion, marked inflammation · tumour spares disc

**Discitis vs Modic I** — Modic I: T1 dark, T2 bright marrow but **disc desiccated (normal / dark T2)**, no endplate erosion, no soft-tissue mass, normal ESR/CRP

**Pyogenic vs TB on MRI (TO)** — collection with **relatively preserved disc** → **pyogenic** (acute, presents early) · **collapsed disc**, subligamentous spread → TB (late presentation) · end result of spinal TB = **bony ankylosis** (weight-bearing + cancellous surfaces)\
✗ Wrong options: fibrous ankylosis as in limb joints

## 5 Management

```
Suspected spondylodiscitis
├─ Septic / critically ill → CULTURES (± CT aspirate) THEN BROAD-SPECTRUM IV
│      (vancomycin + 3rd-gen cephalosporin)
├─ Stable, neuro intact → MRI + BLOOD CULTURES first
│      └─ Organism found → ORGANISM-SPECIFIC IV ABX
│      └─ Cultures negative → CT-GUIDED BIOPSY
└─ Deficit / instability / abscess / cervical → DEBRIDE + STABILISE
```

### Diagnosis – MRI, blood cultures & CT-guided biopsy

**Organism ID is mandatory** before targeted therapy · stable patient → **hold antibiotics** until cultures/biopsy\
✗ Wrong options: empirical IV antibiotics · INH-RIF-PZA before biopsy in suspected TB

**IVDU with UTI, back pain, ↑ ESR** — **admit + blood cultures + MRI with/without gad**\
✗ Wrong options: oral abx · bone scan · renal US

**Blood cultures negative, neuro intact** — **CT-guided closed biopsy**\
✗ Wrong options: IV abx · open surgery · repeat MRI

**MSSA in blood cultures** — **organism-specific IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: CT biopsy · debridement

### Treatment – organism-specific antibiotics

**Course** — **6–12 weeks** ± bracing · IV until improvement (\~**4–6 wks**) → oral · success **80%**

**Bracing** — pain + deformity prevention · **cervical → rigid cervicothoracic orthosis or halo**

**Resistant organisms** — MRSA, VRSA, VRE → **linezolid, daptomycin**

| Infection | Agent |
|---|---|
| Gram-positive | Cefazolin, vancomycin |
| Gram-negative | Fluoroquinolone (ciprofloxacin), 3rd-gen cephalosporin |
| **Fungal** | **Amphotericin B** first line (± azoles) |
| TB | **INH + rifampicin + PZA + ethambutol** |

### Operative criteria

| Debridement + stabilisation indicated |
|---|
| **Cervical** osteomyelitis / cervical epidural abscess |
| **Progressive neurologic deficit** |
| **Progressive deformity / gross instability** |
| **Refractory** to medical therapy |
| **Large abscess** |

**Open biopsy alone** — cultures + CT biopsy fail; lumbar disease without canal abscess

### Surgical technique

**Gold standard** — **anterior debridement + strut graft ± posterior instrumentation** · posterior debridement alone usually inadequate

**Graft** — autograft (tricortical iliac crest, rib, fibula) safe in acute infection · allograft OK · **titanium mesh cage + autograft + posterior instrumentation** → best deformity correction

**Instrumentation in active infection** — controversial (staged vs single stage) · **titanium > stainless steel**

**Posterior instrumentation** — severe kyphosis or multilevel anterior construct

**Two-level cervical osteomyelitis + rapidly progressive gait loss + ↑ WBC/CRP/ESR** — **anterior corpectomy + posterior instrumentation (A + P)** · cervical abscess → early surgery (little space for cord) · lumbar abscess → often antibiotics alone

## X vs Y

| | Pyogenic | Tuberculous |
|---|---|---|
| Onset | Acute, painful, early | Insidious, late |
| Disc (TO) | **Relatively preserved** at presentation | **Collapsed / destroyed** |
| Spread | Endplate → disc → endplate | Subligamentous, skip lesions |
| End result | — | **Bony ankylosis** |

| | Infection | Tumour |
|---|---|---|
| Disc | **Involved** | Spared |
| Endplate | Eroded | Preserved |
| Best test | **MRI + gad** | MRI |

## How it's asked

**"Spondylodiscitis with collection but preserved disc?"** → **pyogenic** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: TB

**"End result of spinal TB?"** → **bony ankylosis** (TO)

**"IVDU, back pain, negative blood cultures, neuro intact — initial management?"** → **CT-guided closed biopsy**

**"IVDU, UTI, focal back pain, ↑ ESR — next step?"** → **admission + blood cultures + MRI with/without gadolinium**

**"Spondylodiscitis on MRI in a diabetic, no deficit — plan?"** → **needle biopsy + IV antibiotics + TLSO**\
✗ Wrong options: Modic I · antibiotics before biopsy · anterior surgery

**"HIV-positive man, suspected TB spondylitis, cultures negative?"** → **CT-guided biopsy + cultures**\
✗ Wrong options: start anti-TB drugs · broad antibiotics

**"IVDU, thoracic discitis, no sepsis or deficit — first step?"** → **blood cultures**\
✗ Wrong options: start IV antibiotics · corpectomy

**"T2 bright disc, T1 loss of margins, rim enhancement on gad — pathognomonic for?"** → **discitis**

**"Stable 17-yr with discitis after pyelonephritis — next step?"** → **MRI with contrast + blood cultures**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration first · start broad abx

**"MSSA in 2 blood cultures, no deficit — next?"** → **organism-specific IV antibiotics**

**"Commonest fungal infection of spine?"** → **Aspergillosis** (TO)

**"Amphotericin B used for?"** → **fungal osteomyelitis**

**"Order of spinal involvement?"** → **lumbar > thoracic > cervical > sacrum**

**"Which scenario needs anterior + posterior fusion?"** → **diabetic with 2-level cervical osteomyelitis + rapid gait loss**

**"Post-discectomy pain, normal ESR + TLC, high CRP, afebrile?"** → **infective discitis** (TO)

**"Best lab to follow treatment response?"** → **CRP**

**"Most sensitive and specific imaging?"** → **MRI with gadolinium**\
✗ Wrong options: indium scan (17% sensitive)

**"Organism in sickle cell / IVDU?"** → **Salmonella / Pseudomonas**

# Adult Spinal Deformity

*Spine · 4 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & types

**Demographics** — mean age **60**; M = F

**Coronal imbalance** — lateral deviation of vertical line **> 10°**

**Sagittal imbalance** — radiographic **> 5 cm** (C7 plumb vs posterosuperior S1)

**Degenerative pathoanatomy** — **asymmetric degeneration** of discs ± facets

**Loss of sagittal balance** — osteoporosis · pre-existing scoliosis · iatrogenic instability · DDD

| | Idiopathic (residual) | Degenerative (de novo) |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | Untreated AIS | Degeneration, iatrogenic, paralysis |
| Curve pattern | **Classic** patterns | Lacks classic pattern |
| Segments | **More** | Fewer |
| Location | **Thoracic** | **Lumbar** |
| Magnitude | Larger | Smaller |

### Natural history & progression

**Progression by curve** — thoracic > lumbar > thoracolumbar > double major · right thoracic **1°/yr** · right lumbar **0.5°/yr** · TL **0.25°/yr**

**By magnitude** — **< 30° rarely progress** · **> 50° commonly progress**

**Other risks** — **intercrestal line below L4-5** · pre-existing rotation

**Symptoms progress to** — side of curve **convexity**

## 3 Presentation

**Low back pain** — **commonest (40–90%)**; spondylosis, instability, discogenic; more severe/recurrent than general population

**Neurogenic claudication** — stenosis on **concave side**; no relief with sitting / flexion (unlike classic claudication)

**Radicular pain** — foraminal + lateral recess stenosis, worse in **concavity** (rotation + translation)

**Exam** — thoracic prominence on forward bend, compensatory posture, weakness

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **36-inch standing AP + lateral + R/L bending** | Cobb, coronal (C7PL vs CSVL) + sagittal balance, flexibility |
| **CT** | Bony deformity, facet arthrosis |
| **CT myelogram** | **Best for stenosis + bony anatomy** when rotation makes MRI hard |
| **MRI** | When leg pain present: stenosis, facet hypertrophy, foraminal encroachment |
| **DEXA** | Bone density for planning |

### Sagittal balance – SVA, PI-LL mismatch, pelvic parameters

**Sagittal vertical axis (SVA)** — horizontal distance **C7 plumb line → posterosuperior corner of S1**; neutral = through it · positive = anterior · negative = posterior

**Strongest predictor of symptoms / HRQOL / PROMs / poor surgical result** — **sagittal balance (C7 SVA)**\
✗ Wrong options: coronal imbalance · Cobb · levels · apex level · residual scoliosis > 25° · L5-S1 disc left out · truncal shift · PI

**Radiographic parameter correlating with pain + disability (VAS, ODI)** — **thoracolumbar kyphosis** (✗ Wrong options: SS · TK · PT · PI) · TL/lumbar curves also correlate (✗ Wrong options: thoracic scoliosis · thoracic hypokyphosis · lumbar hyperlordosis · disc degeneration)

**Pelvic incidence** — **only permanent anatomic parameter**; **PI = PT + SS**; not posture dependent (TO); stabilises after adolescence; determines lordosis needed

**Compensation for positive SVA** — **pelvic retroversion (↑ PT, ↓ SS)** · thoracic hypokyphosis · cervical hyperlordosis · knee flexion → SVA may look normal (< 5 cm) → look for regional imbalance

**T1 pelvic angle (T1PA)** — angle T1 centre → femoral head → S1 endplate centre = **T1 spinal inclination + PT**; accounts for **spinal inclination + pelvic retroversion**; no calibration needed; usable intra-op · goal **< 14°** · **> 20° ≈ ODI 40** (severe) · ↑ T1PA = ↑ ODI

![T1 pelvic angle](../../MCQ/attachments/q217400-illa-t1-pelvic-angle.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria – surgical alignment targets

| Target | Value |
|---|---|
| **SVA** | **< 5 cm** (best reliable improvement correcting to **+3 cm**) |
| **PI − LL** | **LL = PI ± 9°** (PI − LL < 10°) (✗ Wrong options: within 15° · LL = PI + 25° · LL = TK + 15°) |
| **Pelvic tilt** | **< 20°** (OB) · < 25° (alternate goal) |
| **T1PA** | **< 14°** |
| **TK + LL + PI** | **≤ 45°** — most predictive of maintaining correction |
| **GAP score** | **< 3** → fewer mechanical failures / revisions |

**Normal lumbar lordosis** — 40–60° (\~70% from L4-S1; apex L3-4 / L4)

## 5 Management

**Non-op** — coronal curves **< 30°** rarely progress · NSAIDs · **TCAs for sleep** · PT core strengthening · injections / root blocks (diagnostic + therapeutic) · bracing (comfort)

### Operative criteria

| Indication for instrumented fusion |
|---|
| Curve **> 50°** |
| **Sagittal imbalance** |
| Curve progression |
| Intractable back / radicular pain failing non-op |
| Cardiopulmonary decline: thoracic **> 60° → PFTs ↓**; **> 90° → mortality ↑** |
| Cosmesis (controversial) |

```
ASD surgical approach
├─ Thoracic / double structural > 50° → POSTERIOR-ONLY FUSION
└─ Isolated TL / lumbar, > 70°, rigid, L5-S1 interbody → ANTERIOR RELEASE + POSTERIOR
```

**Goals** — restore **spinal balance** · relieve pain · solid fusion

**Worse outcomes** — **baseline depression** · **obesity** · ≥ 4 comorbidities · insufficient sagittal correction · pseudarthrosis\
✗ Wrong options: ethnicity · pre-op ODI · pre-op SVA if corrected

### Fusion level selection

**Proximal** — extend to a **neutral + horizontal vertebra above main curve**

| Distal level | Indication / outcome |
|---|---|
| **L5** | Only if **no L5-S1 pathology**; lowest future L5-S1 degeneration with **normal C7 plumb + normal sacral inclination** |
| **S1** | **Any L5-S1 pathology**: spondylolisthesis, spondylolysis, facet arthrosis, prior laminectomy · ✓ **better correction + maintenance of sagittal balance** · (✗ Wrong options: ↑ pseudarthrosis, OR time, reoperation, **sacral insufficiency fracture**, altered gait)|
| **Ilium (sacropelvic)** | Sacrum in fusion of **> 3 levels**; iliac screws/bolts → **↑ fusion success** at L5-S1 + **↓ sacral fractures** in osteopenic; prominent hardware |

**Maximise fusion rate** — **ilium + sacrum** both in distal fixation\
✗ Wrong options: ilium alone · sacrum alone · coronal balance

**Pubic ramus fractures** — occur with sacral **and** iliac fixation

### Osteotomies – correction per level

| Osteotomy | Correction | Indication / prerequisite |
|---|---|---|
| **Smith-Petersen (SPO) / Ponte** | **\~10° per level** (Orthobullets) · posterior column osteotomy **3–5°** (OB) | Mild–moderate imbalance; needs **mobile disc + disc height**, **no anterior fusion**; more in lumbar |
| **Pedicle subtraction (PSO)** | **30–35° lumbar**, 25° thoracic (Orthobullets) · **25–35°** (OB) | **SVA > 12 cm** (Orthobullets) · **> 10 cm** (TO); **anterior fusion present** (correction through body) |
| **Vertebral column resection (VCR)** | **Up to / > 45°** (avg 62° coronal, 45° sagittal) | **Rigid sharp angular** kyphosis (tumour, fracture, infection), rigid scoliosis, congenital kyphosis, hemivertebra |
| TLIF | \~8° | — |
| TLIF + ALL release + hyperlordotic cage | 10–15° (22° with PCOs) | — |

![Osteotomy types for sagittal correction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2041-osteotomies.jpg)

**3 SPO ≈ 1 PSO** — PSO more blood loss · neuromonitoring preferred

**PSO level** — **L3 / L4** (apex of lordosis L3-4; 70% of LL from L4-S1)\
✗ Wrong options: L1 → non-anatomic · SPOs above apex · thoracic SPOs ↓ TK

**Flat back after prior lateral interbody / L3-S1 fusion** — **L3 PSO + extension to pelvis**\
✗ Wrong options: SPOs through fused discs · T12 PSO · VCR

### Bone health optimisation

**Osteoporosis (T < −2.5) or ≥ 2 vertebral compression fractures (= osteoporosis without DEXA)** — **teriparatide** before surgery (≥ **6 months** → ↓ pseudarthrosis + reoperation)\
✗ Wrong options: continue/start alendronate (impairs fusion) · vit D/Ca alone · denosumab switch · repeat DEXA

**Osteopenia (T −1.7)** — no anabolic referral needed

**Cement (PMMA) augmentation of end vertebrae** — ↑ **fusion rate** + ↓ **junctional failure** + ↑ pullout strength, no extra complications · osteoporotic spine: **≥ 3 fixation points above + below**\
✗ Wrong options: fewer levels fused

**Active smoker** — **smoking cessation clinic + nicotine testing**, postpone elective fusion\
✗ Wrong options: schedule now

**Smoking** — ↑ pseudarthrosis, infection, implant loosening, fracture risk; **cessation programmes improve outcomes** (EXCEPT answer: "cessation doesn't help" is FALSE)

### Perioperative considerations

**Neuromonitoring (SSEP ↓ 60% at correction)** — **stop instrumenting + raise MAP ≥ 85 mmHg**, recheck, then reverse correction\
✗ Wrong options: wake-up test first · MAP 60 · steroids first · inhalational only (reduce inhalational agents)

**Neuro deficit on monitoring after correction** — remove instrumentation, consider wake-up test

**Jehovah's Witness** — **closed-circuit cell saver**\
✗ Wrong options: whole blood, PRBC, plasma, platelets

**Open thoracotomy for anterior release (adult)** — **pulmonary function ↓ and often stays ↓ long term**

**Pedicle anatomy** — cortical:cancellous ratio **thoracic pedicles > lumbar pedicles > vertebral bodies > sacrum** (implant failure likeliest in sacrum) · lumbosacral pedicle **diameter + medial angulation ↑ caudally** (L3 more medial than L1; S1 > L1) · **L5 root on sacral ala** (at risk with S-I screws) · S1 screw start **inferior + lateral** to L5 inferior articular process tip

**Posterior instrumentation system (TO)** — **Cotrel-Dubousset** (rods + hooks/screws, posterior)\
✗ Wrong options: Dwyer · Zielke (anterior) · Boston brace

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Overall** | **\~13.5%**; ↑ with osteotomies, revisions, A/P |
| **Major** | **\~10% (10–20%)**; irreversible; **VTE** → worst outcomes |
| **Strongest predictor of major complication** | **Age > 60** (✗ Wrong options: smoking · BMI > 30 · PSO · comorbidities) |
| **Pseudarthrosis** | **5–25%**; posterior-only 15%; **L5-S1 + TL junction**; **commonest reason for revision within 3 yrs** |
| **Implant failure / rod fracture** | **Commonest cause of reoperation**; rod breakage L4-S1 from **large PI-LL mismatch** (✗ Wrong options: titanium rods · coronal balance) |
| **Proximal junctional failure** | 2nd commonest revision; within 6–9 months |
| Dural tear | 2.9% |
| Infection | Deep 1.5%, superficial 0.9%; **smoking** strongest SSI factor (✗ Wrong options: drains · drain output · prolonged abx) |
| Neuro deficit | Acute \~1%, delayed 0.5% · **after PSO 18%** (Orthobullets) · 11%, 2.8% permanent (OB) · \~20% |
| PSO pseudarthrosis | **29% at 5 yrs**, at TL junction above osteotomy |
| Epidural haematoma / PE / DVT | \~0.2% each · death 0.3% |

**Pseudarthrosis risks** — **age > 55** · kyphosis > 20° · **positive SVA > 5 cm** · hip OA · smoking · **thoracoabdominal approach** · incomplete lumbopelvic fixation · osteoporosis · TL Cobb > 20°\
✗ Wrong options: posterior midline · Cobb 60°

**Post-PSO quadriceps weakness** — screw malposition (30% of deficits → CT), large PI-LL correction, prone positioning (femoral nerve), stroke · **least likely = apical derotation** (coronal manoeuvre)

**Post-op neuro decline + fluid collection at 3 months** — **I&D, retain hardware**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · antibiotics only · remove hardware

## X vs Y

| | SPO / Ponte | PSO | VCR |
|---|---|---|---|
| Columns | Posterior | 3-column wedge (hinge anterior) | All 3 removed |
| Correction | \~10°/level | 25–35° | > 45° |
| Needs mobile disc | **Yes** | No | No |
| Prior anterior fusion | No | Yes | Yes |
| Commonest complication | — | **Pseudarthrosis** | High blood loss, instability |

| | Fusion to L5 | Fusion to S1 + ilium |
|---|---|---|
| L5-S1 healthy | ✓ | — |
| Sagittal correction | Less | **Better + maintained** |
| Pseudarthrosis | — | ↑ (sacrum alone) · ↓ with iliac fixation |

## How it's asked

**"PI — all true EXCEPT?"** → **posture dependent** (TO)

**"Angle x on lateral (perpendicular to S1 endplate vs line to femoral head)?"** → **pelvic incidence** (TO)

**"75° TL curve, SVA 12 cm — what most reliably ↓ disability?"** → **correct SVA to +3 cm**\
✗ Wrong options: LL within 15° of PI · Cobb < 25° · PT > 20° · stop at L5

**"Symptoms in adult scoliosis correlate with?"** → **sagittal imbalance**

**"Which figure correctly measures sagittal imbalance?"** → **C7 plumb → posterosuperior S1**

**"Parameter correlating with VAS + disability?"** → **thoracolumbar kyphosis**

**"Correct which parameter to improve HRQOL?"** → **C7 SVA**

**"Formula for lumbar lordosis correction?"** → **LL = PI ± 9°**

**"Most predictive of poor result after multilevel fusion?"** → **sagittal imbalance**

**"Back pain in adult idiopathic scoliosis correlates with?"** → **thoracolumbar kyphosis**

**"Permanent pelvic parameter guiding lordosis?"** → **pelvic incidence**

**"PI = which two added?"** → **PT + SS**

**"Greatest association with worse PROMs?"** → **C7 plumb line deviation**

**"Best post-op equation (PI 70, LL 45)?"** → **PI − LL = 7**

**"T1 pelvic angle — true?"** → **accounts for spinal inclination + pelvic retroversion**

**"Advantage of fusing to sacrum vs L5?"** → **better correction + maintenance of sagittal balance**

**"Maximise fusion rate?"** → **include ilium + sacrum**

**"Primary reason for iliac fixation?"** → **improved fusion success**

**"Benefit of pelvic fixation in osteopenic long fusion?"** → **↓ sacral fractures**

**"Osteotomy for SVA > 10 cm?"** → **pedicle subtraction** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Ponte · SPO

**"Commonest complication of lumbar PSO?"** → **pseudarthrosis** (29% at 5 yrs)

**"Correction paired correctly?"** → **PSO 25–35°**\
✗ Wrong options: PCO 15–20° · VCR 35–40° · TLIF 15–20°

**"Post lateral interbody fusion flat back?"** → **L3 PSO + fusion to pelvis**

**"Prior L3-S1 fusion, LL 22, PI 55?"** → **PSO at L3**

**"Factor ↑ nonunion in adult idiopathic scoliosis surgery?"** → **thoracoabdominal approach**\
✗ Wrong options: age > 35 · SVA < 5 cm

**"FALSE about smoking?"** → **"cessation doesn't change outcomes"**

**"Incidence of major complications?"** → **10–20%**

**"Most predictive of major complication?"** → **age > 60**

**"Neuro decline + collection 3 months after TLIF?"** → **I&D (keep hardware)**

**"Least likely cause of quad weakness after PSO?"** → **apical derotation**

**"Factor linked to worse PROMs after ASD surgery?"** → **obesity**

**"Strongest SSI risk factor (smoker, DM, BMI 37, drains)?"** → **smoking**

**"Commonest complication needing revision within 3 yrs?"** → **pseudarthrosis**

**"Broken rod at L5 after lumbopelvic fixation?"** → **large PI-LL mismatch**

**"Most common cause of reoperation — expected finding?"** → **implant fracture**

**"Purpose of PMMA at end vertebrae?"** → **↑ fusion rate + ↓ junctional failure**

**"T-score −3.2 on alendronate, planning T11-pelvis fusion?"** → **refer for teriparatide**

**"Smoker with apixaban + oxycodone, T −1.7?"** → **smoking cessation + nicotine tests in 6 weeks**

**"Two compression fractures, low BMD, pre-op?"** → **teriparatide before surgery**

**"Lowest pseudarthrosis risk?"** → **teriparatide started 9 months ago**

**"Fusion level selection — true?"** → **proximal to neutral + horizontal vertebra**

**"Pedicle anatomy — true?"** → **thoracic pedicle cortical:cancellous ratio > sacrum**

**"SSEP ↓ 60% at correction?"** → **stop instrumenting, MAP ≥ 85**

**"Jehovah's Witness may accept?"** → **closed-circuit cell saver**

**"Thoracotomy effect on adult PFTs?"** → **↓ and often stays ↓**

**"Posterior scoliosis system?"** → **Cotrel-Dubousset** (TO)

# Ankylosing Spondylitis

*Spine · 5 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — chronic **seronegative** autoimmune spondyloarthropathy: bridging syndesmophytes, **enthesitis**, sacroiliitis, uveitis

**Incidence** — \~**0.2%** of Caucasians

**Sex / age** — **4:1 male** · presents **3rd decade** · juvenile form < 16 yrs\
✗ Wrong options: more common in females

**HLA-B27 carriers** — **< 10%** of B27-positive people develop AS · B27 in **\~6%** of white population

### Pathoanatomy

**Enthesitis** — inflammation at tendon/ligament insertion → erosion → soft-tissue ossification → **ankylosis**; targets **SI joints**, spinal apophyseal joints, pubic symphysis\
✗ Wrong options: synovial process = RA

**Disc** — annulus inflammation → **syndesmophytes**

**Theories** — autoimmune reaction to environmental pathogen in a genetically susceptible host; B27 peptide aggregation; cytotoxic T-cells against B27

**Spread** — SI fusion first → cephalad spinal progression → often **entire spine**\
✗ Wrong options: "only pelvis + spine": large joints too

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Fact |
|---|---|
| **HLA-B27** | **Class I** MHC antigen, **B locus, chromosome 6**; positive **\~90%** (Orthobullets, TO) · \~95% (OB) |
| B27 prevalence (TO) | **AS 90% · juvenile SpA 70% · RA 6%** |
| **RF** | **Negative** (seronegative) |
| ESR / CRP | Non-specific mild ↑ in active disease (✗ Wrong options: normal) |
| ✗ Traps | COL1A1 = OI · **CD20** = lymphoma · **EXT1** = MHE · **HLA-B8** = DISH + diabetes · HLA-DR3 = SLE · urine phosphoethanolamine = hypophosphatasia |

**Inheritance** — genetic predisposition, mode **unknown**

### Systemic manifestations

| System | Manifestation |
|---|---|
| **Eye** | **Acute anterior uveitis / iritis** — commonest extra-articular; confirms diagnosis with B27 + sacroiliitis |
| Heart | **Conduction abnormalities** |
| Aorta | Aortitis, stenosis, **regurgitation** |
| Lung | **Pulmonary fibrosis** (↓ FEV), costovertebral fusion → ↓ chest expansion |
| Kidney | Amyloidosis |
| **Synovitis** | **Klebsiella pneumoniae** (gram-negative bacillus) — B27-positive more susceptible (✗ Wrong options: Strep pneumoniae · gonococcus · C. acnes · Staph aureus) |
| Other | IBD, psoriasis |

**Orthopaedic** — **bilateral sacroiliitis** · progressive **kyphosis** · **cervical fractures** · large-joint arthritis (**hip + shoulder**, asymmetric)

## 3 Presentation

**Lumbosacral pain + stiffness** — insidious, 3rd decade, **worse in morning**, improves with exercise

**Neck / upper thoracic pain** — later; **acute neck pain = fracture until proven otherwise**

**Other** — sciatica (piriformis), **loss of horizontal gaze**, dyspnoea (costovertebral)

| Exam test | Detects |
|---|---|
| **Chest expansion < 2 cm** | **More specific than HLA-B27** |
| **Schober test** | Lumbar stiffness |
| **Wall (Fleche) test** (TO) | **Cervical spine involvement in AS** — occiput can't touch wall |
| **FABER (Patrick)** | SI joint pain (✗ Wrong options: FADDIR = FAI) |
| **Chin-brow to vertical angle (CBVA)** | Chin-on-chest deformity; **standing lateral**; osteotomy planning — correction ∝ outcome |
| Supine + sitting exam | Separates **hip flexion contracture** from spinal kyphosis |

![Chin-brow to vertical angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2044-cvba.jpg)

**Juvenile AS** — sacroiliitis, spondylitis, **enthesitis** (more than adult), kyphosis, stiffness, peripheral LL arthritis, uveitis · not **urethritis** (= reactive arthritis triad)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

**Orthobullets** — **bilateral sacroiliitis** ± uveitis + **HLA-B27**

**Modified New York criteria (TO)** — HLA-B27 is **NOT** a criterion

| Clinical criteria | Radiological criterion |
|---|---|
| LBP **> 3 months**, better with exercise, not relieved by rest | **Bilateral sacroiliitis grade 2–4** or **unilateral grade 3–4** |
| ↓ lumbar motion in **sagittal + frontal** planes | — |
| ↓ chest expansion for age/sex | — |

**Definite AS** — radiological + **≥ 1 clinical** · **Probable** — 3 clinical alone, or radiological alone

**SI joint injection** — often the **most sensitive diagnostic test**

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray spine** | **Squaring** of vertebrae · **marginal (vertical) syndesmophytes** · **bamboo spine** (late) · ALL/PLL calcification · osteoporosis · **dagger sign** (AP: central line = ossified supra/interspinous ligaments) |
| **X-ray pelvis – Ferguson view** | Beam **10–15° cephalad** → anterior SI joint |
| SI joint | **Bilateral symmetric erosions**; **earliest = iliac side** erosion → narrowing → ankylosis |
| **CT** | Bony change, not inflammation; **most sensitive for cervical fracture** |
| **MRI (STIR / gad)** | Detects inflammation → **earliest diagnosis**; **Romanus lesion** = "shiny corner" erosions at vertebral corners (**TO: MRI of LS spine**) · **Andersson lesion** = spondylodiscitis / pseudarthrosis · active sacroiliitis → dynamic fat-sat MRI |
| Bone scan | SI uptake, not specific |

![Bilateral sacroiliitis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2044-as-si-bilateral-sacroiliitis.jpg)

![Bamboo spine with marginal syndesmophytes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2044-shows-bamboo-spine.jpg)

**Fractures on X-ray** — **negative in 50%** → CT

## 5 Management

**First line** — **NSAIDs / COX-2** + PT (flexibility) · no **oral steroids** (oral or IV — no effect on progression)

**Second line** — **TNF-α inhibitors**: infliximab, etanercept, **adalimumab** (also approved for RA + PsA → "husband AS + wife RA on same drug" = adalimumab)\
✗ Wrong options: denosumab · tofacitinib (RA) · imatinib · rituximab (RA)

**Early AS with fused SIJs** — **NSAIDs + PT + HLA-B27 test**\
✗ Wrong options: SI injections · SI fusion · aspiration

### Spine fractures – image whole spine, immobilise in existing posture

**Epidemiology** — risk **4×** general population · **mid-cervical + cervicothoracic** (C5–7) commonest (> 50%), then TL junction\
✗ Wrong options: lumbar commonest

**Pattern** — **extension**, **3-column**, behaves like a **long-bone fracture** (long lever arm + osteoporosis)

**Outcomes** — neuro involvement **\~75%** (67% AS vs 40% DISH) · occult multi-level fractures **13%** · diagnostic delay up to **40%** · mortality up to **32–34%** · complications up to 84% (pneumonia, respiratory failure, pseudarthrosis) · fusion rates **high** once fixed

```
AS patient + neck/back pain after trivial trauma
├─ Immobilise in EXISTING KYPHOTIC (flexed) posture → HARD COLLAR, ADMIT
│      (no neutral/extension, soft collar, backboard straightening or flex-ex films)
├─ CT OF ENTIRE SPINE (X-rays miss 50%)
└─ New / progressive deficit → MRI → EPIDURAL HAEMATOMA
       └─ Compressive haematoma → POSTERIOR DECOMPRESSION + LONG INSTRUMENTED FUSION
```

**Neuro deficits often delayed** — admit + serial exams even if CT negative

### Epidural haematoma & neurological deterioration

**Incidence** — up to **15%**; hypervascular epidural tissue + torn epidural veins in a rigid canal; high mortality

**Delayed weakness hours after fall (CT negative or nondisplaced)** — **epidural haemorrhage**\
✗ Wrong options: further displacement once immobilised · degenerative stenosis · vertebral artery · myelomalacia · cord infarct

**Dorsal epidural haematoma + progressive deficit** — **posterior laminectomy + instrumented fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: steroids · repeat MRI · anterior fusion · laminectomy alone · halo (↑ mortality)

### Fracture fixation – long-segment posterior instrumentation

**Operative indications** — **progressive deficit** · **epidural haematoma with compromise** · **unstable fracture** (virtually all, even minimally displaced, neuro intact)

**Construct** — **long posterior**, **≥ 3 levels above + 3 below**, multiple fixation points (osteoporosis + lever arm) · **do NOT correct deformity** · 360° if very osteoporotic / incomplete SCI with unstable C6 · (✗ Wrong options: short-segment · uninstrumented fusion · halo (septic + pulmonary issues in elderly))\
✗ Wrong options: stand-alone anterior → high failure

**Non-op** — stable, no deficit, or unfit: immobilise in existing kyphosis, admit; low-weight traction may reduce

**Complications** — progressive deformity, nonunion, hardware failure, infection

### Kyphotic deformity correction

**Exclude hip flexion contracture first** · goal = sagittal balance + **horizontal gaze**

| Osteotomy | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Smith-Petersen (SPO)** | Posterior column (pars + facets), pivot = **posterior annulus**; needs **mobile anterior disc**; **10° per 10 mm** resected (TO); **simplest** (TO); C7 plumb 6–8 cm positive; ↓ foraminal height |
| **Ponte** | Modification of SPO; **wider** facet + lamina resection; originally **thoracic** (SPO originally **lumbar**, AS flat back) — Schwab grade 2 vs SPO grade 1 (TO) |
| **Pedicle subtraction (PSO)** | **Procedure of choice for TL kyphosis**; closing wedge, hinge **anterior cortex**; **30–40° per level**; single level, no disc resection; direct bony apposition → better fusion; works with fused discs / 360° fusion; SVA > 4 cm (> 12 cm ideal); transient deficits up to **20%** |
| Opening wedge (single) | Hinges on **posterior** body edge; splits ALL |
| Multi-segment opening | Less bone loss, preserves ALL |
| Bone-disc-bone | 35–60°; hard in AS (ossified annulus) |
| **Vertebral column resection** | Most correction; requires disc resection |
| **C7-T1 cervicothoracic extension osteotomy** | **Chin-on-chest** deformity; **posterior extension osteotomy + posterior instrumented fusion** (✗ Wrong options: anterior osteotomy (no access to C7 pedicle) · halo traction · in-situ fusion) |

**Why lumbar osteotomy** — below conus, wide canal, avoids thoracic cage, long lever arm → more correction; upper lumbar level · **spinal motion not increased** (TO)

**Why C7-T1** — **vertebral artery outside transverse foramen at C7**, wide canal · remove C7 lateral mass + parts of C7/T1 pedicles · aim **slight undercorrection (CBVA \~10°)** · halo post-op if poor bone · **sitting position → venous air embolus**; paradoxical embolus risk ↑ with **patent foramen ovale**\
✗ Wrong options: N₂O · hypotension · obesity · emphysema

**THA before spinal osteotomy (TO)** — THA carries less risk; operate both hips together if significant flexion contractures\
✗ Wrong options: "cervicothoracic osteotomy before THA"

### Hip arthroplasty in AS

**Acetabula** — more **vertical + anteverted**; altered spinopelvic relationship → ↑ **dislocation**, especially **anterior** (posterior approach still → anterior dislocation risk)\
✗ Wrong options: infection · LLD · sciatic palsy (dysplasia) · abductor lurch (lateral approach)

**Bilateral THA** — kyphosis due to hip flexion contractures

### Reactive arthritis – pathogenesis & treatment

**Definition (TO)** — HLA-B27-associated seronegative SpA after **GU (Chlamydia trachomatis 40–60%)** or enteric (Salmonella, Shigella, Yersinia, Campylobacter) infection; onset **1–6 wks (2–4)** later; M:F 5–10:1

**Features** — **asymmetric lower-limb oligoarthritis**, **dactylitis**, enthesitis (Achilles, plantar fascia), **asymmetric sacroiliitis**, conjunctivitis, anterior uveitis, **circinate balanitis**, **keratoderma blennorrhagicum** (soles), urethritis; full Reiter triad in only 1/3 · HLA-B27 **60–80%**

**Pathogenesis (TO)** — local immune response to **persistent, metabolically active, non-culturable bacteria / antigens in synovium**; synovial fluid WBC 10–50k, culture negative\
✗ Wrong options: "completely sterile"

```
Reactive arthritis treatment (TO)
├─ Treat trigger → DOXYCYCLINE (+ treat partners)
├─ Arthritis → FULL-DOSE NSAIDs ± intra-articular steroid
└─ Persistent > 3–6 months despite NSAIDs → SULFASALAZINE (first csDMARD)
       └─ Refractory / axial / enthesitis → TNF INHIBITOR
```

## X vs Y

| | AS | DISH |
|---|---|---|
| Syndesmophytes | **Marginal**, vertical | **Non-marginal**, "flowing candle wax" (≥ 4 levels) |
| X-ray | **Bamboo spine**, squaring, **Romanus shiny corners** | Disc space **preserved** |
| Disc | Involved / ossified (cervical) | Preserved |
| Bone density | **Osteopenia** | Normal / ↑ |
| HLA | **B27** | **B8** (with diabetes) |
| Age | Young | Older |
| SI joint | **Bilateral sacroiliitis** | Spared |
| Diabetes | No | **Yes** |
| Fracture neuro deficit | 67% | 40% |

![Syndesmophytes: AS (marginal) vs DISH (non-marginal)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2044-syndesmophyte-formation-in-as-vs-dish.jpg)

| | AS | Reactive arthritis |
|---|---|---|
| Sacroiliitis | **Bilateral symmetric** | **Asymmetric** |
| Peripheral | Hip / shoulder | Asymmetric LL oligoarthritis, dactylitis |
| Urethritis | No | Yes |
| B27 | 90% | 60–80% |

| | Smith-Petersen | Ponte | PSO |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pivot | Posterior annulus | — | **Anterior cortex** |
| Correction | 10°/10 mm | Slightly > 10° | 30–40° |
| Needs mobile disc | Yes | Yes | No |

## How it's asked

**"Romanus lesion seen in?"** → **MRI of LS spine in AS** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: X-ray

**"Wall test useful in?"** → **cervical spine involvement in AS** (TO)

**"HLA-B27 prevalence in AS, juvenile SpA, RA?"** → **90%, 70%, 6%** (TO)

**"AS diagnostic criteria — all EXCEPT?"** → **HLA-B27 positivity** (TO, modified New York)

**"Bilateral sacroiliitis + B27 — what confirms diagnosis?"** → **uveitis**\
✗ Wrong options: erythema marginatum · HLA-DR3 · RF · urine phosphoethanolamine

**"Juvenile AS — all EXCEPT?"** → **urethritis**

**"AS patient expresses?"** → **class I antigen, B locus, MHC, chromosome 6**

**"Bicycle fall, AS on X-ray — true?"** → **HLA-B27 positive**\
✗ Wrong options: Klippel-Feil · Japanese OPLL · DISH 4-level · RF positive

**"26-yr with hip flexion contractures + SI sclerosis?"** → **HLA-B27 positive**\
✗ Wrong options: FADDIR · normal ESR/CRP

**"Fused SIJs in 35-yr — next?"** → **NSAIDs + PT + HLA-B27 testing**

**"RF-negative bilateral sacroiliitis — additional finding?"** → **eye inflammation**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ FEV · non-marginal syndesmophytes · champagne-glass pelvis · diabetes

**"AS — true?"** → **often involves entire spine**\
✗ Wrong options: female · diabetes · SI rarely involved · oral steroids

**"B27-positive AS — ↑ synovitis risk from?"** → **gram-negative bacillus (Klebsiella)**

**"Husband AS, wife RA, same drug?"** → **adalimumab**

**"AS after fall on ice, neck pain, neuro intact — next?"** → **CT cervical spine**\
✗ Wrong options: flex-ex · bone scan · soft collar

**"Chin-on-chest + bamboo spine after fall — management?"** → **hard collar in existing kyphosis, admit, advanced imaging**

**"Immobilise cervicothoracic kyphosis during work-up?"** → **maintain flexed position**

**"Marginal syndesmophytes, posterior neck pain after fall?"** → **immobilise + CT C-spine + X-ray T/L spine**

**"AS on etanercept, rear-ended, X-rays negative?"** → **CT cervical, thoracic + lumbar**

**"AS fracture — true statement?"** → **CT entire spinal axis**\
✗ Wrong options: occult injuries rare · lumbar commonest · delay uncommon · low mortality

**"Deficit 7 h after nondisplaced extension fracture, dorsal haematoma on MRI?"** → **posterior laminectomy + instrumented fusion**

**"C7-T1 fracture + posterior haematoma + progressive weakness?"** → **posterior decompression + instrumented fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: halo (↑ mortality)

**"Principal concern with AS fracture-dislocation?"** → **epidural haematoma**

**"Normal CT, severe weakness 3 h later — MRI shows?"** → **epidural haemorrhage**

**"HLA-B27 man, TL fracture after 2-stair fall, intact?"** → **posterior fusion with long-segment pedicle screws**

**"AS shear fracture C5-6 with hand weakness?"** → **posterior decompression + fusion C3-T2**

**"Minimally displaced 3-column lumbar AS fracture, intact?"** → **multilevel posterior instrumentation + fusion**

**"C6 fracture, incomplete SCI, absent BCR — definitive?"** → **open reduction, decompression, A + P stabilisation**

**"AS cervical fracture — true?"** → **long posterior instrumentation ± decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: low fusion rate

**"Which fracture suits posterior cervicothoracic fusion?"** → **3-column AS fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion teardrop → anterior · Jefferson → halo · type 3 hangman → C2-3 ACDF · type 3 odontoid → collar

**"Osteotomy in AS — true?"** → **SPO gives 10° per 10 mm** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ motion · cervical before THA · thoracic for lungs

**"SPO vs Ponte — incorrect statement?"** → **"SPO described for thoracic, Ponte for lumbar"** (TO — reverse)

**"True about osteotomies in AS?"** → **SPO simpler than PSO** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: PSO pivots on posterior body

**"Most single-level correction without disc resection?"** → **PSO**

**"Chin-on-chest deformity — management?"** → **posterior extension osteotomy + posterior instrumented fusion**

**"Greatest risk for paradoxical VAE in beach-chair?"** → **patent foramen ovale**

**"THA in AS (posterior approach) — risk?"** → **anterior dislocation**

**"Bilateral THA in AS — counsel about?"** → **periprosthetic dislocation**

**"Chlamydia reactive arthritis — pathogenesis?"** → **persistent non-culturable bacteria/antigens in synovium** (TO)

**"SARA failing 14 weeks of NSAIDs?"** → **sulfasalazine** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: TNF first · high-dose steroids · methotrexate superior
