# Atlantoaxial Instability

*Spine · 6 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Aetiology

**C1-2** — "transitional zone" → instability from degenerative + traumatic causes

| | Degenerative / congenital | Traumatic / infective |
|---|---|---|
| **Adult** | **Down syndrome** · **rheumatoid arthritis** · **os odontoideum** | Type I odontoid fracture (very rare) · atlas fractures · transverse ligament injury |
| **Paediatric** | **JRA** · **Morquio** (lysosomal storage) | **Rotatory atlantoaxial subluxation** · Grisel syndrome |

**Os odontoideum = ADULT cause (TO)** — not paediatric (EXCEPT answer) · aetiology debated, most support **post-traumatic**

**Other causes (TO)** — skeletal dysplasias (**pseudoachondroplasia – COMP**), neurofibromatosis, Grisel (infection)

**Pseudoachondroplasia vs achondroplasia** — pseudoachondroplasia → **atlantoaxial instability** · achondroplasia → **lumbar stenosis**

### Anatomy & ligaments

| Ligament | Connects / role |
|---|---|
| **Transverse atlantal ligament (TAL)** | Horizontal limb of cruciate; medial tubercles of C1; straps dens to anterior arch; **most important stabiliser — strongest ligament of the spine** |
| **Alar ligaments** | **Paired parasagittal**; dens → occipital condyles (+ C1 lateral mass); "check" ligaments → **limit rotation** |
| **Apical ligament** | **Single midline**; dens tip → foramen magnum; little strength |
| Tectorial membrane | Cephalad continuation of **PLL**; dens/C2 → foramen magnum |
| Anterior / posterior atlanto-occipital membranes | ALL continuation / posterior FM → posterior C1 arch |

![Transverse, alar and apical ligament complex](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2049-combined-4.jpg)

**Biomechanics** — C1-2 provides **\~50%** (Orthobullets) · **60%** (TO) of cervical rotation; peg-in-ring; near-horizontal facets

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — neck pain, neuro symptoms

**Deficits appear late** — capacious canal at C1 (Steel's rule of thirds)

**Myelopathy** — hyperreflexia, weakness, **broad-based gait**, ↓ hand dexterity, loss of motor milestones, bladder dysfunction

**Child, trivial fall → transient quadriparesis, recovered (TO)** — **congenital atlantoaxial instability**\
✗ Wrong options: ossified LF (age) · syrinx (wouldn't recover) · bifid atlas

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Measure | Definition | Threshold |
|---|---|---|
| **Atlantodens interval (ADI)** | Dens → **posterior border of anterior C1 arch**, flexion-extension | Adult **> 3.5 mm** unstable (Orthobullets) · **> 3 mm** (TO) · child **> 5 mm** · **> 10 mm → surgery in RA** |
| **Space available for cord (SAC / PADI)** | Posterior dens → anterior posterior arch | Normal 17–29 mm · **< 14 mm** → ↑ neuro risk, surgery in RA |
| **Sum of lateral mass displacement (open mouth)** | C1 masses over C2 | **> 8.1 mm** → TAL rupture, unstable (Orthobullets) · **> 6.9 mm** Rule of Spence (TO) |

![ADI and SAC on lateral flexion view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2049-adi-labels.jpg)

**Lateral masses can displace only if** — C1 ring fractured **or** TAL ruptured

**Flexion-extension views** — mandatory pre-op in **all high-risk patients** (Down, RA, dysplasia) before any surgery

**Pseudoachondroplasia + myelopathy** — **flexion-extension C-spine; ADI difference 6 mm**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral mass 9 mm = atlas fracture · hip OA · scoliosis · lumbar stenosis

**CT** — essential for rotated C1 on C2 · MRI for TAL

### Fielding & Hawkins classification (TO)

| Type | Displacement | TAL | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | Simple **rotatory**, no anterior shift | Intact | Collar 6–8 wks |
| **II** | Anterior **3–5 mm**, one lateral mass pivots | Deficient | Collar / surgery |
| **III** | Anterior **> 5 mm** | Disrupted | **C1-2 fusion** (unstable) |
| **IV** | **Posterior** displacement (dens erosion) | — | **C1-2 fusion** (unstable) |

## 5 Management

**Determined by specific condition** (RA, Down, os odontoideum, fracture, TAL injury, JRA, Morquio, AARS)

### Operative criteria (TO)

| Adults | Children (≥ 1 of) |
|---|---|
| **ADI > 5 mm** | **Neurologic deficit** |
| Moderate displacement / instability on dynamic films | **Persistent ADI > 4 mm** |
| RA: ADI > 10 mm or SAC < 14 mm | Deformity **> 3 months** |
| — | Recurrence despite **6 weeks** immobilisation |

### C1-2 instability – os odontoideum

```
Os odontoideum
├─ Asymptomatic, stable → STOP CONTACT SPORTS
└─ Neuro signs or widened ADI → POSTERIOR C1-2 FUSION
       (not anterior C1-2 fusion — osseous anatomy)
```

### Grisel syndrome

**Definition (TO)** — inflammatory **atlantoaxial rotatory subluxation** after **upper respiratory / head-neck infection** (or **tonsillectomy, adenoid** inflammation); hyperaemia damages facet capsules + TAL

**Presentation (TO)** — child, **painful torticollis** ("cock-robin": chin rotated one way, neck tilted the other, slight flexion), **facial asymmetry**, **change in voice / nasal resonance**, painful attempted correction\
✗ Wrong options: muscular torticollis (painless, infant) · Sandifer (reflux)

| Grisel type | Treatment (TO) |
|---|---|
| I | **Soft collar** |
| II | **Hard collar** |
| III | **Halo** |
| IV | **Open fixation** (neuro complications) |

## X vs Y

| | Grisel | Muscular torticollis |
|---|---|---|
| Age | Child (\~4–12) | Infant |
| Trigger | URTI / tonsillectomy | Birth (intrauterine positioning) |
| Pain | **Painful** | Painless |
| Mass | No | SCM tumour |
| Head | **Cock-robin**: tilt one side, chin to the other, slight flexion | Tilt toward, chin away from tight SCM |
| Lesion | C1-2 rotatory subluxation | SCM fibrosis |

| | ADI | SAC (PADI) |
|---|---|---|
| Measures | Anterior instability | Cord space |
| Abnormal | > 3.5 mm adult, > 5 mm child | **< 14 mm** |
| RA surgery | > 10 mm | < 14 mm |

## How it's asked

**"NOT a cause of paediatric atlantoaxial instability?"** → **os odontoideum** (TO)

**"10-yr, trivial fall, transient quadriparesis?"** → **congenital atlantoaxial instability** (TO)

**"11-yr os odontoideum with myelopathic signs — treatment?"** → **posterior C1-2 fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · soft collar · stop sports only · anterior fusion

**"Pseudoachondroplasia with myelopathy — study + finding?"** → **flexion-extension C-spine, ADI difference 6 mm**

**"4-yr girl, painful torticollis, facial asymmetry, voice change after URTI?"** → **Grisel syndrome** (TO)

**"Most important C1-2 stabiliser?"** → **transverse ligament**

**"Single midline CVJ ligament?"** → **apical ligament**

**"Sum of lateral mass overhang implying TAL rupture?"** → **> 8.1 mm** (Orthobullets) · **> 6.9 mm** (TO, Spence) — read the options

**"SAC predicting neuro injury in RA?"** → **< 14 mm**

**"ADI needing surgery in RA?"** → **> 10 mm**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Atlantoaxial Rotatory Displacement (AARD)

*Spine · 7 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Aetiology

**Definition** — paediatric C1-2 rotatory instability: spectrum from mild subluxation / muscle spasm → **fixed facet dislocation** (mechanical block)

| Cause | Share |
|---|---|
| **Infection** (pharyngitis, otitis media, URTI, retropharyngeal abscess) | **~35%** — **Grisel's disease**; lymphatic oedema around C-spine |
| **Trauma** (even trivial — fall from bed) | **~24%** |
| **Recent head / neck surgery** (tonsillectomy) | **\~20%** |
| Idiopathic | — |
| Associated | **Down syndrome**, **RA**, tumours, congenital anomalies |

**Mechanism** — ligamentous laxity

**TAL intact** — canal stenosis only with **severe rotation + facet dislocation**

**TAL ruptured + anterolisthesis > 5 mm** — stenosis with **less rotation (45°)**; vertebral arteries at risk

### Anatomy

**C2 ossification** — **5 centres** · **subdental (basilar) synchondrosis** between dens + body fuses **~6 yrs** · secondary (ossiculum terminale) centre appears **~3 yrs**, fuses **~12 yrs**

**Ligaments** — **transverse** limits anterior translation of atlas · **apical + alar** limit rotation

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — head tilt, neck pain, headache, ↓ rotation

**Posture** — **"cock-robin"**: chin rotated **away from** the subluxed facet, head tilted toward it (≈ 20° tilt, 20° rotation, 10° flexion)

**SCM spasm** — on the **SAME side as the chin** (protective, secondary) — e.g. right facet subluxation → chin left → **left SCM spastic**

**5-yr after tonsillectomy** — right neck bend, left head turn, SCM spasm → AARS

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **AP open-mouth** | Asymmetric **size + distance from midline** of lateral masses |
| **Lateral** | Facet appears **anterior + wedge-shaped** (not oval) |
| Flexion-extension | Exclude instability (hard to obtain) |
| **Dynamic CT** | **Gold standard** — neutral + maximal right + left rotation → **fixed C1-on-C2 rotation unchanged** with turning |
| MRI | Little value unless neuro signs |

**Diagnosis is primarily clinical** (OB) · CT/MRI for persistent cases · labs (CBC, ESR, CRP) for infection

### Fielding (& Hawkins) classification

| Type | Lesion | TAL | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Unilateral** facet subluxation; **odontoid = pivot** (one facet forward, one back) | **Intact** | **Commonest, benign** |
| **II** | Unilateral, **3–5 mm** anterior; one facet = pivot | Injured | — |
| **III** | **Bilateral** anterior **> 5 mm** | Disrupted | Rare; neuro risk / sudden death |
| **IV** | **Posterior** C1 displacement (odontoid fracture / hypoplastic dens) | — | Rare; neuro risk / sudden death |

![Fielding type I: rotation about the dens](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2050-aard-pic.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Chronicity is the key factor** — earlier treatment → better conservative success; many reduce spontaneously before presentation

```
AARD by duration (Orthobullets)
├─ < 1 week → SOFT COLLAR + NSAIDs + exercises
├─ > 2 weeks / failed collar × 2 wks → HALTER TRACTION (~5 lb) + BENZODIAZEPINES → hard collar 3 months
├─ > 1 month / failed halter × 2 wks → HALO TRACTION → halo vest 3 months
└─ > 3 months / neuro deficit / failed halo × 2 wks / recurrent → POSTERIOR C1-2 FUSION
```

**Timing variants (OB)** — soft collar < 7 days · halter 1 wk–1 month · halo 1–3 months · or sequential **2-week trials** (collar → halter → skeletal traction → arthrodesis) — at least **2 weeks** of collar before traction

### Early management – collar, NSAIDs & traction

**Acute (3–5 days, post-URTI / tonsillectomy)** — **soft collar** + NSAIDs + antispasmodic\
✗ Wrong options: halter · halo · fusion · observation alone

**1 week of collar without resolution** — **continue collar ≥ 1 more week** then reassess\
✗ Wrong options: immediate halter · stretching

**Fixed torticollis 4 weeks after fall** — **cervical (halter) traction** · halo needed when child's skin can't tolerate halter weight\
✗ Wrong options: soft collar · SCM release · fusion

### Chronic / irreducible – C1-2 arthrodesis

**Failed halter + halo traction, still subluxed on dynamic CT** — **posterior atlantoaxial (C1-2) fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: no treatment · closed reduction under sedation/GA · occipitocervical fusion (loses motion) · more sedation · more weight · halo vest/C-collar unless reduced

**Halo vest** — only to **maintain** a reduction achieved by halo traction

## 6 Complications

**Missed / delayed diagnosis** — commonest problem

**Type III/IV** — neuro injury, vertebral artery compromise, death

## X vs Y

| | AARD | Congenital muscular torticollis |
|---|---|---|
| Primary lesion | C1-2 subluxation | SCM fibrosis |
| SCM spasm vs chin | **Same side** as chin | **Opposite** side to chin |
| Age | Child, acute after URTI / trauma / surgery | Infant |
| Pain | Painful | Painless |
| Rx | Collar → traction → C1-2 fusion | Stretching → SCM release |

## How it's asked

**"5-yr, fixed torticollis 4 weeks after fall, CT rotatory fixation — next?"** → **cervical traction**\
✗ Wrong options: soft collar · SCM release

**"6-yr, head turned 3 days after URTI, no deficit?"** → **soft collar**\
✗ Wrong options: halter · halo · arthrodesis

**"7-yr, 1 week of collar, still tilted?"** → **continue soft collar ≥ 1 week, re-evaluate**

**"Painful head tilt 5 days after tonsillectomy?"** → **cervical collar + NSAIDs + antispasmodic**

**"3-yr, 8 months of torticollis, failed halter + halo, still subluxed?"** → **posterior atlantoaxial fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: occipitocervical fusion

**"12-yr, 10 weeks, failed 2 wks halter + 3 wks halo?"** → **C1-2 arthrodesis**

**"Gold-standard diagnosis?"** → **dynamic CT**

**"Commonest Fielding type?"** → **type I — unilateral, TAL intact, dens pivot**

**"Right facet subluxation — chin + SCM spasm?"** → **chin left, left SCM spastic**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Atlas Fractures & Transverse Ligament Injuries

*Spine · 8 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **\~7% of cervical fractures** · 25% of CVJ injuries · 1–3% of all spinal injuries · **commonly missed**

**Bimodal** — **20–30 yrs** high-energy axial load (\~85% MVC) · **elderly** low-energy ground-level fall (OA, osteoporosis, gait imbalance)

**Associated injuries** — **50%** other spine injury · **40% axis (C2) fracture** · head injury

**Neuro injury rare** — capacious canal: cord occupies only **\~35%** at C1 (vs 50% lower) · burst **increases** space at C1

### Mechanism

| Force | Injury |
|---|---|
| **Axial compression** (blow to vertex, diving) | **Jefferson burst** |
| **Hyperextension** (blow to forehead) | **Posterior arch** fracture; low-energy falls; ↑ with **odontoid fractures**; 30% less energy needed in extension |
| Lateral compression | Anterior arch fracture |
| Lateral distraction | Comminuted lateral mass |

### Anatomy

**Atlas** — ring with 2 lateral masses, **no body, no spinous process**; **3 ossification centres**

**Incomplete posterior arch** — common **congenital variant**, not trauma

**Vertebral artery** — C1 transverse foramen → bends posteromedially across **sulcal groove** (common site of posterior arch fracture)

| Ligament | Role |
|---|---|
| **Transverse (TAL)** | **Main stabiliser of C1-2** (TO); posterior dens → anterior arch, inserts on **lateral mass tubercles**; prevents dens migrating into canal |
| **Alar (paired)** | Dens → occipital condyles; strong (5–6 mm); occipitocervical stability |
| **Apical** | Weak midline, dens → foramen magnum |
| **Tectorial membrane** | Posterior C2 body → anterior foramen magnum; **cephalad PLL** |

**Layers anterior to dura (dorsal → ventral)** — tectorial membrane → cruciate ligament → odontoid (alar + apical) ligaments

**Motion** — **occiput-C1 \~50% of flexion-extension** · **C1-2 \~50% of rotation**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — neck pain, spasm, ↓ motion, suboccipital pain, sense of instability

**C2 / greater occipital nerve** — **occipital neuralgia** (TO: commonest nerve injury — emerges below posterior arch), occipital numbness, rarely alopecia, neck flexion/extension weakness

**Vertebral artery dissection** — LOC, diplopia, vertigo, blindness, ataxia, dysphagia, nausea

**Neuro deficit** — uncommon in isolated C1; ↑ with associated C2 fracture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Measure | Normal / abnormal |
|---|---|
| **ADI** | Adult **< 3 mm** normal (child < 5) · **3–5 mm** = **TAL torn, alar + apical intact** · **> 5 mm** = TAL + alar + tectorial membrane (OB: **> 7 mm**) |
| **Sum of lateral mass displacement (open mouth / coronal CT)** | **> 6.9 mm (rule of Spence)** or **> 8.1 mm with magnification (rule of Heller)** → TAL rupture, unstable (OB: ≥ 7 mm / 8.2 mm) |
| SAC (PADI) | Normal ≥ 13–14 mm; doesn't predict TAL injury |
| Retropharyngeal soft tissue | **> 9.5 mm** → anterior arch injury |
| Basion-dens interval | < 12 mm normal (occipitocervical) |
| Power's ratio | Occipitocervical instability |
| C2 pars displacement | Hangman fracture |

![Jefferson fracture: lateral mass overhang on open-mouth view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2015-xray-jefferson-fx.jpg)

**X-rays** — lateral, **60° obliques** (posterior arch), **open-mouth** · flexion-extension only for **late instability** after non-op (not in ED)

**CT** — every suspected injury; **sagittal** recon = occult anterior arch fracture · **axial** = Dickman II avulsion · **coronal** = LMD · CTA for vertebral artery

**Paediatric pseudospread** — asymmetric atlas vs axis growth → lateral mass overhang without fracture (CT)

**MRI** — every confirmed C1 fracture; **↑ T2 signal in TAL = intrasubstance tear**; **more sensitive than CT for TAL**

**LMD caveat** — measurements may underestimate due to **rebound** after injury

### Landells classification

| Type | Pattern | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Isolated anterior or posterior arch** — **commonest**; "plough" = isolated anterior arch from dens driven through it | **Hard collar** |
| **2** | **Jefferson burst** — bilateral anterior + posterior arch, axial load | TAL intact → **hard collar** · intrasubstance tear → **C1-2 fusion** · bony avulsion → **halo** |
| **3** | **Unilateral lateral mass** | TAL intact → hard collar · disrupted → halo |

![Landells atlas fracture patterns](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2015-illustration-depicting-the-different-fracture-patterns-of-th.jpg)

### Dickman classification (TAL injury)

| Type | Lesion | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Intrasubstance tear** (won't heal) | **C1-2 fusion** |
| **II** | **Bony avulsion** of C1 lateral mass tubercle | **Halo** (75% success) |

## 5 Management

```
Atlas fracture
├─ TAL intact (LMD < 7 mm, normal ADI) → RIGID COLLAR 6–12 WKS
│      (not soft collar or observation)
├─ TAL bony avulsion (Dickman II) → HALO 3 months
└─ TAL intrasubstance tear (Dickman I) → POSTERIOR C1-2 FUSION
       └─ Comminuted C1 / no C1 purchase → OCCIPUT-C2 FUSION
```

**Elderly** — **never halo** (↑ mortality) → hard collar / surgery · clear C-spine expeditiously

**Non-op** — reduce with halo traction first if displaced · **post-treatment flexion-extension films** for late instability

**Operative indications** — unstable type 2/3 (controversial) · **Dickman I** · **combined C1 + C2** fractures (type II odontoid, hangman) · some prefer occiput-C2 (lower revision)

**C1 internal fixation** — lateral mass split; preserves C1-2 motion (small series)

### Fusion technique

| Technique | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Harms / Goel (C1 lateral mass + C2 pedicle screws)** | **10° medial trajectory** protects **internal carotid** (bicortical) (✗ Wrong options: vertebral artery · cord (at risk if too medial)) |
| **C1-2 transarticular screw (Magerl)** | **Vertebral artery** at risk in C2 groove (\~10% on CT planning) (TO) |
| **Gallie sublaminar wiring** | Needs intact posterior arch → valid for **TAL disruption** (✗ Wrong options: OC dissociation · comminuted C1 · type I/III odontoid) |
| **Occiput-C2** | Comminuted C1; occipital plate |

**Motion loss** — C1-2 fusion → **\~50% rotation** · occiput-C2 → **50% rotation + 50% flexion-extension** (TO)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| Vertebral artery | Displaced posterior ring through **sulcal groove** |
| **Cock-robin deformity** | Displaced **unilateral sagittal split lateral mass** → occipital condyle settles on C2 → **occipitocervical fusion ± osteotomy** |
| **Nonunion** | **\~20%** non-op |
| Neck pain / stiffness | 14–80% pain, 8–20% stiffness, 34% activity limits |
| Infection | ↑ with posterior approach |
| Prognosis | Worse PROMs with **> 7 mm** displacement |

## X vs Y

| ADI | Ligaments torn |
|---|---|
| < 3 mm | None |
| 3–5 mm | TAL only |
| > 5 mm (> 7 mm OB) | TAL + alar + tectorial membrane |

| | Dickman I | Dickman II |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Midsubstance | Bony avulsion |
| Heals? | No | Yes (75%) |
| Rx | C1-2 fusion | Halo |

## How it's asked

**"Main stabiliser of C1-C2?"** → **transverse ligament** (TO)

**"ADI > 7 mm implies?"** → **TAL + alar ruptured + tectorial membrane ruptured**

**"Jefferson fracture — which indicates TAL disruption?"** → **combined LMD 8.2 mm**\
✗ Wrong options: ADI 2 · PADI 16 · pars shift · Power's ratio

**"Diving injury, LMD 8.3 mm — true?"** → **TAL disrupted, unstable → rigid orthosis / halo / surgery**

**"Comminuted C1, which suggests TAL rupture?"** → **LMD 9 mm**\
✗ Wrong options: SAC 15 · ADI 2 · BDI 11 · PVS 5 mm

**"Occiput-C2 fusion — motion loss?"** → **50% rotation + 50% flexion-extension** (TO)

**"Gallie C1-2 wiring valid for?"** → **TAL disruption**

**"Jefferson with intact TAL?"** → **rigid collar 6–12 weeks**

**"MRI shows intrasubstance TAL rupture 2 weeks after injury?"** → **surgical stabilisation**\
✗ Wrong options: collar · halo

**"Elderly, stable C1 fracture after slip?"** → **hard collar**\
✗ Wrong options: halo · flex-ex films · fusion

**"10° medial C1 lateral mass screw protects?"** → **internal carotid artery**

**"Transarticular C1-2 screw — structure at risk?"** → **vertebral artery** (TO)

**"True about Jefferson fracture?"** → **greater occipital neuralgia** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: high neuro injury · type 1 → halo · intact TAL → fusion

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cauda Equina Syndrome

*Spine · 9 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **rare**: 1.5–3.4 per million/yr; prevalence \~1/65,000 · in **\~3%** of lumbar disc herniations (TO: 1–10%)

**Demographics** — **males**, **4th decade (30s)**

**Level** — **L4-5** commonest

### Causes

| Cause | Note |
|---|---|
| **Disc herniation** | **Commonest** (large central HNP) (✗ Wrong options: burst fracture commonest) |
| **Spinal epidural haematoma** | Spontaneous on high-dose DVT prophylaxis, **epidural anaesthesia**, early post-op prophylaxis |
| Tumours | Myxopapillary ependymoma, schwannoma, meningioma |
| Synovial facet cyst · epidural abscess | Ask about **IVDU** |
| Trauma | **Lumbar burst** retropulsion, dislocation |
| High-grade developmental spondylolisthesis | Intact lamina |

### Pathobiology

**Compression** → ↓ nutrient delivery (↓ blood flow, ↓ CSF diffusion) → venous congestion + **intraneural oedema** → "intraneural compartment syndrome" → **root ischaemia**

**Vulnerability** — roots covered only by **endoneurium** (no Schwann cell covering per TO) + hypovascular central zone

### Anatomy

**Conus medullaris** — terminal cord, ends **T12–L1** · **filum terminale** → coccyx

**Cauda equina** — **L1–S5 roots** in lumbar canal → **lower motor neuron** lesion

| Bladder innervation | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Parasympathetic** (pelvic splanchnic, S2–4) | **Voiding** — detrusor contracts, internal sphincter relaxes |
| **Sympathetic** (hypogastric plexus) | **Retention** — detrusor relaxes, internal sphincter contracts |
| **Pudendal nerve** | **External sphincter** (voluntary) |

**CES bladder** — LMN interrupts reflex arc → can't sense filling → **retention → overflow incontinence**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — heavy lifting with flexed spine, coughing spell · ask **anticoagulants**, recent procedures (haematoma), **IVDU** (infection)

| Symptom | Frequency |
|---|---|
| **Back pain** | **Commonest** (Orthobullets, OB) — may be the only initial symptom · 83% |
| **Leg pain (radicular)** | **2nd commonest** (Orthobullets) · 90% — "commonest presenting symptom" in some OB stems — read the options |
| Urinary retention | 60% |
| Urinary incontinence | 55% |
| Leg weakness / sensory deficit / absent ankle jerk | 55% / 62% / 62% |
| Perianal sensory deficit | 76% |
| ↓ rectal tone | 50% (relatively **late**) |
| Sexual / bowel dysfunction | Rare |

**Order of frequency** — **leg pain > urinary retention > urinary incontinence**

**Saddle anaesthesia** — less common but **most specific** → trigger emergency surgery protocol

**Saddle anaesthesia alone, no other LL deficit (TO)** — lesion **below S2** (S3–S5)

**Exam** — palpate bladder · ↓ voluntary rectal tone · **pinprick** loss S2–S4 perianal/perineum/posterior thigh · ↓ / absent LL reflexes · **↓/absent bulbocavernosus + anal wink** \
✗ Wrong options: **Babinski** (UMN sign — EXCEPT answer)

**Classic triad (TO)** — **asymmetric areflexic paralysis** + **bladder/bowel (S2–4)** + **saddle anaesthesia** (S3–4, **perianal S5 spared initially**)

**32-yr, unilateral radiculopathy + saddle anaesthesia (TO)** — **CES + urinary retention + absent ankle jerk**\
✗ Wrong options: perianal anaesthesia + areflexia · conus

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis is clinical** (history + exam); MRI confirms cause + plans surgery

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **MRI** | **Study of choice**, emergent (**within 1–2 h** of ED arrival); large **central disc** obliterating canal, haematoma, abscess |
| **CT myelography** | **Can't have MRI (pacemaker)** (TO: myelo-CT; ✗ Wrong options: plain CT · angiography · no imaging) |
| X-ray | Usually normal; don't delay MRI; burst fracture / high-grade slip |

### Genes & lab markers

**CBC, ESR, CRP** — if abscess suspected (fever, IVDU) — not routine\
✗ Wrong options: labs before MRI without infection signs

**Post-void residual (PVR)** — pre-op if it doesn't delay surgery · normal **< 50–100 mL** · **PVR < 200 mL → 97% NPV** · **> 200 mL highly suggestive**\
✗ Wrong options: "> 200 mL excludes"

### Classification – bladder function

| Grade | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Incomplete (CES-I)** | ↓ urgency / sensation, **no** retention or incontinence |
| **Complete (CES-R)** | Clear **retention** and/or incontinence |

## 5 Management

```
Suspected CES (saddle anaesthesia / bladder change + back-leg pain)
├─ EMERGENT MRI (CT myelogram if MRI contraindicated)
└─ Compression confirmed → DECOMPRESSION ASAP
       (< 24 h preferable · < 48 h standard of care)
```

### Timing of decompression

**< 48 h vs > 48 h** — significant improvement in **sensory, motor, urinary + rectal** function when < 48 h · **no added benefit < 24 h vs 24–48 h** (Ahn meta-analysis)\
✗ Wrong options: < 24 h better than < 48 h · < 60 h · < 1 week

**Not indicated** — epidural steroid injection · methylprednisolone · outpatient MRI · reassurance/bed rest

### Surgical technique

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Microdiscectomy** (unilateral laminotomy + medial facetectomy + discectomy) | Massive soft disc, **young**, minimal degeneration |
| **Wide laminectomy** (bilateral laminectomy, LF + medial facetectomy, discectomy) | **Older**, hypertrophic LF + lateral recess stenosis; ↓ dural retraction, better visualisation |
| **Laminectomy + fusion** | High-grade spondylolisthesis; insidious CES with degenerative spondylolisthesis |

**Microdiscectomy vs wide laminectomy** — **similar outcomes**; goal is adequate thecal sac decompression\
✗ Wrong options: laminectomy → greater recovery

## 6 Complications

### Prognosis – bladder dysfunction recovery

**Worse prognosis** — **saddle anaesthesia + bladder dysfunction** at presentation · **decompression > 48 h** · pre-op chronic back pain (urinary/rectal) · pre-op rectal dysfunction · older age (sexual function, neuro recovery)\
✗ Wrong options: age per se in some OB stems

**Least likely to recover** — **bladder dysfunction** · **long-term urinary dysfunction common (\~50%)** · bladder improves up to **16 months**; motor up to **1 yr**; sexual over years\
✗ Wrong options: EHL weakness · perianal sensation · foot numbness · leg pain

**Expected course** — **pain + sensory improve; residual bladder dysfunction possible**\
✗ Wrong options: complete recovery · no change

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Dural tear** | 1–4%; primary repair ± fat graft, bed rest 4–7 d, ± lumbar drain; outcomes unaffected if treated |
| Iatrogenic instability | Aggressive medial facetectomy |
| **Epidural fibrosis** | Back/leg pain at **\~3 months**; **gad MRI** separates from recurrent HNP; poor response to re-exploration |
| Wound infection | \~1%; diabetics |
| **Vascular injury** | Curette through **ALL**; mortality up to **50%** |
| DVT | Prophylaxis held for haematoma fear; antiplatelets safe at **48–72 h** post-op |

## X vs Y

| Feature (TO) | Cauda equina | Conus medullaris |
|---|---|---|
| Level of lesion | Roots, **L1 vertebra and below** | Sacral cord, **T12 (D12)–L1** (T11–L2) |
| Presentation | **Asymmetric** | **Symmetric** |
| Radicular pain | **Severe** | Usually absent |
| Sensory | **Saddle (S2–S4)**, perianal S5 spared initially | **Perianal (S5)** anaesthesia; sensory dissociation possible |
| Motor | Asymmetric **flaccid** (LMN) | Symmetric, usually flaccid, sometimes hyperreflexic |
| Reflexes | **Areflexia** (ankle; knee lost if L3–4 involved) | **Knee reflex preserved** |
| Bladder / bowel | **Late** (TO table) — retention first → overflow | **Early** |
| BCR / anal wink | Absent | **Persistently absent** (irreversible sacral cord injury) |
| Babinski | No | — |

**Diabetic, bilateral saddle anaesthesia + bladder/bowel + absent BCR (TO)** — **conus medullaris**\
✗ Wrong options: central cord · Brown-Séquard · mixed

| | Hemi-cauda equina | Complete CES |
|---|---|---|
| Legs | **Unilateral** | Bilateral |
| Saddle | Unilateral S1–S5 | Bilateral |
| Motor | Often absent | Present |
| Sphincter | **Retention / incontinence present** | Present |
| Cause | **Disc herniation (same — EXCEPT answer, TO)** | Disc herniation |

**Neurology rather than spine referral** — non-compressive cause (e.g. MS / demyelination)\
✗ Wrong options: burst fracture · large disc · sacral metastasis · epidural lymphoma

## How it's asked

**"Young man, unilateral radiculopathy + saddle anaesthesia — diagnosis + signs?"** → **CES, urinary retention, absent ankle jerk** (TO)

**"Order of findings in CES, commonest → least?"** → **leg pain > urinary retention > urinary incontinence**

**"Back pain + perianal numbness, bilateral weakness — next?"** → **immediate MRI lumbar spine**\
✗ Wrong options: CT myelogram · labs · X-ray · discharge

**"Cough → back pain, retention, faecal incontinence, saddle paraesthesia?"** → **immediate MRI**

**"Commonest presenting problem?"** → **back + leg pain**

**"Bladder + bowel loss, saddle numbness — management?"** → **immediate MRI + acute surgery**

**"Decompression window for bladder + motor recovery?"** → **< 48 hours**

**"After decompression, least likely to improve?"** → **bladder dysfunction**

**"Prognosis after urgent surgery with dense perineal anaesthesia?"** → **pain + sensation improve, bladder may remain**

**"12 h of retention + buttock numbness — true?"** → **saddle anaesthesia + bladder dysfunction → poor prognosis**\
✗ Wrong options: CT myelogram · ESI · steroids

**"CES treatment — true?"** → **long-term urinary dysfunction common**\
✗ Wrong options: < 24 h better than < 48 h · laminectomy better · sexual dysfunction commonest · burst fracture commonest

**"L4-5 HNP occluding sac — true?"** → **urinary function can have a prolonged recovery**\
✗ Wrong options: PVR > 200 mL excludes · elderly recover better

**"Not true about CES?"** → **positive Babinski** (TO)

**"Diabetic, saddle anaesthesia, bladder/bowel, absent BCR?"** → **conus medullaris** (TO)

**"Hemi-CES differs from CES EXCEPT?"** → **cause is mostly herniated disc** (TO)

**"Saddle anaesthesia only — lesion level?"** → **below S2** (TO)

**"Pacemaker + conus-cauda picture — pre-op study?"** → **myelo-CT** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cerebral Palsy - Spinal Disorders

*Spine · 10 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Scoliosis in CP** — overall **20%**; ↑ with severity · **spastic quadriplegia** highest (esp. non-sitters) · **bedridden → \~100%** · rare in ambulators

**Spondylolisthesis** — **4–21%** in **spastic diplegia** → check lateral film

### Pathophysiology

**Cause** — muscle weakness + truncal imbalance (little evidence) · **pelvic obliquity** drives deformity

| CP scoliosis vs idiopathic | |
|---|---|
| Progression | **More likely**; **1–2° per month** from age 8–10 |
| Onset | Earlier |
| Shape | **Long, stiff C-shaped**; left curves common |
| Sagittal | Greater kyphosis / lordosis |
| Pelvis | **Pelvic obliquity** |
| Maturity | Delayed |
| Bracing | **Less effective** |
| Fusion | Longer, often **to pelvis** |
| Host | Medically fragile → multidisciplinary |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Weinstein classification

| Group | Curve | Pelvic obliquity |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Double** thoracic + lumbar | Minimal |
| **II** | Large **lumbar / thoracolumbar** | **Marked** |

### Key radiology findings

**X-rays** — same technique over time; erect films when possible · AP + lateral (rib deformity, wedging, rotation, spondylolisthesis) · **bending films**: push-pull / fulcrum if uncooperative

**MRI** — **not routine** pre-op; only for **rapid progression** or **neuro change**

**Exam** — **hamstring contracture → ↓ lumbar lordosis** · **hip contracture → ↑ lordosis** · curve flexibility, balance, pelvic obliquity

## 5 Management

**Non-op** — **non-progressive curves < 50°** or age **< 10** (delay surgery) · custom seat (sitting only) · **TLSO improves sitting balance**, doesn't change natural history (may slow progression only if immature) · **Botox** short-term (2–3 months)

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **PSF ± pelvis** | **Group I, 50–90°**, ambulators, progressive / interfering with sitting; **age > 10**; adequate hip ROM; stable nutrition |
| **PSF ± ASF ± pelvis** | **Group I > 90°**, **non-ambulators**, **Group II**; immature (**crankshaft**); anterior release for **> 100–120°** rigid curves (don't bend < 60°) |
| Growing rods | Young child |
| Pre-op traction | Severe rigid curves |

**Surgery threshold (OB)** — progressive **> 40–50°** interfering with sitting in **> 10 yrs**

### Fusion levels

**Proximal** — **T1 or T2** (avoid proximal junctional kyphosis)

**Distal** — L4/L5 for long curves · **pelvis if obliquity > 15°** (else loss of correction + pseudarthrosis)

```
CP scoliosis needing fusion
├─ Pelvic obliquity > 15° / non-sitter → POSTERIOR T2 → PELVIS
│      (do not fuse to L4 — late loss of correction)
└─ Immature or > 100–120° rigid → ADD ANTERIOR RELEASE (same day)
```

### Fixation techniques

| Technique | Note |
|---|---|
| **Luque rod + sublaminar wires** | Classic |
| **Unit rod + sublaminar wires** | Pelvic limb may penetrate pelvis |
| **Pedicle screws** | Better correction, may avoid anterior surgery |
| **Galveston** | Rod bent from **S1 lamina → PSIS → between iliac tables** just above **sciatic notch** |
| Others | Bilateral sacral screws, iliosacral screws, spinopelvic transiliac, **Dunn-McCarthy** (S-rod over sacral ala) |

![Unit rod with Galveston pelvic fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2057-jaaos-unit-rod-and-galves-moved.jpg)

**A/P same day** — ↓ complications vs staged (nutrition, blood loss, stay) · post-op brace usually unnecessary

### Pre-operative assessment

| Domain | Action |
|---|---|
| **Nutrition** | Weight < 5th centile → ↑ infection, intubation, stay; **albumin > 3.5 g/dL**; consider **G-tube** |
| Respiratory | History, exam, CXR (formal PFTs hard) |
| GI | Treat **GERD** (aspiration) |
| Seizures | Control; **valproic acid → check bleeding time** |

**Goal** — painless solid fusion, balanced spine, **level pelvis**; family goals + risk-benefit

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Pulmonary** | **Insufficiency = commonest**; aspiration, pneumonia |
| Implant failure | Often asymptomatic |
| **Wound infection** | **3–5%**, > idiopathic; local debridement usually enough |
| GI | GERD, malnutrition |
| Neuro | Seizures |
| **Death** | **0–7%** |

**Natural history** — larger curves progress more; pelvic obliquity; decubitus (conflicting)

## X vs Y

| | Weinstein I | Weinstein II |
|---|---|---|
| Curve | Double | Large lumbar/TL |
| Obliquity | Minimal | Marked |
| Fusion | PSF (50–90°, ambulator) | PSF ± ASF to pelvis |

## How it's asked

**"GMFCS V, 75° C-curve with pelvic obliquity, mature?"** → **posterior fusion T2 to pelvis**\
✗ Wrong options: brace · anterior-only · A/P · T2–L4

**"When add anterior release in CP?"** → **> 100–120° rigid curves** or **immature (crankshaft)**

**"Extend fusion to pelvis when?"** → **pelvic obliquity > 15°**

**"Commonest post-op complication?"** → **pulmonary insufficiency**

**"Pre-op lab for patient on valproate?"** → **bleeding time**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Adjacent Segment Disease

*Spine · 11 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

**Adjacent segment degeneration** — **radiographic** degeneration next to a fusion, ± symptoms

**Adjacent segment disease (ASD)** — **clinical** radiculopathy / myelopathy from the adjacent motion segment

**Adjacent level ossification development (ALOD)** — large anterior marginal osteophyte / ALL heterotopic bone next to a plated level; disc height preserved, no herniation; clinical significance unclear

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — clinically relevant ASD **1.6–2.4% / yr** (reoperation \~2.4%/yr) · **25.6% at 10 yrs** after ACDF

**Levels** — C4-5, **C5-6 (highest)**, C6-7 · **C2-3 lowest**

| Risk factor for ASD | |
|---|---|
| **Smoking** | **Strongest** |
| **Female** | Yes (✗ Wrong options: male) |
| **Age < 60** | Yes |
| **< 3 levels fused** | Yes (more remaining motion segments) |
| **Fusion ending next to C5-6 / C6-7** | Most mobile levels |
| Pre-op dural indentation / disc protrusion at C5-7 | Yes |
| Fusion vs non-fusion (foraminotomy, discectomy) | **No difference** |

### Pathophysiology

**ASD** — ↑ compensatory motion → ↑ intradiscal pressure, stress, shear → herniation, height loss, osteophytes → stenosis · also ALL / longus colli damage · **needle marker in wrong disc**

**Counter-evidence** — **CDA (motion-preserving) does not reduce ASD** (Orthobullets ASD note) — partly natural history of spondylosis

**ALOD** — **anterior plate within 5 mm of adjacent disc**; variable-angle screws allow subsidence toward the disc\
✗ Wrong options: diabetes · static screws (protective) · male · age > 60

### Park classification of adjacent level ossification

| Grade | Ossification |
|---|---|
| 0 | None |
| 1 | < 50% of disc space |
| 2 | > 50% of disc space |
| **3** | **Complete bridging** |

![Grade 3 adjacent-level ossification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322194-lateral-radiograph-of-the-cervical-spine-with-grade-3-adjace.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Radiculopathy** — dermatomal pain / weakness at adjacent root

**Myelopathy** — gait instability, hand clumsiness, urinary change

**Exam** — inspect wound · painful ROM arc · rule out **cubital / carpal tunnel** for sensory change · **Hoffmann** (myelopathy) · **Spurling** (radiculopathy) · **shoulder abduction relief** (radiculopathy) · **Lhermitte** (myelopathy)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| X-ray AP, lateral, **flexion-extension** | Disc narrowing, foraminal stenosis, osteophytes, facet arthropathy; **poor correlation with symptoms** |
| **CT** | **Pseudarthrosis**, OPLL |
| **MRI** | Foraminal / central stenosis at adjacent level |

**Differential (top 5)** — **pseudarthrosis** · progressive DDD · **indolent infection** · myelopathy / radiculopathy at a non-adjacent level

**Prevalence context** — radiographic degeneration in **> 80%** of asymptomatic unfused people > 60 · in **25–91%** of fused patients

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic ASD
├─ Radiculopathy / mild myelopathy → NSAIDs, gabapentin, PT, activity change ± brief collar
│      (avoid narcotics; NSAID caution in elderly)
└─ Failed non-op radiculopathy / any significant myelopathy → EXTEND FUSION
       └─ Single adjacent level → CERVICAL DISC REPLACEMENT (≈ ACDF)
```

**Extension of fusion** — approach by pathology location · **re-operate from the prior side** (assume ipsilateral RLN palsy unless ENT scope is normal → protects contralateral nerve) · include C5-6/C6-7 · fusing **> 3 levels** ↓ further ASD · plate **> 5 mm** from adjacent disc

**CDR for ASD** — single level; **equivalent to ACDF**; ↓ adjacent ossification vs plating; align centre of rotation · as index procedure **no clear ASD protection** (meta-analyses: possibly ↓ reoperation)

## 6 Complications

**Progression** — new adjacent segment degenerates after extension → further revision

**Reoperation** — overall **6.1–25.6%**; **0.7–3.7%/yr**

## X vs Y

| | Adjacent segment degeneration | Adjacent segment disease | ALOD |
|---|---|---|---|
| Definition | Radiographic | Clinical symptoms | Anterior ossification |
| Cause | Motion / natural history | Same | **Plate < 5 mm from disc** |
| Rx | Observe | Non-op → extend fusion / CDR | Observe |

## How it's asked

**"Asymptomatic anterior ossification 2 yrs after ACDF — associated factor?"** → **plate placed within 5 mm of adjacent disc**\
✗ Wrong options: diabetes · static screws · male · age > 60

**"Strongest risk factor for cervical ASD?"** → **smoking**

**"Level with highest ASD risk?"** → **C5-6**

**"Revision anterior approach — which side?"** → **same side as prior surgery** (unless ENT shows normal cords)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Disc Arthroplasty

*Spine · 12 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### History

**1966 Fernström** — stainless-steel **ball bearing**; device complications → abandoned as ACDF rose

**2002** — modern CDA; premise = maintain motion → ↓ adjacent segment disease

**2010 →** — RCTs: **superior to fusion for reoperation rates + quicker return to work**

### Benefits & risks

| Benefit | Risk |
|---|---|
| **Preserves motion** | **Hardware failure** → retropulsion into canal, **paralysis** |
| Pseudarthrosis not a concern | **Persistent facet-origin neck pain** |
| Quicker return to activities | — |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| X-ray AP + lateral | Alignment, disc height |
| **CT** | Implant **positioning + sizing**; facet arthrosis |
| **MRI** | Central + foraminal stenosis |

## 5 Management

### Indications & contraindications

| Indications | Contraindication |
|---|---|
| **1- and 2-level radiculopathy** | **Significant facet degeneration (advanced facet arthrosis)** → persistent axial neck pain → fuse instead |
| **1- and 2-level myelopathy** (thorough decompression) | — |
| Revision CDA **within 2 weeks** = primary results | — |

**Not contraindications** — two-level disease · **loss of disc height** · neutral lordosis/kyphosis · bilateral Hoffmann (myelopathy)

### Evidence

| | vs ACDF |
|---|---|
| Neurologic improvement | **Equivalent** (1 + 2 level) |
| PROMs | **Equivalent** |
| **Reoperation** | **Superior (lower)**; CDA reoperations mostly **index-level, implant-related** |
| Adjacent segment disease | "Mounting evidence of ↓" (Orthobullets CDA note) · "no protective effect, possibly ↓ reoperation" (Orthobullets ASD note) — read the options |

**Single-level myelopathy, no fixed kyphosis (smoker, inverted brachioradialis)** — **CDA at C5-6** gives best function\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs/PT · laminoplasty C4-7 · laminectomy-fusion C4-7 (multilevel, ↑ NDI) · C6 corpectomy

### Technique

**Anterior approach** · **align centre of rotation in coronal + sagittal planes** (critical in 2-level)

![Single-level cervical disc arthroplasty (lateral)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12279-single-level-lateral.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Hardware failure** — catastrophic retropulsion

**Facet pain** — if arthritic facets ignored

## X vs Y

| | CDA | ACDF |
|---|---|---|
| Motion | Preserved | Lost |
| Pseudarthrosis | N/A | Possible |
| Reoperation | **Lower** | Higher |
| Facet arthrosis | contraindicated | ✓ |
| Levels | 1–2 | Any |

## How it's asked

**"51-yr single-level myelopathy, no kyphosis — best functional outcome?"** → **C5-6 disc arthroplasty**

**"C7 radiculopathy — contraindication to CDA?"** → **advanced facet arthrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: two-level · disc height loss · neutral alignment · Hoffmann

**"Where is CDA superior to ACDF?"** → **reoperation rate**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Facet Dislocations & Fractures

*Spine · 13 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Young** — high-energy (MVC, motorcycle, contact sport) · **elderly** — low-energy

**C7 / C7-T1** — **17%** of injuries → always visualise cervicothoracic junction

**Associated** — head injury, **non-contiguous spine injuries** (TL, CT, OC junctions), limb injuries

### Pathoanatomy

| Injury | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Facet fracture** | **Superior facet** more often; ↓ threshold for dislocation (loses interlock); may be a **spontaneously reduced dislocation** |
| **Unilateral facet dislocation** | **Most frequently missed** C-spine injury on X-ray · **\~25%** subluxation · **monoradiculopathy** that improves with traction (inferior facet of cephalad vertebra in foramen) · rotation component |
| **Bilateral facet dislocation** | **\~50%** subluxation · **SCI in \~80%** |

**Mechanism** — **flexion-distraction** ± rotation → posterior structures torn (ISL, capsule, LF, posterior annulus), **ALL usually preserved**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion-compression · vertical compression · extension

**PLL / posterior annulus disruption** — **40%** unilateral · **80%** bilateral

## 2 Mechanism

### Allen & Ferguson classification (research)

| Group | |
|---|---|
| 1 Flexion-compression | — |
| 2 Vertical compression | — |
| **3 Flexion-distraction** | **Stage 1** facet sprain, < 10° kyphosis · **Stage 2 unilateral dislocation** · **Stage 3 bilateral, 50% (perched)** · **Stage 4 complete (100%)** |
| 4 Extension-compression | — |
| 5 Extension-distraction | — |
| 6 Lateral flexion | — |

**Clinical (descriptive)** — compression, burst, flexion-distraction, unilateral/bilateral dislocation, facet fracture

## 3 Presentation

**Unilateral** — single-arm paraesthesia: **C5-6 → C6** (wrist extension weakness, thumb numbness) · **C6-7 → C7** (triceps + wrist flexion weakness, index + middle finger numbness) · angular deformity

**Bilateral** — bilateral UL + LL weakness, sensory change → **ASIA** documentation

**History** — AS / DISH, prior fusion · scalp lacerations → head injury

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Findings / indication |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP, lateral, oblique, open mouth) | Subluxation (25% unilateral, 50% bilateral), disc height loss (retropulsed disc), widened interspinous distance, focal hypolordosis, soft-tissue swelling |
| **Flexion-extension** | **Whenever a facet fracture is seen** (spontaneous reduction / occult instability) |
| **CT** | Most cases; subtle facet subluxation, facet/pedicle/lamina fractures |
| **MRI** | **Before any open reduction / surgery**; **obtunded** patient (emergent); **failed closed reduction**; **neuro worsening during reduction** — looks for **disc herniation**, PLC injury, cord compression; **cord haematoma = poor motor prognosis** |

**MRI timing (TO)** — best sensitivity for haemorrhage/oedema **within 48 h**

![Bilateral facet dislocation: 50% subluxation on lateral X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2064-lat-xray-bilateral-facet-dislocation-c4-5-moved.jpg)

**Differential** — **lateral mass fracture-separation** → needs **2-level** fusion (dislocation = 1 level)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria – facet fractures

| Stable (non-op 6–12 wks, collar / halo) | Unstable (single-level instrumented fusion) |
|---|---|
| Reduced unilateral fracture, **no instability on flex-ex** | **Bilateral** facet fractures |
| **< 40% of lateral mass** or **< 1 cm** height | **> 40% of lateral mass** or **> 1 cm** height |

**Non-op risk** — **> 30% re-subluxation / redislocation**, persistent pain

**Halo** — suboptimal in lower C-spine; **most likely to fail with facet fracture-dislocation** · hard orthosis often satisfactory\
✗ Wrong options: C1 lateral mass · C2 pars · burst

### Closed reduction vs MRI first

```
Facet dislocation
├─ Awake, alert, cooperative → EMERGENT CLOSED REDUCTION (Gardner-Wells)
│      → MRI after reduction → URGENT STABILISATION (< 24 h)
├─ Obtunded / intoxicated / intubated / GCS ↓ → EMERGENT MRI FIRST
│      → OPEN REDUCTION + STABILISATION
└─ Failed closed reduction or neuro worsening → STOP → MRI → OPEN REDUCTION
```

**Awake patient — source conflict** — **immediate closed traction** (Orthobullets, OB — especially with deteriorating neuro, ASIA E → D) · **hard collar + MRI first** (TO answer for awake, intact patient 30 min post-injury) — read the options

**Pre-reduction MRI** — not routine in awake patients (delays reduction, early reduction ↑ recovery)

**MRI finding that changes plan** — **herniated disc at dislocated level** → **anterior discectomy before/with reduction**\
✗ Wrong options: cord oedema · PLC disruption · lamina fracture · ALL signal

| Reduction fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Unilateral | **Harder** to reduce, **more stable** after; closed success \~25% |
| Bilateral | **Easier** (PLL torn), **less stable** after |
| Fail closed reduction | **26%** |
| Never | Closed reduction with **altered mental status** |

### Closed reduction technique

**Gardner-Wells tongs** — **1 cm above pinna**, in line with **EAM**, **below skull equator** · **posterior pin placement** → flexion moment

**Weights** — 5–10 lb increments · up to **140 lb or 70% body weight** · \~**9.4–9.8 lb per level above injury** · carbon-fibre tongs for MRI (\~80 lb limit)

**After reduction** — drop to **10–15 lb**, add **extension** (pulley / thoracic pad)

**Abort** — neuro worsening (→ immediate MRI) · **overdistraction > 1.5× adjacent disc space**

**Requirements** — sedation, respiratory supervision, serial cross-table laterals + neuro exams

### Surgical options

| Procedure | Indication / technique |
|---|---|
| **ACDF (single level, plate)** | Disc herniation compressing cord after reduction; anterior open reduction of **unilateral** dislocation — Caspar pin distraction + **rotate cephalad pin toward dislocated side** |
| Anterior reduction of bilateral | Converging Caspar pins (10–20°) compressed to unlock; curette pushes rostral body back · **lamina spreaders not effective for bilateral** |
| **Posterior reduction + lateral mass screws** | No disc herniation; irreducible from front/closed; Penfield 4 lever, remove tip of caudal superior facet; often **2 levels** (poor purchase) |
| **Anterior → posterior** | Disc needs removal but can't reduce anteriorly: discectomy, plate fixed to **superior body only** (stops graft kick-out, allows rotation) |

**Biomechanics** — posterior fixation > anterior plating for flexion-distraction (Sutterlin, Coe) · anterior overdistraction risks cord + hardware

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| SSI | ↑ posterior approach |
| **Redislocation** | Unilateral treated in orthosis → ACDF |
| Respiratory | ARDS, pneumonia, tracheostomy |
| **Vertebral artery** | Up to **11%**; ↑ with lateral mass / transverse process involvement; often unrecognised |
| Oesophageal | Anterior reduction → primary repair |
| Pin-tract | Halo; meningitis if inner table breached |

**Prognosis** — worse with severe deficit, older age, **cord haematoma** · **bilateral dislocation = worst outcome** among subaxial patterns

## X vs Y

| | Unilateral | Bilateral |
|---|---|---|
| Subluxation | **25%** | **50%** |
| Neuro | **Radiculopathy** | **SCI \~80%** |
| PLL torn | 40% | 80% |
| Reduce | Harder, stable after | Easier, unstable after |
| X-ray | Most missed | Obvious |

## How it's asked

**"Characteristic mechanism of facet dislocation?"** → **flexion-distraction**

**"Right C6-7 facet dislocation — which CT image?"** → **unilateral right perched/jumped facet**

**"Awake, alert, 30 min post-injury, C4-5 jumped facets — next (TO)?"** → **hard collar + MRI** (TO) · OB awake-patient stems → **immediate closed reduction** — read the options

**"Awake, cooperative, ASIA E → D after CT?"** → **immediate closed reduction with traction**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI first · steroids

**"GCS 3, intubated, bilateral C5-6 dislocation?"** → **MRI**

**"Intubated, moving limbs, bilateral C6-7?"** → **cervical MRI then reduction**

**"Intoxicated, obtunded, bilateral C4-5?"** → **immediate MRI then surgery**

**"MRI finding mandating surgery instead of closed reduction?"** → **herniated disc at dislocated level**

**"GCS 10, C7-T1 fracture-dislocation?"** → **immediate MRI**

**"Altered mental status — MRI finding that changes approach?"** → **herniated disc (→ anterior)**

**"Nondisplaced C6 facet fracture, stable on flex-ex?"** → **hard collar**

**"Halo most likely to fail?"** → **C6-7 facet fracture-dislocation**

**"Worst outcome with incomplete SCI?"** → **bilateral facet dislocation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Lateral Mass Fracture Separation

*Spine · 14 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Demographics** — **M:F 2:1**, mean age **35** (20–70)

**Level** — **C6 > C5 > C7 > C4 > C3**

### Pathoanatomy

**Lesion** — fracture lines through **lamina + pedicle** → lateral mass (with its superior + inferior facets) floats free

**Two-level instability** — both the facet joint above and below are compromised → **fuse 2 levels** (vs facet dislocation = 1 level)

| Associated finding | Rate |
|---|---|
| Anterior listhesis of fractured vertebra | **77%** |
| Listhesis of vertebra above / below | 24% / 10% |
| Coronal translation | 33% |
| Body collapse | 33% (less in type A) |

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — traffic accident, fall, heavy object on head → **hyperextension + lateral compression + rotation**

**Allen-Ferguson groups** — extension-compression · **lateral flexion → type B comminution** · flexion-distraction

## 3 Presentation

**Neuro symptoms** — common (**up to 66%**): radiculopathy, SCI / myelopathy (Frankel / ASIA)

**Exam** — **torticollis**, paravertebral spasm

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP, lateral, oblique) | **Low sensitivity — 38%**; disc narrowing; instability = **> 3.5 mm** displacement, **> 10° kyphosis**, **> 10° rotation** vs adjacent level |
| **CT** | **Diagnosis**; fracture extends into adjacent facets, foramen transversarium, transverse process, pedicle, lamina; translation, uncovertebral subluxation |
| **MRI** | **ALL rupture 50–75%** · PLL 30–35% · ISL/SSL 10–75% · disc disruption, bone bruise |

### Kotani classification

| Type | Description | Same-level / adjacent translation |
|---|---|---|
| **A – Separation** | **2 fracture lines: unilateral lamina + pedicle** | 91% / 20% |
| **B – Comminution** | Multiple lines, lateral wedging (lateral flexion) | — / 50% |
| **C – Split** | Vertical coronal line; superior articular process of caudal vertebra invaginates | 80% / 0% |
| **D – Traumatic spondylolysis** | Bilateral horizontal pars fractures → anterior/posterior elements separate | 100% / 50% |

![Kotani classification of lateral mass fractures](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2000-kotani.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Non-op (Miami J / halo)** — stable, no deficit; **halo poorly controls hyperextension/rotation**; spontaneous fusion only **20%**; late instability + pain → surveillance

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Posterior decompression + 2-level instrumented fusion** | **Most cases** (injured structures posterior); lateral mass / pedicle screws or lateral mass plating; late instability after non-op |
| **Two-level ACDF** | **Minimally displaced**, no posterior reduction needed; **controls anterior collapse + rotation** |
| **Single pedicle screw** | **Type A** separation **without instability** |
| A + P | Extra anterior support; failed anterior reduction / late presentation |

**Posterior caveats** — anterior disc collapse + late kyphosis; **midline (wiring) fusion doesn't control rotation**

```
C5 lateral mass fracture-separation + ASIA C
├─ Posterior → INSTRUMENTED FUSION C4-6 (both levels)
│      (not C5-6 only or C5 corpectomy — body intact)
└─ Anterior alternative → 2-LEVEL ACDF C4-6
```

### Lateral mass screw safety

**Technique (TO)** — start **1 mm medial to pillar midpoint**, aim **30° lateral + 15° cephalad**

**Structure most at risk (TO)** — **nerve root** (exits anterolateral to facet; long screw)\
✗ Wrong options: vertebral artery (too medial) · cord (safe) · thoracic duct

## 6 Complications

**Vertebral artery injury** (pedicle screws) · **late kyphosis** · **late instability** · chronic neck pain · radiculopathy

## X vs Y

| | Lateral mass fracture-separation | Facet dislocation |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | **Hyperextension + lateral compression + rotation** | **Flexion-distraction** |
| Unstable levels | **2** | 1 |
| Fusion | **2 levels** (above + below) | 1 level |
| X-ray detection | 38% | Unilateral often missed |

## How it's asked

**"ASIA C, C5 lateral mass fracture-separation — management?"** → **posterior instrumented fusion C4-6**\
✗ Wrong options: C5-6 · corpectomy · 1-level ACDF

**"Structure most at risk with lateral mass screw?"** → **nerve root** (TO)

**"Why fuse two levels?"** → **superior + inferior facets of the floating mass both incompetent**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Myelopathy

*Spine · 15 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Aetiology

**Commonest cause** — **degenerative cervical spondylotic myelopathy (CSM)**: osteophytes, disc-osteophyte complex, degenerative spondylolisthesis, **LF hypertrophy**

**Others** — **congenital stenosis** (symptoms when spondylosis adds on) · **OPLL** · tumour · epidural abscess · trauma · kyphosis

**Cord injury mechanism** — direct compression + **ischaemia (anterior spinal artery compression)**

**Tandem stenosis (cervical + lumbar)** — **\~20%** (Orthobullets) · **5–25%** (OB) → myelopathy **takes precedence**

### Epidemiology & canal diameter

**Commonest level of spondylosis (TO)** — **C5-6**, then C6-7 (most subaxial motion)\
✗ Wrong options: C3-4 · C1-2

| Canal (AP) | Value (TO) |
|---|---|
| Normal | \~17 mm (C1 23, C2 20, C3–6 17, C7 15) |
| **Relative stenosis** | **10–13 mm** |
| **Absolute stenosis** | **< 10 mm** (area < 100 mm²) |
| Cord compression | Canal **< 13 mm** (Orthobullets) |
| **Pavlov (Torg) ratio** | **< 0.8** → congenitally narrow canal |

**X-ray degeneration** — **70% by age 70** · MRI false positives: **28%** of > 40 yrs have HNP / foraminal stenosis

### Natural history

**Pattern** — **slow, stepwise deterioration with plateaus** · rarely improves non-op\
✗ Wrong options: stable · rapid continuous decline · improves with steroids/rest

**Worse non-op outcome** — segmental kyphosis, circumferential compression

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — neck pain/stiffness (often absent; **\~15%** of severe cases painless), occipital headache · **diffuse non-dermatomal** bilateral paraesthesia · **hand clumsiness** (buttons, handwriting, dropping things) · **gait instability** (most important clinical predictor; stairs) · urinary retention (**rare, late**)

**Severe CSM** — proximal LL muscles weaker than distal (opposite in lumbar stenosis)

### Clinical diagnosis – UMN signs

| Sign | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Gait ataxia** | **Commonest physical finding (91%)** (OB) |
| **Hoffmann** | Flick middle finger DIP → thumb/index flexion; "most common exam finding" (Orthobullets) · **80%** (OB) / 59% in another series; ↑ with severity |
| Hyperreflexia | LL 81%, UL 67%; **absent if concomitant peripheral neuropathy / root compression / diabetes** |
| **Inverted radial reflex** | Tap distal brachioradialis → finger flexion |
| **Sustained clonus (> 3 beats)** | **Sensitivity \~13%, specificity \~100%** |
| **Babinski** | **Least common** (≤ 1/3) |
| **Finger escape (Wartenberg)** | Small finger abducts/flexes (intrinsic weakness) |
| **Grip-and-release** | Normal **20 in 10 s** |
| **Lhermitte** | Neck **flexion → electric shock** down spine/limbs; specific, insensitive; also MS |
| Romberg / tandem gait | Posterior column dysfunction |
| Proprioception loss | Advanced, **poor prognosis** |
| Pinprick ↓ | Lateral spinothalamic tract |

**UMN vs LMN** — **fasciculations, atrophy, tongue fasciculations, areflexia = LMN** (EXCEPT answers) · weakness = both

**Myelopathy hand** — intrinsic/webspace atrophy, finger escape, **dysdiadochokinesia**, Hoffmann \
✗ Wrong options: **intrinsic plus posture**

**Other tests** — Spurling (radiculopathy) · Jackson (hyperextension) · Lasègue (lumbar SLR)

![Finger escape sign](../../MCQ/attachments/q216655-illa-illus-a-finger-escape.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Findings |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP, lateral, oblique, flex-ext) | Uncovertebral + facet degeneration, osteophytes, disc narrowing; **Pavlov < 0.8**; **C2-C7 alignment** + **local kyphosis angle**; obliques → foraminal stenosis; flex-ext → instability / compensatory subluxation |
| **MRI** | **Study of choice**; CSF effacement = functional stenosis; **T2 bright = myelomalacia**; **T1 signal change → poorer prognosis**; **compression ratio < 0.4 → poor prognosis** |
| CT | OPLL, osteophytes |
| **CT myelogram** | Pacemaker / hardware artefact (C1-2 or lumbar puncture) |
| NCS | High false negatives; separates peripheral vs central (ALS) |

**Compression ratio** — smallest **AP** cord diameter ÷ largest **transverse** diameter

![Compression ratio of the cord](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2031-compression-ratio.jpg)

**Prognostic MRI** — **focal** T2 signal better than **multisegmental** T2 signal · isolated **low T1** = gliosis/cavitation, little improvement · cord shape boomerang → teardrop → **triangular** (worst)

**Non-op success predictor** — **transverse cord area > 70 mm²**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ CMCT · low T1 signal · canal < 13 mm · younger age

**Differential** — normal ageing, stroke, movement disorders, **B12 deficiency**, **ALS**, **MS**

### Tandem stenosis

**Lumbar claudication + UMN signs (hyperreflexia, clonus, broad gait, clumsy hands)** — **MRI cervical (± thoracic) spine before lumbar surgery** · pacemaker → **CT myelogram of C-spine** · gait problem with 5/5 strength + mild lumbar stenosis → **MRI thoracic + cervical** (brain MRI second-line)\
✗ Wrong options: lumbar decompression ± fusion · ESI · PT · discogram · ABI

### Classifications

| Nurick | |
|---|---|
| 0 | Root symptoms only |
| 1 | Cord signs, **normal gait** |
| 2 | Gait difficulty, **fully employed** |
| 3 | Gait prevents employment, walks unassisted |
| **4** | **Walks with assistance** |
| 5 | Chair / bedbound |

| Ranawat | |
|---|---|
| I | Pain, no deficit |
| II | Subjective weakness, hyperreflexia, dysaesthesia |
| IIIA | Objective weakness + long-tract signs, **ambulatory** |
| IIIB | Same, **non-ambulatory** |

**JOA / mJOA (17 points)** — UL motor, LL motor, sensory, bladder · **severe = mJOA < 12** · excellent outcome ≥ 16

## 5 Management

**Non-op** — mild disease **without functional impairment** or poor surgical candidate (function > exam findings) · NSAIDs, gabapentin, collar in slight flexion, PT · no traction/chiropractic · **cord compression without myelopathy signs → PT**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery

**Surgery** — significant functional impairment / progression; **mild myelopathy by mJOA** also indicated; **operate as soon as medically stable**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for Nurick IV, multisegmental T2 signal, intolerable pain, incontinence

### Goals & outcomes of surgery

**Goal** — **halt stepwise neurologic decline**\
✗ Wrong options: complete recovery · mJOA > 16 · resolve myelomalacia · restore normal lordosis (aim neutral)

**Expected** — **significant mJOA improvement at 1 yr even in severe (mJOA 10) CSM**; gait / sensory improvement variable

### Approach selection

```
Cervical myelopathy surgery
├─ 1–2 levels, anterior pathology → ACDF (± corpectomy)
├─ ≥ 3 levels, lordotic / neutral
│      ├─ No axial neck pain, want motion → LAMINOPLASTY
│      └─ Axial neck pain / instability → LAMINECTOMY + FUSION
└─ Fixed kyphosis > 10–13°
       ├─ ≤ 2 levels → ANTERIOR (ACDF / corpectomy)
       └─ ≥ 3 levels → ANTERIOR + POSTERIOR
```

### Operative criteria – alignment thresholds

| Threshold | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Fixed kyphosis > 10°** | Anterior procedure (corrects kyphosis); posterior alone not enough |
| **Local kyphosis > 13°** | Contraindication to posterior decompression / **strongest predictor of poor laminoplasty outcome** (✗ Wrong options: multilevel stenosis · osteoporosis · CSF effacement) |
| **Kyphosis > 12° + > 2 levels** | **Combined A/P** (OB) |
| **≥ 3-level corpectomy** | Anterior alone → **> 70% catastrophic failure** → add posterior |

**Bowstringing** — in kyphosis cord drapes over anterior osteophytes; posterior decompression can't let it drift back

### Anterior decompression – ACDF/corpectomy

**ACDF** — **mainstay for 1–2 levels**, anterior pathology (soft disc, disc-osteophyte, focal OPLL), fixed kyphosis > 10° · single-level CSM gold standard (TO) · Smith-Robinson approach · avoid with poor swallowing

**Anterior vs posterior** — anterior: **↓ infection, ↓ blood loss, ↓ post-op pain** · no difference in C5 palsy, C7 radiculopathy, overall complications

**Corpectomy** — retrovertebral disease / kyphosis · 2-level corpectomies vulnerable (prefer **1 corpectomy + adjacent discectomy**) · **graft dislodgement 7–20%** (can be fatal) · **2-level → add posterior fixation**; **3-level → A + P mandatory**

**3-level corpectomy, anterior plate failure** — prevented by **combined anterior + posterior fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: discectomies (can't decompress OPLL) · mesh cage · longer collar/halo · screw mix

### Cervical disc arthroplasty

**Ideal (TO)** — skeletally mature, 20–70 yrs, **1–2 levels C3–C7**, soft disc, minimal spondylosis, preserved facets, disc height ≥ 3 mm, ≥ 6 wks failed non-op · single-level myelopathy without kyphosis → CDA gives best function (OB)

**Contraindications (TO)** — **≥ 3 levels** · **instability (> 3 mm translation / > 11° rotation)** · **facet degeneration** · axial neck pain alone · severe spondylosis (bridging, > 50% height loss, < 2° motion) · osteoporosis / metabolic bone disease · adjacent fusion / prior surgery at level · **morbid obesity (BMI > 40)** · **RA / spondyloarthropathy** · infection, malignancy, steroids, pregnancy · kyphotic deformity \
✗ Wrong options: **2-level disease** (NOT a contraindication)

**Complication** — hardware failure → retropulsion

### Posterior decompression – laminectomy/fusion vs laminoplasty

| | Laminectomy + fusion | Laminoplasty |
|---|---|---|
| Indication | **Multilevel**, kyphosis < 10°, **axial neck pain** (facet) | **Multilevel**, **preserved lordosis**, **congenital stenosis**, **OPLL**, motion desired, pseudarthrosis risk (diabetes, steroids) |
| Contraindication | Fixed kyphosis > 10–13° | **Kyphosis > 13°** (e.g. 15°, 20°); severe axial neck pain (relative) |
| Motion | Lost | **Preserved** (some loss) |
| Wound complications | Higher | **Lower** |
| Cost (2 yr) | **Higher** | Lower |
| Revision | Comparable | Comparable |
| ASD | Higher | Lower |
| C5 palsy | Higher (some series) | Similar / lower |

**Laminoplasty mechanism** — ↑ **AP** canal dimension + cord drifts dorsally · **open door** = unilateral hinge, opening held by bone/anchors/plate · **French door** = bilateral hinges, midline opening · C3–7 · facets not violated · bilateral exposure still needed\
✗ Wrong options: medial-lateral

**Not contraindications to laminoplasty** — OPLL · multilevel stenosis · 30° flex-ext motion · age > 55 · SAC < 8 mm · prior posterior surgery · radiculopathy (add keyhole foraminotomy) · C7 SVA +5 cm · compression ratio 0.3

**Laminoplasty + fusion after ACDF** — preserves **paraspinal muscle attachments / ↓ atrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · neuro · stay benefit

**Laminectomy alone** — rarely; **post-laminectomy kyphosis 11–47%**

**Posterior-alone ideal image** — **multilevel stenosis with preserved lordosis**\
✗ Wrong options: teardrop fracture · kyphotic multilevel → A/P · skip-level lordotic → skip ACDF · anterior abscess

**Central cord syndrome on background CSM + axial neck pain, multilevel** — **posterior decompression + fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: 2-level corpectomy without posterior · lumbar MRI (proximal weaker)

### Combined anterior-posterior

**Indications** — **multilevel stenosis in rigid kyphotic spine** · multilevel corpectomies · **post-laminectomy kyphosis**

**Occipitocervical fusion** — periodontoid pannus (posterior-only resolves pannus; transoral ↑ morbidity > 4 h)

## 6 Complications

### C5 palsy

**Definition** — post-op **deltoid ± biceps** weakness ≥ 1 grade without myelopathy worsening

**Incidence** — **\~4.6%** (Orthobullets) · 5–12% after laminoplasty · 0–30% range

**Timing** — immediate to **weeks** (most within 1 week; up to 20 days); **92% unilateral**; half with C5 pain/sensory change

**Risk factors** — **male** (strongest) · **laminectomy + fusion** (higher in some studies) \
✗ Wrong options: age · myelopathy severity · OPLL · level

**Anterior vs posterior** — **no significant difference** (Orthobullets, OB, TO); laminoplasty lower in some studies

**Mechanism** — **dorsal cord drift tethers C5 root** (C5 at apex of lordosis) vs segmental cord ischaemia/reperfusion · prevention: prophylactic **C4-5 keyhole foraminotomy**, avoid excessive cord shift/lordosis, deltoid/biceps MEP monitoring

**Prognosis** — **good**; observe · \~80% recover by 6–12 months · **prolonged recovery: multilevel paresis, motor grade ≤ 2, sensory involvement + intractable pain**\
✗ Wrong options: revision · extension · steroids

### Anterior approach complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **RLN palsy (hoarseness)** | Commonest nerve injury (\~1%); **prolonged tracheo-oesophageal retraction / ET cuff**; **right = left** in studies; **↑ in revision (up to 9.5%)**; observe → ENT at **6 wks** (Teflon); revision on **same side** as prior palsy · left RLN loops **aortic arch**, right **subclavian** |
| **Dysphagia** | 30–50%; **local retropharyngeal steroid** before closure ↓ it (✗ Wrong options: TXA · incision type) |
| **Airway compromise** | **> 5 h surgery, > 300 mL blood loss, multilevel at/above C3-4 (C2–C4 exposure)** → **keep intubated 24–48 h** |
| **Retropharyngeal haematoma (TO)** | **6–24 h**, tense incision, dyspnoea/dysphagia/dysphonia → **immediate evacuation** |
| Angioedema (TO) | **6–12 h**, face/tongue swelling → IV steroids, antihistamine, adrenaline |
| Pharyngolaryngeal oedema (TO) | **12–72 h**, voice change, worse supine → may need **cricothyrotomy** |
| **Vertebral artery** | **Burr through lateral wall** of body in corpectomy |
| Horner | Lateral retraction on **longus colli** (sympathetic chain) |
| Superior laryngeal nerve | Rare; C4 and above |
| Hypoglossal | Rare; tongue deviation |
| Oesophageal | Rare; trauma, revision, diverticula |

### Posterior approach & general complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Infection** | **Higher posterior** than anterior |
| **Pseudarthrosis** | **12% single, 30% multilevel** (3-level ACDF + plate fusion \~80%) |
| **Post-laminectomy kyphosis** | Loss of posterior tension band + muscle denervation; ↑ with pre-op kyphosis, **> 30–50% facet resection**, **children (\~100%)** → **A/P revision** |
| **Epidural haematoma** | **1/1000**; new weakness → **emergent MRI + evacuation** |
| Hardware failure | 7–20% after 2-level corpectomy → stabilise posteriorly |

| Epidural haematoma age | T1 | T2 |
|---|---|---|
| **Hyperacute (< 24 h)** | **Hypo** | **Hyper** (STIR bright) |
| Acute (1–3 d) | Iso | Hypo |
| Subacute (3 d–2 wk) | Hyper | Hypo |
| Chronic (> 2 wk) | Hypo | Hypo |

### Root-level localisation

| Disc → root | Motor | Sensory | Reflex |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C4-5 → C5** | **Deltoid** (± biceps) | **Lateral arm** | Biceps |
| **C5-6 → C6** | **Biceps (elbow flexion) + wrist extensors** | **Radial forearm, thumb** | **Supinator (brachioradialis)** |
| **C6-7 → C7** | **Triceps + wrist flexors** | Index, middle (ring) | **Triceps** |
| **C7-T1 → C8** | **Finger flexors (FDP)** | **Little finger, ulnar hand/forearm** | — |
| T1 | Finger abduction | Medial forearm | — |

**Cervical rule** — disc compresses the **exiting lower root** (C5-6 → C6)

**Shoulder abduction relief sign** — radiculopathy (C7 here; elbow flexion weakness = C6)

## X vs Y

| | Myelopathy | Radiculopathy |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Cord (UMN) | Root (LMN) |
| Signs | Hoffmann, clonus, broad gait | Dermatomal pain, weak reflex |
| Tests | Lhermitte | Spurling, shoulder abduction relief |

| Early airway problem after ACDF (TO) | Time | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| Angioedema | 6–12 h | Steroids / antihistamine / adrenaline |
| **Retropharyngeal haematoma** | **6–24 h** | **Immediate evacuation** |
| Pharyngolaryngeal oedema | 12–72 h | Reintubation / cricothyrotomy |

## How it's asked

**"UMN sign EXCEPT?"** → **fasciculations**

**"Neck flexion → electric shocks?"** → **Lhermitte sign**

**"Clumsy hands, Hoffmann, finger escape — next?"** → **cervical MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: splint · carpal tunnel injection · EMG

**"Least common finding in myelopathy?"** → **Babinski**

**"Myelopathy hand EXCEPT?"** → **intrinsic plus posturing**

**"Finding supporting UMN condition?"** → **sustained clonus**\
✗ Wrong options: absent reflexes · atrophy · tongue fasciculations

**"Lumbar stenosis + clumsy hands + 3+ patellar reflexes — next?"** → **MRI cervical spine**

**"Lumbar DS + myelopathy, pacemaker?"** → **CT myelogram of cervical spine**

**"Wide-based gait, 5/5 strength, mild lumbar stenosis?"** → **MRI thoracic + cervical spine**

**"Non-op success predictor?"** → **transverse cord area > 70 mm²**

**"Natural history?"** → **stepwise deterioration after stable periods**

**"Cord compression, no myelopathic signs?"** → **physical therapy**

**"Most likely outcome after surgery?"** → **halt stepwise decline**

**"When to operate?"** → **when medically stable**

**"mJOA 10, post C3-7 fusion — expected?"** → **significant mJOA improvement at 1 yr**

**"Goal of surgery in severe CSM?"** → **prevent further decline**

**"Myelomalacia, lordotic, C4-7 — best pairing?"** → **posterior decompression + fusion C4-7 to prevent deterioration**

**"Single-level CSM — best approach?"** → **ACDF** (TO)

**"Myelopathy + > 10° kyphosis?"** → **anterior decompression + fusion**

**"Kyphotic, myelomalacia, broad gait?"** → **multilevel anterior decompression + fusion**

**"C4/5 + C5/6 compression, progressive?"** → **ACDF both levels**

**"Kyphosis + 3-level compression, needs walker?"** → **multilevel anterior strut + plate, then posterior fusion**

**"Anterior vs posterior — anterior has?"** → **lower blood loss**

**"Poor laminoplasty outcome predictor?"** → **local kyphosis > 13°**

**"Laminoplasty contraindicated in which image?"** → **rigid kyphosis > 13°**

**"Posterior-only most appropriate?"** → **multilevel stenosis, preserved lordosis**

**"Laminoplasty — true?"** → **preserves more motion than laminectomy + fusion**

**"Strongest contraindication to open-door laminoplasty?"** → **C2-7 rigid kyphosis 15°**

**"Laminoplasty decompresses by?"** → **↑ AP canal dimension**

**"Relative contraindication to laminoplasty?"** → **kyphosis 20°**\
✗ Wrong options: OPLL · age

**"Contraindication to laminoplasty?"** → **fixed cervical kyphosis**\
✗ Wrong options: SAC < 8 mm · OPLL · prior posterior surgery · radiculopathy

**"Long-standing multilevel spondylotic compression?"** → **multilevel laminectomy + fusion**

**"Contraindication to isolated posterior approach?"** → **rigid kyphotic alignment**

**"Best indication for laminoplasty alone?"** → **multilevel congenital stenosis with lordosis**

**"Laminoplasty vs laminectomy after ACDF — benefit?"** → **preserved muscle attachments**

**"80-yr fall, CSM, axial neck pain, multilevel?"** → **posterior decompression + fusion**

**"Laminoplasty vs laminectomy-fusion — true?"** → **local kyphosis > 13° = poor laminoplasty outcome**

**"3-level corpectomy failed — prevention?"** → **combined anterior + posterior fixation**

**"Fixed kyphosis + multilevel compression?"** → **combined anteroposterior decompression + fusion**

**"Favours combined A/P?"** → **axial pain, kyphosis 17°, 3 levels**

**"Bilateral deltoid weakness day 2 after C5-6 surgery?"** → **C5 palsy** (TO)

**"Commonest neuro complication after laminoplasty?"** → **biceps weakness (C5)**

**"Radiculopathy pattern from cord drift?"** → **motor-dominant, deltoid**

**"Root affected after laminoplasty?"** → **C5**

**"New 3/5 weakness after C4-6 ACDF?"** → **C5, biceps**

**"Deltoid 3/5 two weeks after C2-4 fusion — treatment?"** → **reassurance + observation**

**"C5 palsy risk factor?"** → **male sex**

**"Deficit common to post-ACDF and post-laminectomy palsy?"** → **can't abduct arm against gravity**

**"C5 palsy — true?"** → **no difference anterior vs posterior**

**"C5 palsy prolonged recovery?"** → **motor grade ≤ 2**

**"Shoulder abduction weakness + lateral shoulder numbness after C4-7 fusion?"** → **C5 root**

**"Post-CDA presentation needing only reassurance?"** → **bilateral deltoid/biceps 3/5 + lateral arm numbness from day 5**

**"12 h after ACDF, dyspnoea, dysphagia, tense incision, SpO₂ normal?"** → **retropharyngeal haematoma → immediate evacuation** (TO)

**"Burr through C5 lateral wall?"** → **vertebral artery**

**"4-level ACDF, 6 h, 800 mL — post-op?"** → **keep intubated 24–48 h**

**"Hoarseness after ACDF — true?"** → **↑ with revision surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: right > left · immediate re-exploration

**"Reduces dysphagia?"** → **local retropharyngeal steroid**

**"Prolonged retraction at tracheo-oesophageal junction?"** → **vocal cord paresis**

**"Most concerning after 6-h C3-6 ACDF?"** → **airway complications**

**"Neck pain, can't hold horizon after prior laminectomy?"** → **post-laminectomy kyphosis**

**"True about CSM surgery?"** → **posterior has higher infection rate**

**"Hyperacute epidural haematoma on MRI?"** → **T1 hypo, T2 hyper**

**"Little finger dermatome?"** → **C8** (TO)

**"Deltoid 3/5 + lateral arm numbness?"** → **C4-5 disc** (TO)

**"C6-7 disc, shoulder abduction relief — EXCEPT?"** → **elbow flexion weakness** (TO)

**"Wrist extensor weakness — sensory change?"** → **radial forearm** (TO)

**"Weak wrist dorsiflexion + sluggish supinator reflex?"** → **C6** (TO)

**"Can't flex fingers, ulnar hand numb?"** → **C8** (TO)

**"NOT a contraindication to cervical disc replacement?"** → **2-level involvement** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: morbid obesity · RA · instability

**"Image shows anterior discectomy + ?"** → **disc replacement** (TO)

**"Canal diameter suggesting stenosis?"** → **< 10 mm** (TO)

**"Commonest site of cervical spondylosis?"** → **C5-6** (TO)
