# Cervical Radiculopathy

*Spine · 16 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **107.3 / 100,000 men**, 63.5 / 100,000 women per year · peak **50–54 yrs**

**Risk factors** — white race, **smoking**, prior lumbar radiculopathy

**Degeneration prevalence** — X-ray changes in **70% by 70** · MRI: HNP/foraminal stenosis in **28%** of asymptomatic > 40 · **≥ 1 degenerated disc in \~57% (50–60%)** of asymptomatic > 40 (Boden) · up to 19% asymptomatic root compression → **treat only if clinical findings match MRI**

### Aetiology

| Cause | Note |
|---|---|
| **Cervical spondylosis** | Disc-osteophyte, height loss, **uncovertebral + facet spurs** |
| **Soft disc** | **Posterolateral = commonest** (between uncinate and lateral PLL) → mostly **motor** · intraforaminal → **radicular pain** · midline → **myelopathy** |
| **Double crush** | Root + distal entrapment (↓ axoplasmic flow) |
| Rare | Tumour, root avulsion, synovial / meningeal cyst, dural AVF, tortuous vertebral artery |

**Chemical mediators** — **IL-1, IL-6, substance P, neuropeptide Y, CGRP, bradykinin, TNF-α, prostaglandins**

### Anatomy

**Pedicle-root mismatch** — cervical root exits **above** its pedicle (C6 above C6 pedicle) · lumbar root exits **below** (L5 under L5) · extra **C8** root makes the switch

**Disc → root** — cervical disc compresses the **lower (exiting) root**: **C6-7 → C7**

**Horizontal cervical root** — **central/paracentral and foraminal discs hit the SAME root** (vs lumbar: paracentral → traversing, foraminal → exiting) · far-lateral discs not typically described in C-spine

**Compression sources** — facet hypertrophy (posterior) · **uncovertebral osteophyte (anterior)** · disc height loss (↓ foraminal volume) · ventrolateral root course → ventral compression

**Disc** — annulus **type I collagen** (thicker ventrally) · nucleus **type II collagen + GAGs**; water **90% (< 30 yrs) → 70%** (8th decade)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — occipital headache, **trapezial / interscapular pain**, neck pain, unilateral arm pain (often non-dermatomal), dermatomal paraesthesia, weakness (overhead = C7, grip = C8)

### Root-level localisation

| Root (disc) | Motor | Reflex | Sensory |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C4** (C3-4) | **Scapular winging** | — | Base of neck; Tinel at levator scapulae |
| **C5** (C4-5) | **Deltoid + biceps** (shoulder abduction, ER, elbow flexion) | **Biceps** | **Superior shoulder, lateral arm** |
| **C6** (C5-6) | **Brachioradialis (elbow flexion in mid-pronation) + wrist extension (ECRL)**, biceps | **Brachioradialis (supinator)** | **Thumb, (index), dorsoradial forearm** |
| **C7** (C6-7) | **Triceps, wrist flexion**, finger extension, pronation | **Triceps** | **Middle finger** (index/ring), dorsal forearm, posterior arm |
| **C8** (C7-T1) | **Finger flexion — DIP of middle + index (FDP)**, grip, **thumb extension** | — | **Ring + little finger**, ulnar hand/forearm |
| **T1** (T1-2) | **Hand intrinsics** (finger abduction) | — | **Axilla / medial arm**, ipsilateral **Horner** |

**Commonest root** — **C7** (several studies)

**C8 vs ulnar neuropathy** — C8 weakens **index/middle FDP** (AIN-innervated) — ulnar entrapment does not

**C6 vs tennis elbow vs PIN** — C6: thumb paraesthesia + relief with shoulder abduction · lateral epicondylitis: pain with resisted wrist/long-finger extension · PIN: painless wrist extension weakness **with radial deviation**

**Facet fracture localisation** — **C6 superior facet fracture → C5-6 instability → C6 root** (radial forearm + thumb numbness)

### Provocative tests

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Shoulder abduction relief (SAR)** | Hand on head relieves pain (↓ root tension) — **virtually pathognomonic**, best separates cervical vs shoulder/peripheral causes; analogue of SLR |
| **Spurling** | **Extension + rotation + lateral bend to affected side + axial load** → ipsilateral arm pain; **specific, not sensitive** (✗ Wrong options: contralateral rotation) |
| Upper limb tension, Valsalva, neck distraction | Supportive |
| Hoffmann, inverted radial reflex, clonus, Babinski | **Myelopathy** (✗ Wrong options: radiculopathy) |

**Peripheral mimics** — Froment (ulnar), **"OK" sign** failure (AIN), Guyon (no dorsal wrist sensory change), cubital tunnel (worse with elbow **flexion**), diabetic neuropathy (glove-stocking)

**Differential** — carpal / cubital tunnel, **Parsonage-Turner**, thoracic outlet, myelopathy, brachial plexus injury, rotator cuff (history must match MRI)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| X-ray AP, lateral, **oblique** | **Oblique = best for foraminal stenosis** from osteophytes; flex-ext for instability |
| **MRI (T2 axial)** | Red flags (fever, weight loss, night pain), **> 6 weeks** of failed care, motor weakness; loss of perineural fat |
| CT | Bony anatomy, **OPLL**, instrumentation planning; **post-op pseudarthrosis study of choice** |
| CT myelogram | Pacemaker, hardware artefact |
| Discography | Rarely; oesophageal puncture, discitis |

### Genes & lab markers – electrodiagnostics

| EMG / NCS finding | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Fibrillations + positive sharp waves** | In affected root muscles (✓) — appear **\~3 weeks**; **paraspinals first** |
| **SNAPs normal** | Lesion **proximal to DRG** |
| CMAP | ↓ in proportion to atrophy |
| F-wave | ↓ / absent in severe cases (✗ Wrong options: ↑) |
| H-reflex | S1 only (✗ Wrong options: cervical) |
| Prolonged distal latency | Peripheral compression (✗ Wrong options: radiculopathy) |
| Sensitivity | **40–70%** — not a screening test; not needed with concordant exam + MRI |

**Selective nerve root block** — localises level in multilevel disease

## 5 Management

### Natural history & non-op care

**Natural history** — favourable: **75% (75–90%) improve non-op**\
✗ Wrong options: stepwise decline = myelopathy · needs PT/surgery to resolve

**Medications** — **NSAIDs** = most appropriate for chronic radicular pain (TO) · brief **oral steroids** · gabapentin · **nortriptyline** if depressed (↓ narcotic need)\
✗ Wrong options: narcotics · muscle relaxants (habituation, depression) · anticonvulsants

**Other** — short immobilisation **< 1–2 weeks** (avoid prolonged → atrophy) · isometrics, heat, traction (not in myelopathy)

**Selective nerve root steroid injection** — long-term relief **40–70% (50–70%)**; steroid + LA = LA alone long term; ↑ risks vs lumbar (dural puncture, meningitis, abscess, root injury)

**Axial neck pain only (no root/cord signs, solid old fusion)** — **education + PT**\
✗ Wrong options: hardware removal · revision · PSF · ESI

**Return to play** — after symptom resolution + **repeat MRI without cord compression**; **oral methylprednisolone** speeds return; **no ↑ catastrophic SCI risk** after C4-5 HNP · single-level ACDF not a contraindication · collegiate C5 radiculopathy → **NSAIDs + PT, RTP after resolution + repeat imaging**\
✗ Wrong options: immediate RTP on steroids · EMG · surgery

### Operative criteria

| Indication |
|---|
| Disabling pain after **3 months** of non-op care |
| **Progressive / significant neurologic deficit** |
| Static deficit with significant radicular pain |

### Surgical options

| Procedure | Indication / outcome |
|---|---|
| **ACDF** | **Gold standard**; ventral compression; e.g. 6 months C7 radiculopathy + ventral cord compression (✗ Wrong options: posterior foraminotomy (cord risk) · anterior foraminotomy (vertebral artery) · corpectomy · laminoplasty) |
| **Posterior foraminotomy** | **Foraminal soft disc / osteophyte, single level**, prior anterior surgery; **> 91% success**, \~3% complications, = ACDF outcomes, faster return to work, lower cost · not for central disc, myelopathy, instability, OPLL, kyphosis |
| Anterior foraminotomy | Unilateral root, avoid fusion (smokers, diabetics); uncovertebral resection; **vertebral artery + sympathetic chain risk**; not widely accepted |
| **CDA** | Single level, minimal facet arthrosis; **= ACDF** (arm pain, NDI, SF-36, neuro; **PROMs equivalent at 2–5 yrs**); ASD + reoperation + dysphagia similar; less neck pain; **heterotopic bone 60%** (no motion effect) |

**Best posterior foraminotomy candidate** — **young, foraminal soft disc at C6-7, C7 radiculopathy**\
✗ Wrong options: myelopathy · SCI · midline disc-osteophyte · MS

### ACDF technique

**Approach** — Smith-Robinson · **transverse incision 1–2 levels**, longitudinal for multilevel / short thick neck

**Landmarks** — mandible **C1-2** · hyoid **C3-4** · thyroid cartilage **C4-6** · **cricoid C5-6**

**Side** — left-sided **lower RLN risk** theoretically; **C7-T1 left → thoracic duct** risk · **deflate ET cuff after retractor placement** (RLN compressed against tube)

**Plates** — ↑ fusion in **multilevel** (↓ graft complications, reoperation, earlier return to work); **single-level: no difference** · dynamic (controlled settling) vs static (fixed angle) · old Caspar bicortical → neuro risk · screw length > 80% AP → ↓ pseudarthrosis

**Graft** — **ICBG autograft gold standard** (donor pain) · allograft ↑ pseudarthrosis (**41% vs 27%**) + subsidence (**28% vs 16%**) (Orthobullets) · **no difference single level** (OB) — read the options

**Post-op** — walk day 0, soft collar briefly (hard collar if no plate), ROM at 6 wks, full activity 3 months

## 6 Complications

### Pseudarthrosis

**Rates** — single level **5–10%** (Orthobullets complications) · 20% (Orthobullets outcomes) · **2.6–4.8%** (OB) · multilevel **30% / > 50%** · doesn't always correlate with outcome

**Risk factors** — **smoking + diabetes** (strongest, modifiable) · multilevel · revision (✗ Wrong options: graft type · plate · level · alignment single level) · **smoking cessation ≥ 4 weeks pre-op** = greatest fusion impact (✗ Wrong options: plate · autograft · collar)

**Treatment** — asymptomatic → observe · symptomatic → **posterior cervical fusion** (↑ fusion, ↓ revision; but ↑ blood loss, stay, complications) or anterior revision if radiculopathy (EXCEPT: posterior does NOT ↓ fusion rate)

### ACDF complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **RLN palsy** | **Commonest nerve injury** (1–11%; 2.7%); vagus branch; **left hooks aortic arch, right hooks subclavian** (shorter, crosses lateral → medial higher, can be aberrant) → right **theoretically** riskier (TO: left approach preferred) · **no proven clinical difference** (Orthobullets, OB); posterior cricoarytenoid paralysis; observe → ENT at 6 wks (Teflon) |
| **Revision after RLN palsy** | **Same side** as prior approach; **pre-op laryngoscopy** for revision / prior thyroidectomy (bilateral palsy → stridor) · not contralateral approach |
| **Superior laryngeal nerve** | Runs with **superior thyroid artery** (upper C-spine); cricothyroid → pitch / voice fatigue |
| **Hypoglossal** | Upper C-spine (C2-3); **tongue deviates toward injured side** |
| **Horner** | Sympathetic chain on **lateral longus colli at C6**; retractors not under longus colli → ptosis, miosis, anhidrosis, enophthalmos |
| **Vertebral artery** | **Lateral discectomy / uncovertebral** bleeding; aberrant medial course (C6) |
| **Dysphagia** | ↑ at C3-4; **zero-profile cages** ↓ prominence; local steroid helps |
| **Oesophageal perforation** | Early: sharp instruments → repair now · late: plate loosening → NG + TPN |
| **Airway** | **> 5 h surgery**, exposure **above C4**, **> 4 levels / ≥ 4 bodies** (✗ Wrong options: smoking · lung disease · myelopathy · drains) |
| **CSF leak (TO)** | \~1% (↑ OPLL 12.5%, revision, corpectomy, men); intra-op → watertight repair; post-op → bed rest → **lumbar subarachnoid drain** → open repair |
| **Adjacent-level ossification** | **Plate < 5 mm from supra-adjacent disc** (✗ Wrong options: needle · stripping · longus colli); **start screws near inferior endplate** of top vertebra ↓ it |
| ASD | \~3%/yr, 30% at 10 yrs |

## X vs Y

| | Cervical | Lumbar |
|---|---|---|
| Root vs pedicle | Exits **above** | Exits **below** |
| Root course | Horizontal | Vertical |
| Paracentral disc | **Same root as foraminal** | Traversing root |
| Example | C5-6 → C6 | L4-5 paracentral → L5, foraminal → L4 |

| | Radiculopathy | Myelopathy |
|---|---|---|
| Natural history | **75% improve** | Stepwise decline |
| Signs | Dermatomal, SAR, Spurling | Hoffmann, clonus, gait |
| First line | Non-op | Surgery if impaired |

## How it's asked

**"Left foraminal C4/5 disc — exam?"** → **shoulder abduction + elbow flexion weakness**

**"Triceps weakness, ↓ triceps reflex, middle finger numbness — level?"** → **C6-7**

**"Posterolateral C5/6 disc?"** → **C6: brachioradialis + wrist extension weakness**

**"C5-6 disc — sensory loss?"** → **dorsoradial forearm + thumb**

**"Thumb numbness, weak wrist extension + supination?"** → **C5/6, C6 root**

**"Small finger numbness, weak index/middle DIP flexion + thumb extension?"** → **C7/T1**

**"Index-long-ring numbness, triceps weak, SAR positive?"** → **foraminal C6-7 disc**

**"Lateral forearm pain relieved by abduction, thumb numb, wrist extension 4/5?"** → **paracentral C5/6 disc**\
✗ Wrong options: ECRB tendinosis · PIN

**"C7 distribution pain — motor + MRI?"** → **wrist flexion weakness + C6-7 uncovertebral foraminal stenosis**

**"Biceps, brachialis, wrist extensors weak, thumb/index numb — posterolateral HNP?"** → **C5-6**

**"Middle finger numb, finger extensors 4/5, triceps 1+?"** → **C6-7**

**"C6-7 HNP — distribution?"** → **forearm/hand pain, middle finger numbness, triceps + finger extensor weakness**

**"Anterior arm → dorsoradial forearm → index, biceps weak, BR reflex lost?"** → **C5-C6**

**"Long finger extension weak, SAR positive, normal obliques?"** → **C7 radiculopathy, paracentral C6/7 disc**

**"EMG finding with C6 radiculopathy?"** → **fibrillations + positive sharp waves in C6 muscles**

**"Foraminal C7/T1 disc?"** → **small finger numbness + weakness of all finger flexion**

**"C5-6 axial disc, negative Hoffmann?"** → **brachioradialis weakness**

**"Unstable superior facet fracture + radial forearm/thumb numbness?"** → **C6 superior facet, C5-6 instability**

**"C6-7 paracentral disc — exam?"** → **weak elbow extension, ↓ triceps reflex, dorsal middle finger numbness**

**"Supports radiculopathy over peripheral neuropathy?"** → **relief with arm above head**

**"Diabetic, ulnar hand tingling, better with hand above head?"** → **cervical radiculopathy**

**"Exam supporting radiculopathy?"** → **shoulder abduction test** · **Spurling sign**\
✗ Wrong options: Hoffmann · inverted BR reflex · intrinsic wasting

**"Hypothenar pain — finding most suggestive of cervical cause?"** → **relief with shoulder abduction**

**"Lateral shoulder pain but C7 compression on MRI?"** → **further history + exam**

**"Chronic radicular pain — drug?"** → **NSAIDs** (TO)

**"Axial neck pain 12 yrs after solid C6-7 ACDF, no deficit?"** → **education + PT**

**"C7 radiculopathy, wants injection — level + 1-yr success?"** → **C6/7, 50–70%**

**"Expected outcome with NSAIDs + rest?"** → **> 75% chance of resolution**

**"NFL player after C4-5 HNP, now normal — added SCI risk?"** → **none**

**"Triceps weakness, sleeps with arm overhead, Spurling positive — true?"** → **will improve with NSAIDs + PT**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery now · EMG next · C7/T1

**"Asymptomatic > 40 — degenerated disc prevalence?"** → **50–60%**

**"Collegiate linebacker, C5 radiculopathy, wants fast return?"** → **NSAIDs + PT; RTP after resolution + repeat imaging**

**"C6 radiculopathy with 3+ patellar reflexes — true?"** → **likely resolves non-op**\
✗ Wrong options: interferon · EMG required · root exits below pedicle · Hoffmann

**"Best posterior foraminotomy candidate?"** → **foraminal soft disc C6/7, young male**

**"6 months C7 radiculopathy, ventral compression?"** → **ACDF**

**"CDA vs ACDF at 2–5 yrs?"** → **equivalent PROMs**

**"Anterior approach CSF leak — correct?"** → **may need lumbar CSF drain** (TO)

**"Structure at more risk on RIGHT-sided approach?"** → **recurrent laryngeal nerve** (TO)

**"Ptosis + miosis after left ACDF?"** → **retractor placement (sympathetic chain)** (TO)

**"True about anterior approach (TO)?"** → **left preferred — right RLN more vulnerable**

**"Hoarseness + unilateral cord palsy after ACDF?"** → **RLN palsy** (TO)

**"Left hypoglossal injury at C2-3?"** → **tongue deviates left**

**"Prior left ACDF + left cord palsy, now C4/5 myelopathy?"** → **hardware removal + C4/5 ACDF via left approach**

**"Left cord paralysis — CONTRAINDICATED revision?"** → **right-sided ACDF**

**"True about RLN?"** → **commonest nerve injury in ACDF**

**"Airway complication risk factor?"** → **surgery > 5 h**

**"Brisk bleeding during lateral discectomy?"** → **vertebral artery**

**"Posterior cricoarytenoid paralysis?"** → **RLN**

**"Prolonged longus colli retraction?"** → **Horner syndrome**

**"Bilateral cord palsy after left ACDF with prior thyroidectomy — prevention?"** → **pre-op laryngoscopy**

**"Posterior fusion for pseudarthrosis — EXCEPT?"** → **decreased fusion rate**

**"Greatest impact on fusion in a smoker?"** → **smoking cessation 4 weeks pre-op**

**"Pseudarthrosis after single-level ACDF — factor?"** → **diabetes + tobacco**

**"Osteophyte at disc above C6-7 fusion?"** → **plate close to adjacent disc**

**"Factor ↑ adjacent segment disease?"** → **plate distance from adjacent disc**

**"Adjacent segment ossification?"** → **plate < 5 mm from supra-adjacent disc**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Spine Anatomy

*Spine · 17 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Embryology

**Hox (homeobox) genes** — direct craniocaudal differentiation + segmentation

**Atlas (C1)** — **3 ossification centres**: 2 lateral masses (join posteriorly → posterior arch) + anterior arch (appears **\~1 yr**); lateral masses fuse to body at **7 yrs**

**Axis (C2)** — **5 centres**: 2 neural arches/lateral masses, 2 halves of dens (usually fused at birth; persistence = dens bicornis), body · **subdental (basilar / dentocentral) synchondrosis** open in **all children to 3 yrs**, half at 4–5, fused **\~6 yrs** · secondary tip centre (ossiculum terminale) appears **\~3 yrs**, fuses **\~12 yrs** (persistence = ossiculum terminale persistens, insignificant)

**3-yr-old with synchondrosis between dens and body** — **normal physiologic finding**\
✗ Wrong options: developmental anomaly · type 1/3 odontoid fracture

### Osteology

| Feature | Levels |
|---|---|
| **Transverse foramen** | **C1–C7** (all subaxial vertebrae in most people) |
| **Vertebral artery passes through** | **C6 → C1**; **C7 foramen usually empty** |
| **Bifid spinous process** | **C2–C6** (C7 not bifid) |
| **Carotid (Chassaignac) tubercle** | **C6** — anterior approach landmark |
| C8 | No vertebra; **C8 root** exists |
| Atlas | **No body, no spinous process** |

**Canal** — normal **17 mm**; **< 13 mm** → possible cord compression

**Alignment** — lordosis measured C2–C7

### Facets & kinematics

**Subaxial superior facets** — face **posteromedial at C3 → posterolateral at C7**\
✗ Wrong options: reverse

**Axial view** — **superior articular process is anterior** to the inferior articular process

**Motion** — greatest at **cephalad** subaxial levels (facet orientation)\
✗ Wrong options: caudal

| Joint | Contribution |
|---|---|
| **Occiput-C1** | **\~50% of flexion-extension** |
| **C1-C2** (diarthrodial) | **50° of 100° rotation**, 10° of 110° flex-ext, 0° lateral bend |
| C2–C7 | 50° flex-ext, 50° rotation, **60° lateral bend** |

### Odontoid blood supply

**Watershed** between **apex (internal carotid branches)** and **base (vertebral artery branches)** → poor healing of **type II odontoid** fractures

### Ligaments

**Transverse** — limits anterior translation of atlas (strongest ligament of spine) · **apical + alar** — limit rotation

| Connects | Ligaments (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Atlas → occiput** | **Anterior atlanto-occipital membrane** (ALL extension) · **posterior atlanto-occipital membrane** · **ascending band of cruciate** |
| Axis (dens) → occiput | **Tectorial membrane** (PLL extension; C2–3 bodies → FM) · **alar** (dens → condyles) · **apical** (dens tip → FM, weak) |
| Axis → atlas | **Transverse atlantal ligament** · atlanto-alar part of alar · descending band of cruciate |

![Transverse, alar and apical ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2069-combined-moved.jpg)

### Vertebral artery segments (TO)

| Segment | Course |
|---|---|
| V1 **Pre-foraminal** | Subclavian → front of C7 transverse process (between longus colli + scalenus anterior) |
| V2 **Foraminal** | Transverse foramina **C6 → C2** |
| V3 **Extradural (atlantic)** | Above C2 → **through C1 transverse foramen** → horizontal in occipital triangle → canal |
| V4 **Intradural** | Pierces dura → anterior medulla → joins opposite → **basilar** at lower pons |

**C1 transverse foramen contains** — **extradural segment of vertebral artery** + venous plexus + sympathetic plexus (TO)

**Medial / enlarged transverse foramen on CT before ACDF** — ↑ **vertebral artery injury** risk\
✗ Wrong options: IJV · ICA · vagus

### Pedicle & body dimensions (TO)

| Measure | Level |
|---|---|
| **Smallest transverse pedicle diameter** | **C3** (5.1 mm); cervical pedicles C2 largest, C3 smallest, ↑ C3 → C7 |
| Narrowest pedicle overall (average) | **T4** |
| Largest pedicle diameter / longest pedicle | **L5** |
| Shortest pedicle | T10 |
| Largest canal cross-section | **C2** (374 mm²) |
| Smallest canal | **T3–T8** |
| Smallest body | C3 + C5 · narrowest body width C4 |
| Tallest / shortest disc | L2-3 / T1-2 |
| Lumbar pedicles | Angle more medially caudally; L1 smallest lumbar |

### C1 lateral mass screw

**Trajectory** — **10° medial + 22° cephalad** · safe even with **anomalous vertebral artery** that precludes transarticular screws · medial angle protects **internal carotid**

### Muscles

**Sternocleidomastoid (TO)** — one side: **rotates head to OPPOSITE side** + tilts to same side · both: **flex neck + extend head**, lift head off pillow, accessory inspiration \
✗ Wrong options: rotate to same side (EXCEPT answer)

## X vs Y

| | C1 (atlas) | C2 (axis) | C7 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | No | ✓ + dens | Yes |
| Spinous process | No | Bifid | Non-bifid (prominens) |
| Ossification centres | 3 | 5 | — |
| Vertebral artery in foramen | Yes | Yes | Usually no |

## How it's asked

**"Structure in C1 transverse foramen?"** → **extradural segment of vertebral artery** (TO)

**"3-yr-old, synchondrosis between dens and body?"** → **normal**

**"Subaxial anatomy — true?"** → **all subaxial vertebrae have a foramen transversarium**\
✗ Wrong options: artery through all · rotation greatest caudally · facets posterolateral → posteromedial caudally · inferior process anterior

**"Medially placed, enlarged transverse foramen before ACDF — risk?"** → **vertebral artery injury**

**"C1 lateral mass screw trajectory?"** → **10° medial, 22° cephalad**

**"SCM functions EXCEPT?"** → **rotate neck to same side** (TO)

**"Smallest pedicle diameter (C2–C5)?"** → **C3** (TO)

**"Ligament connecting atlas to occiput?"** → **ascending band of cruciate** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: tectorial membrane · alar · TAL

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Spine Trauma Evaluation

*Spine · 18 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Rule** — every trauma patient has a C-spine injury **until proven otherwise**

**Clearance** — confirming **absence** of injury by exam ± imaging; do it **efficiently** (delay ↑ complications)

**Missed injuries** — permanent disability; causes: **inadequate imaging of the injured level**, LOC, multisystem trauma

**Noncontiguous spinal injuries** — **10–15%** (Orthobullets) · up to **16%** (OB) → **image the whole spine** once one fracture is found

**High-energy markers** — MVA **> 35 mph**, fall **> 10 ft**, closed head injury, neuro deficit, pelvis/limb fractures

**Mechanism clues** — elderly fall onto forehead / high-speed rear-end → **hyperextension**

**Comorbidities to identify** — **AS, DISH**, prior fusion, ligamentous laxity disorders

## 3 Presentation

**Primary survey** — ABC; **manual in-line traction** whenever collar off for airway; inspect whole spine; **seat-belt sign** → TL flexion-distraction

**Secondary survey** — remove collar, inspect face/scalp, head posture (rotation → **unilateral facet dislocation**), **palpate posterior midline** (no tenderness in alert patient → low probability of injury), **log roll**, full neuro exam

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – clearance

| Clinical clearance (no imaging) — ALL of | Radiographic clearance — ANY of |
|---|---|
| **Awake, alert, not intoxicated** | Intoxicated |
| **No neck pain, tenderness or neuro deficit** | Altered mental status / obtunded |
| **No distracting injury** | Neck pain or tenderness |
| — | Distracting injury |

**Paediatric** — **NEXUS-based clinical guidelines → fewer CTs** = most effective radiation reduction · < 3 yrs can't cooperate; X-ray screen first, CT if abnormal\
✗ Wrong options: fluoroscopy · low-dose CT · avoiding radiographs · MRI for all

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **Lateral X-ray** | Must show **inferior endplate of C7** (Orthobullets) · **top of T1** (OB) |
| AP + **open-mouth** odontoid | Standard trio |
| **Swimmer's view (TO)** | **Cervicothoracic (C7-T1) junction** (✗ Wrong options: Hill-Sachs · Bankart · AO junction) |
| **CT** | Higher sensitivity, faster; must include **superior endplate of T1** (Orthobullets) · **inferior endplate of T1** (OB answer) — read the options |
| MRI | Ligament / cord / disc |
| Flexion-extension | Not in acute / known instability or deficit; only to exclude occult instability **after** clearance |

**Lateral X-ray lines** — anterior vertebral line · posterior vertebral body line · spinolaminar line

![Lateral C-spine alignment lines](../../MCQ/attachments/q4500-illa-cervical-lines.jpg)

| Prevertebral / retropharyngeal soft tissue (TO) | Upper limit |
|---|---|
| **C2** | **6–7 mm** ("above cricoid" → **< 7 mm**) |
| C3 | 7 mm |
| **C6 (cricoid)** | **18–22 mm** (retropharyngeal **< 22 mm**) |
| Child at C6 | 14 mm |

**Obtunded / intubated after 3 days, X-rays negative, occipital skin erythema** — **definitive clearance with CT ± MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: keep collar until surgeries done · halo · wait to examine

**GCS 9, multitrauma** — **CT C-spine**\
✗ Wrong options: remove collar · wait without imaging · X-rays to C7 only · flex-ext

**Burst fracture found (C5 / C7)** — **CT of entire spine** before surgery\
✗ Wrong options: steroids · flex-ext · MRA · EMG · lumbar MRI

## 5 Management

**Collar** — from scene until directed exam

**Whiplash** — rear-end low-speed extension injury, normal neuro + X-rays → **soft collar + early ROM / PT** (✗ Wrong options: Philadelphia collar · halo · C2 screws) · persistent pain at 4 wks → **NSAIDs + activity modification + PT** (✗ Wrong options: observation · ESI · facet blocks · MRI)

**Injury → procedure (OB)** — **flexion teardrop** → **anterior corpectomy + strut + plate** · AAI with cord compression → posterior C1-2 fusion · bilateral facet dislocation → anterior or posterior reduction + fusion · central cord + spondylosis → ACDF or posterior decompression-fusion · C7 Chance (bony) → halo or posterior fusion

### Transient quadriparesis – return to play

**Recurrent transient quadriparesis + cervical stenosis on CT/MRI** — **no further contact sport** · stenosis **< 13 mm**, absolute **< 10 mm** · **Torg ratio has low predictive value** (Cantu)\
✗ Wrong options: neck roll · EMG · ESI · steroid pack

## 6 Complications

**Delayed clearance** — **aspiration**, ↓ respiratory function, **occipital / submandibular pressure ulcers**, ↑ ICP

### Prognosis

**Elderly (75 vs 60 yrs) incomplete cervical SCI** — **↑ mortality** · elderly: lower-energy falls with head strike, fewer deficits\
✗ Wrong options: more severe deficits · more paralysis · epidural haematoma · chronic pain

**Preadolescent SCI (e.g. 3-yr-old T2/3 paraplegia)** — **≥ 90% develop paralytic scoliosis**; bracing doesn't prevent it · uncommon post-adolescence

## X vs Y

| | Clinical clearance | Imaging clearance |
|---|---|---|
| Mental state | Alert, sober | Obtunded / intoxicated |
| Symptoms | No pain, tenderness, deficit | Pain, tenderness, deficit |
| Distracting injury | None | Present |

## How it's asked

**"C5 burst on X-ray, MRI shows retropulsion — before surgery?"** → **CT of entire spine**

**"Intubated 3 days, X-rays negative, occipital erythema?"** → **definitive CT and/or MRI clearance**

**"C7 burst on MRI with deficits — next study?"** → **CT cervical, thoracic + lumbar**

**"Most effective radiation reduction in paediatric trauma?"** → **clinical guidelines → fewer CTs**

**"GCS 9, collar on, multiple fractures?"** → **CT C-spine to T1**

**"Injury suited to anterior corpectomy + plate?"** → **flexion teardrop fracture**

**"< 5 mph rear-end, neck stiffness, normal X-rays?"** → **soft orthosis + early PT**

**"4 weeks of whiplash pain?"** → **NSAIDs, activity modification, PT**

**"75- vs 60-yr incomplete SCI — 75-yr has ↑?"** → **mortality**

**"3-yr-old with complete paraplegia — later deformity?"** → **≥ 90%**

**"Recurrent transient quadriparesis with stenosis?"** → **stop football**

**"Normal prevertebral space above cricoid?"** → **< 7 mm** (TO)

**"Normal retropharyngeal space at cricoid?"** → **< 22 mm** (TO)

**"Swimmer's view shows?"** → **cervicothoracic junction** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Cervical Spondylosis

*Spine · 19 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Prevalence & high-risk sports

**Onset** — 40–50 yrs · **85%** show spondylosis regardless of symptoms: **> 65 yrs** (Orthobullets) · **> 60 yrs** (OB) · men > women

**Levels** — **C5-6 > C6-7** (most subaxial flexion-extension) · risk: excessive driving, smoking, lifting, professional athletes

**Highest spinal degeneration in sport** — **jockeys (horse racing)** · also wrestling, rugby, ski-jumping, gymnastics\
✗ Wrong options: badminton · baseball · volleyball · golf

### Pathoanatomy

**Motion segment** — disc + **2 facet joints + 2 uncovertebral joints (Luschka)**

```
Degenerative cascade
├─ Disc → desiccation, height loss, bulge / herniation
├─ Joints → uncinate spurs + facet arthrosis
├─ Ligaments → LF thickening + infolding (height loss)
└─ Deformity → kyphosis → load shifts to facets/uncovertebral → more spurs
```

**Clinical syndromes** — axial / discogenic neck pain · radiculopathy · myelopathy

## 2 Mechanism

**Root compression (radiculopathy)** — foraminal spurs (facet + uncovertebral) · posterolateral disc / disc-osteophyte between uncinate and lateral PLL · foraminal soft disc → **exiting root** (C6-7 → C7)

**Central stenosis (myelopathy)** — canal **< 13 mm** (normal 17) · **worse in extension**: cord pinched between disc anteriorly and facets + infolded LF posteriorly

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Findings |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | Uncovertebral + facet degeneration, osteophytes, disc narrowing, **endplate sclerosis**, ↓ sagittal diameter; **poor symptom correlation** · lateral: alignment, canal size |
| **Oblique** | **Foraminal stenosis** (uncovertebral arthrosis) |
| Flexion-extension | Angular / translational instability; compensatory subluxation next to stiff segment |
| **MRI (axial)** | Modality of choice; disc degeneration, myelomalacia; **28%** false positives > 40 yrs |
| CT myelogram | Bony detail + neural status; pacemaker / hardware |
| Discography | Rarely; oesophageal puncture, discitis |

![Degenerative cervical spondylosis on lateral X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2029-degen-cervical.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Treatment** — by pain severity/chronicity, instability, neuro deficit: observation → medical → surgery

**Axial neck pain only, normal neuro (70-yr, 2 months)** — counsel that **85% of > 60s show these X-ray findings**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery to prevent decline · MRI for radiculopathy (clinical diagnosis) · "< 10% improve non-op"

## How it's asked

**"Sport with highest cervical + lumbar degeneration?"** → **jockeys**

**"70-yr axial neck pain, spondylotic X-ray, no deficits — counsel?"** → **85% of > 60-yr-olds have these findings**

**"Commonest level?"** → **C5-6**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Chance Fracture (Flexion-Distraction Injury)

*Spine · 20 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — thoracolumbar **flexion-distraction (seat-belt / jack-knife)** injury; described by **GQ Chance 1948** (purely osseous)

**Level** — **T12–L2 / T12–L3** (transitional zone; TO: D12–L2)

**Types** — **bony** (fracture through spinous process → lamina → pedicles → body; heals well) · **ligamentous / discoligamentous** (through disc + ligaments; heals poorly) · mixed

**Neuro deficit** — relatively **low** incidence (TO)

### Associated intra-abdominal injuries (lap belt)

**GI injury \~50%** — **commonest associated injury** (✗ Wrong options: head injury · pulmonary contusion · pelvis · GU) · colon rupture, small bowel / **duodenal** tear (✗ Wrong options: tamponade · bladder rupture · cholecystitis)

**Seat-belt sign** (abdominal ecchymosis) → suspect **flexion-distraction injury** + bowel injury · lap-only belts (old cars, rear seat, children)\
✗ Wrong options: L5 TP fracture (pelvic ring) · burst · pars · HNP

## 2 Mechanism

**Axis of rotation** — **anterior to the spine** (abdominal wall / **abdominal viscera**) — lap belt acts as fulcrum\
✗ Wrong options: ISL · PLL · ALL

**Column failure** — **posterior + middle columns fail in tension (distraction)** · **anterior column**: fails in **compression** (Orthobullets, OB) · "no crushing of the body" (TO) — TO answer: **middle + posterior columns fail in distraction**

**Posterior column failure mode** — **tension**\
✗ Wrong options: torsion · shear · compression

**Pure ligamentous variant (X-ray)** — **splayed spinous processes + ↑ posterior disc height**, no body fracture

![Chance fracture: posterior + middle tension, anterior compression](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2023-chance-fx-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| X-ray AP + lateral | **Interspinous widening**, horizontal fracture line, empty pedicle sign on AP |
| Flexion-extension | Instability |
| **CT** | Bony extent; retropulsion |
| **MRI** | **Posterior ligamentous complex (PLC)** — ↑ T2 in ISL / PLL = ligamentous injury |

![Lateral X-ray: Chance fracture through posterior elements](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2023-lateral-xray-moved.jpg)

### AO thoracolumbar classification (TO)

| Type | Pattern |
|---|---|
| **A0** | Spinous / transverse process fracture |
| A1 | Wedge, one endplate, posterior wall intact |
| A2 | Split, both endplates, posterior wall intact |
| A3 | Incomplete burst — one endplate + posterior wall |
| A4 | Complete burst — both endplates + posterior wall |
| **B1** | **Monosegmental osseous posterior tension band failure = bony Chance** |
| **B2** | **Osseoligamentous posterior tension band** disruption (± bone; ISL T2 signal; heals slower) |
| B3 | **Hyperextension** — anterior tension band failure (ankylosed spines) |
| C | Translation / dislocation (no subtypes; all operative) |

**Neuro modifier** — N0 intact · N1 transient · N2 radicular · N3 incomplete SCI / cauda equina · N4 complete · NX unknown · "+" ongoing compression

**Case modifiers** — **M1** indeterminate tension band injury · **M2** comorbidity (AS, skin, rheumatologic)

**B1 vs B2** — B1: CT line through spinous process, MRI posterior elements may look normal · B2: ISL hyperintense, body comminution favours B2

### Operative criteria (TO – TLICS)

| TLICS component | Points |
|---|---|
| **Distraction** mechanism | **4** |
| PLC injury suspected / confirmed | 2 / 3 |
| Neuro intact | 0 |
| **Total ≥ 5** | **Surgery** (≤ 3 non-op) |

## 5 Management

```
Chance / flexion-distraction injury
├─ Purely bony, PLC intact, neuro intact, kyphosis < 15–20° → HYPEREXTENSION CAST / TLSO (12–16 wks)
│      (serial X-rays for nonunion + kyphosis)
├─ Ligamentous / mixed (PLC torn) → POSTERIOR COMPRESSION INSTRUMENTATION + FUSION
│      (1 level above + 1 below with pedicle screws)
└─ Neuro deficit → DECOMPRESSION + STABILISATION
```

**Non-op union** — > 90% in selected bony injuries (cancellous bone heals)

**Surgical construct** — **compression** construct for Chance · historically 3 above + 2 below · modern pedicle screws **1 above + 1 below** (short segment ± index screws)\
✗ Wrong options: distraction (that's for burst)

**Anterior** — vertebrectomy + strut + instrumentation (decompression)

**Laminectomy** — only for compressive deficit; further destabilises posterior column

**Bowel perforation** — laparotomy first; spine staged or same sitting

**Paediatric soft-tissue Chance (8-yr, L3-4, CT negative for bone)** — **L3-4 posterior instrumented fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous screws (bony only) · TLSO · L1-5 fusion + laminectomy · PLIF

**Bony-ligamentous L2 (PLC + PLL signal, through superior endplate), ASIA E (TO)** — **posterior short-segment instrumentation**\
✗ Wrong options: cast (not purely osseous) · surgery justified by seat-belt sign · TLSO because neuro intact

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Note |
|---|---|
| **Pain** | Commonest |
| **Progressive kyphosis** | **Unrecognised PLL/PLC injury** |
| Scoliosis, flat back | Forward-flexed posture, fatigue |
| Post-traumatic syringomyelia | — |
| Nonunion | Ligamentous / non-op |

## X vs Y

| | Chance (flexion-distraction) | Burst |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | Flexion + distraction, axis **anterior** | **Axial load** ± flexion, axis middle column |
| Posterior column | **Tension** failure | Usually intact |
| Middle column | Tension | **Compression** (retropulsion) |
| Associated | **Bowel injury 50%** | Calcaneus, other spine |
| Construct | **Compression** | Distraction |

| | Bony Chance (AO B1) | Ligamentous (AO B2) |
|---|---|---|
| Healing | Good | Poor |
| Rx | **Hyperextension brace / cast** | **Posterior fusion** |

## How it's asked

**"Lap-belt bruising + Chance fracture — commonest associated injury?"** → **colonic / GI injury**

**"Seat-belt sign, GCS 8 — suspect?"** → **flexion-distraction injury**

**"Lap belt + small bowel tear — spinal injury?"** → **flexion-distraction**

**"Lap belt, posterior column involved — likely associated injury?"** → **gastrointestinal**

**"Mechanism of Chance fracture?"** → **flexion-distraction** (TO)

**"Columns failing in Chance fracture?"** → **middle + posterior in distraction** (TO)

**"Posterior column failure mode?"** → **tension**

**"Centre of rotation, ligamentous Chance?"** → **abdominal viscera (anterior to spine)**

**"8-yr soft-tissue Chance L3-4, no deficit?"** → **L3-4 posterior instrumented fusion**

**"Bony Chance — AO type?"** → **B1** (TO)

**"L2 bony-ligamentous Chance with PLC signal, ASIA E?"** → **posterior short-segment instrumentation** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Muscular Torticollis

*Spine · 21 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest cause of infantile torticollis** — **0.3–2.0%**\
✗ Wrong options: Klippel-Feil · Chiari · AARD · paroxysmal torticollis

**Sex** — **M:F 3:2** · mostly **right** side (TO)

**Risk factors** — **first pregnancy**, **breech**, traumatic delivery, oligohydramnios (packaging)

### Pathophysiology

**Lesion** — **sternocleidomastoid (SCM) contracture / fibromatosis**\
✗ Wrong options: platysma · omohyoid · longus colli · trapezius

**Theories** — compression/stretch injury · **venous outflow obstruction → compartment syndrome** of SCM

**Mass** — palpable at birth or **within 4 weeks**, middle–lower third of sternal head; regresses over months → fibrous cord

### Associated packaging disorders

| Condition | Note |
|---|---|
| **DDH** | **5–15%** → examine hips |
| **Metatarsus adductus** | ✓ |
| Calcaneovalgus feet | ✓ |
| **Plagiocephaly** | **Contralateral** side |
| Congenital atlanto-occipital anomalies | ✓ |

### Anatomy – SCM

**Origin** — sternal head (manubrium) + clavicular head (medial third of clavicle)

**Insertion** — mastoid process + lateral occipital bone

**Nerve** — **spinal accessory (CN XI)** — at risk in release

**Action** — ipsilateral tilt/flexion + **contralateral rotation** of the chin

## 3 Presentation

**Posture** — **head tilt TOWARD** affected SCM + **chin rotated AWAY** ("cock-robin")

**Painless** passive motion · older child: tight band, ↓ rotation + lateral flexion

**Exam** — vision, hearing, neuro, **hips**, feet, spine

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Indication / finding |
|---|---|
| **Ultrasound** | **Modality of choice with palpable mass** + ↓ ROM; enlarged **hyperechoic** (fibrotic) SCM; separates CMT from serious causes; follows fibrosis over time |
| **X-ray C-spine** | Tilt/rotation **without a mass**; exclude bony causes |
| Dynamic CT C1-2 | Exclude AARD |
| MRI brain + C-spine | Non-muscular / central causes |

## 5 Management

```
Congenital muscular torticollis
├─ < 1 yr, < 30° ROM loss → PASSIVE STRETCHING (90–95% respond in year 1)
│      (tilt away + rotate chin toward affected side)
└─ Failed ≥ 1 yr stretching → BIPOLAR RELEASE / Z-LENGTHENING of SCM
```

**Surgical timing** — after **≥ 1 yr failed stretching**; 92% success even in older children; facial asymmetry improves if done **before 10 yrs** (Orthobullets) · **after 3–4 yrs** — **release before 3 yrs → deep painful scarring** (TO)

**Technique** — short proximal incision behind ear (mastoid) ± distal sternal/clavicular incision · unipolar (clavicular) or **bipolar (preferred)**; Z-plasty clavicular head + mastoid release · CN XI at risk · botox for refractory (OB)

## 6 Complications

**Untreated** — permanent rotational deformity, **positional plagiocephaly**, craniofacial asymmetry / **hemihypoplasia**, compensatory scoliosis

## X vs Y

| | CMT | AARD / Grisel | Klippel-Feil | Paroxysmal torticollis of infancy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pain | **Painless** | **Painful** | Painless | Episodic |
| Mass | **SCM mass** | No | No | No |
| SCM spasm vs chin | **Opposite** side (SCM primary) | Same side (secondary) | — | — |
| Key | Breech, first-born, DDH | URTI / trauma | Short webbed neck, low hairline, ↓ ROM | Vestibular; pallor, ataxia; resolves by 2–3 yrs |
| Rx | Stretching | Collar → traction → fusion | — | Self-limiting |

![Congenital muscular torticollis: tilt toward, chin away](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2052-combined-chla.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"True about SCM contracture?"** → **surgery before 3 yrs → deep painful scarring** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: disappears after infancy · best age 1–2 · unipolar sternal release suffices

**"Treatment of choice in infancy?"** → **passive stretching** (TO)

**"Muscle implicated?"** → **sternocleidomastoid**

**"6-week infant, head tilt since birth + neck mass — commonest cause?"** → **congenital muscular torticollis**

**"Imaging with palpable mass?"** → **ultrasound**

**"Associated condition to screen?"** → **DDH**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Congenital Scoliosis

*Spine · 22 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & aetiology

**Definition** — failure of normal vertebral development at **4–6 weeks of gestation** (mesenchymal anlage) — same window as **heart + kidneys**

**Prevalence** — **1–4%**

**Causes** — mostly **sporadic**; maternal **diabetes, alcohol, valproic acid, hyperthermia** · environmental / vascular disruption

### Genes & inheritance

**Familial inheritance** — **not** associated → "deformity NOT familial" = congenital scoliosis (e.g. child with myelomeningocele) · genetics uncertain\
✗ Wrong options: unlike AIS

**Jarcho-Levin (spondylocostal dysostosis)** — most commonly **autosomal recessive**

### Associated anomalies

| System | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Any systemic anomaly** | **Up to 60–61%** |
| **Cardiac** | **10%** (Orthobullets) · **25–26%** (OB) — **VSD** commonest, then ASD, PDA, ToF, TGA |
| **Genitourinary** | **25%** (Orthobullets) · 20% / up to one-third (OB) — unilateral absent kidney, horseshoe, obstructive uropathy, duplicate ureters, hypospadias |
| **Intraspinal (neural axis)** | **20–40%** (Orthobullets) · 37% (OB) · 18% pre-MRI era — diastematomyelia, **tethered cord**, syrinx, **Chiari**, intradural lipoma, dural bands; ↑ with segmentation + mixed defects |
| Orthopaedic | Clubfoot, DDH, limb hypoplasia, **Sprengel**, Klippel-Feil, vertical talus, cavus, **thumb hypoplasia / radial club hand** |

| Syndrome | Features |
|---|---|
| **VACTERL** (38–55%) | Vertebral, anal atresia, cardiac, TE fistula, renal, radial / limb |
| **Goldenhar** (oculo-auriculo-vertebral) | **Hemifacial microsomia + epibulbar dermoids** |
| **Jarcho-Levin** | **Short-trunk dwarfism, multiple vertebral + rib fusions** → **thoracic insufficiency syndrome** |
| **Klippel-Feil** | Short neck, low hairline, cervical fusion |
| **Alagille** | Peripheral pulmonic stenosis, cholestasis, facial dysmorphism |

**Not associated** — Sever's disease (calcaneal apophysitis)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification

| Category | Type | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Failure of formation** | **Fully segmented hemivertebra** | Disc + growth plate above and below → progresses; **commonest anomaly (38%)** (TO) |
| | Semisegmented | Fused one side |
| | Unsegmented | Fused both sides |
| | **Incarcerated** | Within margins of adjacent vertebrae → little progression |
| | Unincarcerated | Laterally placed |
| | Wedge / butterfly | — |
| **Failure of segmentation** | **Block vertebra** | **Least progression (< 2°/yr)** |
| | **Unilateral unsegmented bar** | Common, likely to progress |
| **Mixed** | **Unilateral bar + contralateral hemivertebra** | **Most rapid progression** |

![Hemivertebra with contralateral unsegmented bar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2060-hemi-with-contralateral-bar-moved.jpg)

### Progression risk

| Anomaly (fastest → slowest) | Rate |
|---|---|
| **1 Unilateral bar + contralateral (convex) hemivertebra** | **5–10°/yr** (Orthobullets) · **6–7°/yr**, most > 50° by age 2 (TO) |
| 2 Unilateral unsegmented bar | \~5°/yr; > 50° by 10, > 70° at maturity |
| (Double convex hemivertebra) | 3–4°/yr (TO) |
| 3 Fully segmented hemivertebra | 1–2°/yr |
| 4 Unincarcerated hemivertebra | — |
| 5 Incarcerated hemivertebra | — |
| 6 Unsegmented hemivertebra | — |
| 7 Block vertebra | **< 2°/yr** |

**Timing** — fastest in **first 3 years** · **fused ribs** ↑ progression

**Anterior failure of formation (kyphosis)** — **progressive sagittal deformity + paralysis** · anterior failure of segmentation → progressive but **rarely paralysis**

### Key radiology findings & work-up

| Study | Purpose |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral X-ray | Usually diagnostic |
| **3D CT** | Posterior anatomy, surgical planning (judicious radiation) |
| **MRI whole spine** | **All before surgery**; immediately if **hairy patch / neuro signs** (sedation in infants → may defer if no surgery planned) |
| **Renal US (or MRI)** | **All patients** — "renal US indicated in?" → **congenital scoliosis** |
| **Echocardiogram** | Cardiac evaluation |

**New congenital scoliosis in infant** — **echo + renal US (+ spinal MRI)** first (✗ Wrong options: HLA-B27 · bone scan · skeletal survey · EMG · brace · surgery yet) · MRI already normal → **renal US** next (✗ Wrong options: blood cultures · flex-ext C-spine · platelets)

## 5 Management

**Observation** — non-progressive: incarcerated, non-segmented, some partially segmented hemivertebrae; serial X-rays every 4–6 months; **document progression before surgery**

**Bracing** — **NOT effective** for congenital curves; may control supple compensatory curves · derotational casting may delay growing constructs\
✗ Wrong options: primary treatment

```
Congenital scoliosis
├─ Unilateral bar + contralateral hemivertebra → SURGERY AT ANY AGE
│      (hemivertebra excision + short-segment posterior fusion)
├─ Progressive fully segmented hemivertebra, young → HEMIVERTEBRECTOMY + SHORT FUSION
├─ Unilateral bar, minimal deformity → IN-SITU FUSION
└─ Multiple fused ribs (> 4) / thoracic insufficiency → VEPTR / rib osteotomies / growing rods
```

### Operative criteria & procedures

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Hemivertebra excision + short posterior fusion** | Progressive hemivertebra (esp. **lumbosacral** → oblique takeoff, truncal imbalance); best **< 5–6 yrs, flexible < 40°** (Orthobullets) · ages 3–8 (posterior; younger = poor anchors) · indication = **documented progression** (✗ Wrong options: failed bracing · Cobb > 45° · Risser 4 · maturity) |
| **In-situ arthrodesis (A/P or posterior)** | Unilateral bars with minimal deformity |
| **Posterior fusion ± osteotomy** | Hemivertebra opposite bar not needing vertebrectomy; older patients with progression/deficit/respiratory decline; many pedicle screws ↓ crankshaft |
| **A/P fusion ± vertebrectomy** | Young (**girls < 10, boys < 12**) with progression, deficit, respiratory decline |
| **Convex hemiepiphysiodesis** | Concave growth intact, **< 5 yrs, < 40–50°**; mixed results |
| **Distraction growing rods** | Control deformity during growth, delay fusion; lengthen \~6-monthly |
| **VEPTR / rib osteotomies** | **> 4 fused ribs**, **thoracic insufficiency syndrome** (may stiffen chest) |
| Spinal column shortening resection | Late, severe, rigid deformity; fixed pelvic obliquity |

**22-month / 14-month with bar + contralateral hemivertebra (\~40°), normal MRI** — **hemivertebrectomy + short-segment posterior instrumented fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: observe · brace · Risser cast · VEPTR · growing rods

**7-yr, hemivertebra curve ↑ 10°** — **hemivertebra excision + limited fusion**

**18-month, lumbar hemivertebra** — echo + renal US first; no long TL fusion (crankshaft, limits growth)

### Congenital kyphosis

**Level (TO)** — **T7–T9** (gibbus) · associations: MPS, SED congenita, Morquio, pseudoachondroplasia

| Type | Prognosis |
|---|---|
| **I – failure of formation (anterior)** | **Worst**; relentless progression → **paraplegia** |
| II – failure of segmentation | Rounded, slower, paraplegia uncommon |

**Treatment (TO)** — **no bracing** · **< 5 yrs + < 50–55° → posterior in-situ fusion** (anterior growth continues → some correction) · **> 5 yrs, > 55°, or neuro deficit → anterior release/decompression + strut + posterior instrumented fusion** (neuromonitoring)

**Supports early in-situ posterior fusion (8-month)** — 50° curve, failure of formation, bar, neuro weakness \
✗ Wrong options: **block vertebra** (EXCEPT — least progression)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Note |
|---|---|
| **Crankshaft** | Posterior fusion alone in young |
| **Short stature** | Fusion limits trunk growth (younger worse) |
| **Neurologic injury** | Overdistraction / shortening, overcorrection, segmental vessel ligation → SSEP + MEP |
| Soft tissue | Optimise nutrition pre-op |

## X vs Y

| | Congenital | Idiopathic (AIS) |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | Vertebral anomaly (4–6 wks) | Unknown, familial |
| Inheritance | No | Yes |
| Brace | Ineffective | Effective 25–45° |
| Work-up | Echo + renal US + MRI | MRI only if atypical |

| | Anterior failure of formation | Anterior failure of segmentation |
|---|---|---|
| Deformity | Sharp kyphosis | Rounded kyphosis |
| Paralysis | **Often** | Rare |

## How it's asked

**"2-yr-old with hemivertebrae — additional work-up?"** → **echocardiography**

**"Renal ultrasound indicated in?"** → **congenital scoliosis**

**"LEAST likely association?"** → **Sever's disease**

**"Infant with congenital scoliosis + hairy patch, one kidney?"** → **MRI of entire spine**

**"3-month-old, MRI normal — next?"** → **cardiac + renal evaluation**

**"Screening tests with congenital vertebral anomalies?"** → **renal US + echo + spinal MRI**

**"2-yr, progressed 15°, MRI normal — additional study?"** → **renal ultrasound**

**"18-month, new congenital scoliosis — next step?"** → **echocardiogram + renal US**

**"Most progressive anomaly?"** → **unilateral bar with convex hemivertebra** (TO)

**"Worst prognosis?"** → **hemivertebra with contralateral bar** (TO)

**"Pattern with worst prognosis, surgical indication?"** → **hemivertebra + contralateral bar**

**"Anterior failure of formation leads to?"** → **progressive sagittal deformity + paralysis**

**"When to resect a hemivertebra (4-yr)?"** → **on deformity progression**

**"8-month congenital kyphosis — EXCEPT for early fusion?"** → **block vertebra**

**"14-month, 40°, convex hemivertebra + concave bar?"** → **hemivertebra excision + short posterior fusion**

**"7-yr hemivertebra curve ↑ 10°?"** → **hemivertebra excision + limited fusion**

**"22-month, bar + hemivertebra, 41°?"** → **hemivertebrectomy + short-segment fusion**

**"Deformity NOT familial?"** → **congenital scoliosis**

**"Commonest level of congenital kyphosis?"** → **T7–9** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Degenerative Spondylolisthesis

*Spine · 23 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — anterior slip with an **intact pars** (Wiltse **type III**) (TO)

**Prevalence** — ~5% men, ~9% women (5–9%)

**Demographics** — **women > 40** (**~8×**; 5–10× in OB), **African Americans**, **diabetics**; hormonal ligamentous laxity

**Level** — **L4-5** (5× other levels)\
✗ Wrong options: L5-S1 = isthmic

**Risk factors** — **sacralisation of L5** (transitional L5) · **sagittally oriented facets**

### Pathoanatomy

**Cascade** — disc degeneration → facet capsule degeneration → microinstability → macroinstability → anterolisthesis (worse with congenitally sagittal facets)

**Grade** — rarely > Meyerding II (posterior arch intact → canal narrows as body slides)

### Wiltse classification (TO)

| Type | Name |
|---|---|
| I | Dysplastic (no pars defect) |
| II | Isthmic (lytic, elongated, acute pars) |
| **III** | **Degenerative** |
| IV | Traumatic (non-pars arch fracture) |
| V | Pathologic (e.g. OI) |
| VI | Iatrogenic (added later) |

## 2 Mechanism

| Stenosis | L4-5 slip compresses | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| **Central + lateral recess** | **Traversing L5** | Slip, **LF hypertrophy**, facet osteophytes |
| **Foraminal** | **Exiting L4** | Vertical: disc height loss + posterolateral body osteophyte pushes root against pedicle · AP: superior facet + posterior body changes |

## 3 Presentation

**Back pain** — **commonest**; mechanical, relieved by rest/sitting · **start-up pain** (sit → stand) suggests instability

**Neurogenic claudication** — 2nd commonest: buttock/leg pain with walking upright, **relieved by sitting / flexing** (shopping cart), **not by standing still** · stationary bike tolerated

**L4** — quadriceps (sit-to-stand), ↓ patellar reflex, dorsiflexion (heel walk)

**L5** — dorsiflexion (heel walk), **EHL**, gluteus medius (hip abduction) · EHL weakness = commonest documented deficit (L5)

**Hamstring tightness** — common; separate from neurogenic pain · cauda equina very rare

### Neurogenic vs vascular claudication

| | Neurogenic | Vascular |
|---|---|---|
| Relief | **Sitting / flexion** | **Standing still** |
| Stationary bike | **Tolerated** | Painful |
| Pulses / ABI | Normal (**ABI > 0.9**) | ↓ |
| Walking test | Stop + stand → no relief; sit → relief | Stop + stand → relief |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **Standing lateral + flexion-extension** | Slip; **instability = 4 mm translation or 10° angulation** vs adjacent segment |
| **MRI (T2 sagittal + axial)** | **Persistent leg pain after failed non-op**; neural compression |
| CT | Bony pathology; **most reliable for pseudarthrosis** after fusion |
| **CT myelogram** | MRI contraindicated (**pacemaker**) |

![Degenerative L4-5 spondylolisthesis with stenosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2039-illustration-with-correction-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Non-op (most)** — activity restriction, NSAIDs, PT · **ESI second-line**

### Operative criteria

| Indication for surgery |
|---|
| **Persistent incapacitating pain after 6 months** non-op **incl. ESIs** (commonest) |
| **Progressive motor deficit** |
| Cauda equina syndrome |
| **Active smoker → smoking cessation first** (continue PT) |

```
Symptomatic L4-5 DS failing 6 months non-op
├─ Standard → DECOMPRESSION + INSTRUMENTED POSTEROLATERAL FUSION (± TLIF/PLIF)
├─ Medically frail, can't tolerate fusion → DECOMPRESSION ALONE
│      (~31% progressive instability)
└─ Revision pseudarthrosis → ALIF
```

**SPORT** — surgery → **greater pain relief + better function at 4 yrs** vs non-op

**Herkowitz & Kurz** — decompression + fusion **90% good/excellent** vs **44%** decompression alone · satisfaction ~79% with fusion vs ~69% decompression alone

**Not appropriate** — decompression alone (iatrogenic instability, ~30% progress) · microdiscectomy · TDR (doesn't address facet pain generator) · stand-alone ALIF (suboptimal fusion, cage extrusion; doesn't decompress) · fusion alone (must decompress lateral recess + foramen) · uninstrumented fusion (↑ nonunion)

### Fusion technique & outcomes

**Pedicle screws** — **↓ pseudarthrosis** (↑ fusion rate); outcome benefit inferred, not directly proven · slip reduction: limited role in adults, not correlated with outcome

**Solid fusion** — **most important for long-term relief** of back + leg pain (short-term relief similar with pseudarthrosis)\
✗ Wrong options: interbody device · rhBMP-2 · preserving facet capsules

**Interbody cage (TLIF/PLIF)** — **↑ hospital cost** without ↑ fusion, ODI, SF-36 or LOS (OB) · "may ↑ fusion + ↓ slip progression" (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Cortical bone trajectory (CBT) screws** — start **more medial + caudal**, aim **cephalad + lateral**, smaller screws · ↓ blood loss, smaller incision, ↓ 1-wk pain; fusion + function = pedicle screws; usually with PLIF/TLIF · e.g. single-level PLIF with **local autograft + CBT screws**

**Fusion after laminectomy (no DS/scoliosis)** — if destabilised: **pars fractured** or whole facet removed\
✗ Wrong options: dural tear repair · 2 levels · smoker · < half facets removed

### Smoking

**Smoking** — ↑ pseudarthrosis, infection, reoperation; **↓ functional outcome scores**\
✗ Wrong options: LOS · airway after corpectomy · haematoma

**Post-op cessation ≥ 6 months** → ↑ fusion rate vs continued smoking, but risk **stays above non-smokers** whether quitting pre- or post-op · pre-op cessation ≥ 4 wks ↓ wound complications · multilevel fusion ↑ nonunion risk in smokers

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Pseudarthrosis** | **5–30%**; **CT** best (✗ Wrong options: MRI · flex-ext (miss 60–80%) · bone scan · ESR/CRP) |
| **Adjacent segment disease** | 30–40% radiographic (Orthobullets) · **20–29% need surgery at 10 yrs** (18% at 8 yrs SPORT) · ↑ with **laminectomy at cranial adjacent level** (✗ Wrong options: 1-level fusion · DS itself · obesity) |
| **SSI** | **0.1–2%**; **I&D + retain hardware** (stability aids eradication) (✗ Wrong options: aspiration · antibiotics alone · remove hardware) |
| SSI risk factors | **Age > 60–65**, **diabetes**, **smoking**, ↑ BMI, alcohol, blood loss, operative time, levels, **staged A/P**, prior SSI (✗ Wrong options: **previous spine surgery** (EXCEPT)) |
| **LFCN palsy** | **Prone iliac bolster** → lateral thigh numbness (meralgia paraesthetica) |
| Ulnar nerve / brachial plexopathy | Arm positioning |
| Dural tear | — |
| **In-hospital complications** | **Age** (strongest), ≥ 3 comorbidities, blood loss, operative time, levels fused (✗ Wrong options: gender, race, hospital size) |

**Adjacent-level symptoms not matching MRI (vague radicular pain, L5/S1 weakness, L3-4 degeneration)** — **EMG** before surgery

## X vs Y

| | Degenerative | Isthmic |
|---|---|---|
| Level | **L4-5** | L5-S1 |
| Pars | **Intact** | Defect |
| Sex | **Female** | Male |
| Root | **Traversing L5** (+ exiting L4 foraminal) | Exiting L5 |
| Canal | **Narrowed** | Widened |
| Grade | ≤ II | Can be high |

## How it's asked

**"L4/5 DS, failed 6 months incl. ESIs — next?"** → **decompression + fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: TDR · microdiscectomy · decompression alone · fusion alone

**"Failed non-op, flexion-extension + MRI DS?"** → **laminectomy + partial facetectomy + foraminotomy + instrumented PSF**

**"DS with EHL weakness, failed ESIs?"** → **decompressive laminectomy + posterior instrumented fusion**

**"Pacemaker, CT myelogram DS, ABI 0.95, bikes painlessly?"** → **decompression + instrumented fusion**

**"70-yr, trouble heel-walking, failed non-op?"** → **laminectomy + instrumented fusion**

**"SPORT — surgery vs non-op at 4 yrs?"** → **greater pain relief + improved function**

**"Greatest effect on pain, function, ODI (4 mm translation)?"** → **decompression + instrumented fusion**

**"Laminectomy for stenosis — fuse if?"** → **pars fractured**

**"Best long-term improvement?"** → **open laminectomy + instrumented fusion**

**"Pedicle screws in posterolateral fusion?"** → **↓ pseudarthrosis**

**"Most important for long-term relief?"** → **solid L4/5 fusion**

**"Fusion vs pseudarthrosis outcome?"** → **better long-term back + leg pain relief**

**"Interbody spacer adds?"** → **increased hospital cost**

**"↓ blood loss + incision, high fusion?"** → **PLIF with local autograft + cortical trajectory screws**

**"Smoker quits post-op — nonunion risk?"** → **still higher than non-smokers**

**"Smoking associated with?"** → **lower functional scores**

**"Post-op cessation needed to ↑ fusion?"** → **≥ 6 months**

**"Heavy smoker, failed 8 months non-op, no deficit?"** → **continue PT + smoking cessation**

**"Six-week wound erythema, ↑ ESR, dorsal collection?"** → **debridement, delayed closure, retain hardware**

**"SSI risk factors EXCEPT?"** → **previous spine surgery**

**"Recurrent pain 1 yr after fusion, normal labs — confirm diagnosis?"** → **CT lumbar spine**

**"Complication risk factors?"** → **age + blood loss + levels fused (all)**

**"Lateral thigh numbness after prone fusion?"** → **positioning (LFCN)**

**"Highest in-hospital complication risk?"** → **age**

**"Highest risk of ASD needing surgery?"** → **laminectomy at cranial adjacent level**

**"Need for adjacent-level surgery at 10 yrs?"** → **20–29%**

**"Expected weakness (L4-5 DS)?"** → **EHL**

**"Vague radicular pain, symptoms don't match adjacent-level MRI?"** → **EMG**

**"Wiltse type 3?"** → **degenerative** (TO)

# Disc Space Infection - Pediatric

*Spine · 24 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — children > adults · **males** · usually **< 5 yrs**

**Where** — **lumbar spine (50–60%)**

### Pathophysiology

**Why children** — vessels run from **cartilaginous endplate into nucleus pulposus** → direct haematogenous seeding of disc → spreads to endplate/body · **adults**: vessels reach **annulus only** → isolated discitis rare

| Organism | Note |
|---|---|
| **Staph aureus** | **> 80%** |
| **TB** | **Always consider** — esp. not improving on first-line antibiotics |
| **Salmonella** | **Sickle cell** |

## 3 Presentation

**Toddler** — **limp / refuses to sit or walk**, **loss of appetite**, abdominal pain, fever only **25%**

**Older child** — back pain with **point tenderness**, ↓ ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| ESR, CRP, WBC | **High-normal or mildly ↑**; WBC normal in \~45% |
| **CRP** | **Most sensitive for monitoring treatment** (half-life **19 h**) (✗ Wrong options: ESR (3 wks to normalise) · WBC · blood cultures · procalcitonin) |
| Blood cultures | Identify organism (often negative) |

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — unreliable; earliest changes at **\~1 week**

| Order | Finding | Timing |
|---|---|---|
| **1 (earliest)** | **Loss of lumbar lordosis** | Before bony change |
| 2 | **Disc space narrowing** | **10–21 days** (Orthobullets) · from 1 wk (OB) |
| 3 | **Endplate erosion** | 10–21 days / 1–3 wks |
| Later | "Sawtooth" erosion 3–4 wks · endplate scalloping | Long-standing |
| Healed | **Vertebra magna**, permanent height loss, **block vertebra** (spontaneous fusion) | Resolved |

**MRI** — **diagnostic test of choice**

![Paediatric discitis on T2 MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2028-pediatric-diskitis-t2-mri.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Paediatric discitis
├─ Early, no abscess / thecal sac displacement → ADMIT: BED REST + IV ANTI-STAPH ABX
│      (7–10 d IV → total 4–6 wks; ± immobilisation; follow CRP)
├─ No response at 7–10 d → CT-GUIDED BIOPSY (rule out TB)
└─ Late / abscess + deficit / failed non-op → SURGICAL DEBRIDEMENT + ABX + BRACE
```

**Bacteraemic 5-yr, no collection** — **admit, bed rest + IV antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: oral antibiotics at home · surgery · cast alone (slow, recurrent)

**IV antibiotics** — speed resolution + ↓ recurrence

## 6 Complications

**Long-term** — disc space narrowing, **interbody fusion**, back pain

## X vs Y

| | Paediatric discitis | Spondylolysis |
|---|---|---|
| Labs | ↑ ESR/CRP | Normal |
| Exam | Pain flexion + extension, refuses to walk | Pain with **extension** (gymnast) |
| Rx | **IV antibiotics** | Activity restriction ± brace |

## How it's asked

**"Disc space infection in children — all true EXCEPT?"** → **loss of disc height is the earliest sign** (TO) — earliest = loss of lordosis

**"Order of radiographic findings?"** → **loss of lordosis → disc narrowing → endplate erosion**

**"6-yr gymnast, ↑ ESR/CRP, discitis on MRI — true?"** → **IV antibiotics usually resolve it**\
✗ Wrong options: urgent debridement · bracing first line (spondylolysis)

**"Best marker to monitor antibiotic response?"** → **CRP**

**"5-yr, gram-positive bacteraemia, no collection?"** → **admit for bed rest + IV antibiotics**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Discogenic Back Pain

*Spine · 25 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **axial** low back pain from disc degeneration, **no radicular symptoms**

**Biology** — disc characteristics modified by **bone morphogenetic proteins**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain ↑ with** — **bending, sitting, axial loading** · flexion aggravates, extension relieves

**Exam** — **SLR / nerve tension negative**, neuro normal

**Acute lumbago (3 wks), no red flags** — **PT + NSAIDs**, no imaging · red flags: diabetes / IVDU (infection), cancer, smoking, **night pain**\
✗ Wrong options: X-ray · MRI · discography · steroid taper

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **Best initial study** — degeneration, **vacuum phenomenon**; excludes other causes |
| MRI | Degenerated discs **without** significant stenosis / herniation |
| **Provocative discography** | Primary confirmatory test but **accelerates degeneration**: ↑ herniation, ↓ disc height, **reactive endplate changes** (✗ Wrong options: myelogram · TFESI · spinal anaesthesia) |

### Diagnostic criteria – positive discogram

| Criterion |
|---|
| **Concordant pain** on injection |
| **Abnormal disc morphology** on fluoroscopy / post-discography CT |
| **Negative (painless) control level(s)** |

## 5 Management

**First line (majority)** — NSAIDs, PT, **cognitive therapy**, lifestyle change

**Fusion vs cognitive-exercise therapy** — **equivalent ODI improvement** at **1 + 10 yrs**\
✗ Wrong options: fusion superior · therapy superior

**Fusion** — controversial; **poor results when based on positive discography**

### Lumbar total disc replacement (TDR)

**Indication** — **single-level DDD with disease-free facets** (controversial)

| TDR vs fusion / ALIF | Finding |
|---|---|
| Safety + efficacy at 2 yrs | **Comparable / better** |
| Hospital stay | **Shorter** |
| Reoperation | **Lower** |
| Disc height restoration | **Greater** |
| Subsidence | **Less** |
| Adjacent segment disease | **Lower** |

**Contraindications** — pars defect, fracture, **facet arthrosis**, **spondylolisthesis**, posterior element insufficiency, prior laminectomy, scoliosis, instability

**Metal-on-metal TDR** — **↑ serum cobalt + chromium, like MoM THA**; persist; renally excreted\
✗ Wrong options: transient · only cobalt · hepatic neutralisation · negligible

```
Failed lumbar TDR
├─ Asymptomatic subsidence, no extrusion → OBSERVE
├─ Facet pain / micro-instability, implant well placed → POSTERIOR STABILISATION
└─ Poly dislocation / malposition → REVISION ARTHROPLASTY or ARTHRODESIS VIA RETROPERITONEAL APPROACH
       (not far-lateral — iliac crest blocks L5-S1)
```

## X vs Y

| | Discogenic pain | Radiculopathy |
|---|---|---|
| Pain | Axial | Leg > back |
| SLR | Negative | Positive |
| Worse with | Sitting, bending, loading | Sitting/cough (HNP) |

## How it's asked

**"3 weeks of lumbago, no red flags?"** → **PT + NSAIDs**

**"Best initial study for discogenic pain?"** → **radiography**

**"MoM vs poly TDR — labs?"** → **↑ cobalt + chromium like MoM THA**

**"TDR vs fusion for single-level DDD?"** → **comparable safety + efficacy at 2 yrs**

**"Anterior poly dislocation 6 wks after TDR?"** → **revision to arthrodesis via retroperitoneal approach**

**"Fusion vs cognitive/exercise therapy?"** → **equivalent improvement**

**"Procedure accelerating disc degeneration?"** → **provocative discography**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# DISH (Diffuse Idiopathic Skeletal Hyperostosis)

*Spine · 26 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Also** — **Forestier disease**; **enthesopathy** of spine + extremities; ossification of the **anterior longitudinal ligament** (TO: non-inflammatory, "degenerative")

**Incidence** — **6–12%** overall · uncommon < 50 · > 50: 25% M / 15% F · > 80: 28% M / 26% F · non-diabetics 1.6–13% vs **diabetics 13–49%**

**Race** — commonest in **Caucasians**; less in Black, Native American, Asian\
✗ Wrong options: "most common in African Americans" (EXCEPT answer)

**Location** — **thoracic (right side) > cervical > lumbar** · thoracic **T7–T11**, often isolated · **right-sided thoracic** because the **pulsatile aorta protects the left** · cervical + lumbar **symmetric**

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Fact |
|---|---|
| **HLA-B8** | Associated (esp. DISH + diabetes) |
| HLA-B27 | **Not** associated (✗ Wrong options: "90% B27 positive") |
| **↑ HbA1c / diabetes** | Image showing DISH → elevated HbA1c |
| **↑ Growth hormone + IGF-1** | ✓ |
| ESR | Normal |

**Risk factors** — **gout, hyperlipidaemia, diabetes**, coronary artery disease

### Associated conditions

**Lumbar** — **spinal stenosis**

**Cervical (large anterior osteophytes)** — **dysphagia**, stridor, hoarseness, **sleep apnoea**, difficult intubation, **myelopathy**

**Fracture** — ankylosed segments → **long lever arms**; **hyperextension** injuries; low-energy falls → unstable fractures

**Extra-spinal** — elbow and other entheses · **heterotopic ossification after THA 30–50%** (vs < 20% without DISH)

## 3 Presentation

**Often asymptomatic** (incidental)

**Thoracolumbar** — **mild chronic back pain** (ankylosis stabilises) + **stiffness worse in morning + cold weather**

**Cervical** — pain, stiffness, **dysphagia** (food "stuck"), stridor, hoarseness, sleep apnoea

**Exam** — ↓ spinal ROM; myelopathy / stenosis signs

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

| Criterion (Resnick) |
|---|
| **Flowing ossification along anterolateral aspect of ≥ 4 contiguous vertebrae** (= **non-marginal syndesmophytes at 3 successive levels**) |
| **Preserved disc height**; little degenerative change (no marginal sclerosis, no vacuum) |
| **No facet ankylosis**; **no SI joint erosion / sclerosis / fusion** |

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **"Flowing candle wax"** non-marginal ossification; thoracic **right-sided**; **lateral C-spine**: anterior bone with **preserved disc space** (best view to separate from AS) |
| Bone scan | ↑ uptake — may **mimic metastases** |
| **CT** | **Any neck pain after trauma** → rule out occult fracture |
| MRI | Ligament / cord injury |

![DISH: flowing anterior ossification with preserved discs](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2045-xray-cervical-lateral-shows-dish-google.jpg)

**Dysphagia + neck stiffness in a diabetic** — **X-rays of cervical, thoracic, lumbar spine** first\
✗ Wrong options: HLA-B27 · MRI first · ESI · inflammatory labs

### Osteophyte vs syndesmophyte vs DISH (TO)

| Structure | Origin / orientation |
|---|---|
| **Traction osteophyte** | Small, horizontal, parallel to endplate, few mm from margin (Sharpey fibres) |
| **Claw osteophyte** | Larger, curved, may bridge — progression of traction osteophyte in degenerated disc (TO image answer) |
| **Syndesmophyte (AS)** | Ossified **annulus**, **vertical**, at the **endplate margin** (marginal) |
| **DISH** | Ossified **ALL**, undulating, away from endplate margin (non-marginal) |

**Osteophytes** — 80% men / 60% women by 50, 95% by 70; most at **L3-4 + T9-10**

## 5 Management

**Non-op (most)** — activity modification, PT, brace, NSAIDs, **bisphosphonates**

**Cervical traction (fracture)** — caution: **over-distraction** (compromised ligaments)

**Surgery** — for sequelae: **lumbar stenosis, cervical myelopathy, adult spinal deformity**

### Fractures – CT & long posterior fusion

```
DISH + trauma (even standing-height fall)
├─ Neck pain → CT C-SPINE (not soft/hard collar + discharge or flex-ext)
└─ Fracture into posterior elements → LONG POSTERIOR FUSION
       (≥ 3 levels above + 3 below, e.g. C6 → C3-T2)
       (not C5-7 short, anterior alone — failure, or halo / collar — ↑ mortality)
```

## 6 Complications

**Cervical trauma mortality** — **15% operative vs 67% non-operative** · ↑ morbidity + mortality vs non-ankylosed spines · vs AS: "higher" / "similar" (Orthobullets) · **equivalent** (OB) — read the options

**Neurologic injury with trauma** — **AS > DISH** (67% vs 40%); AS also more surgery + epidural haematoma

**HO after THA** — **30–50%**

## X vs Y

| | DISH | Ankylosing spondylitis |
|---|---|---|
| Syndesmophytes | **Non-marginal**, flowing candle wax | **Marginal**, bamboo spine, Romanus shiny corners |
| Disc space | **Preserved** | Ossified (cervical) |
| Bone density | Normal / ↑ | **Osteopenia** |
| HLA | **B8** | **B27** |
| Age | > 50 | Young (15–40) |
| SI joints | **Spared** (abnormality excludes DISH) | **Bilateral sacroiliitis** |
| Facets | Spared | Ankylosed |
| Diabetes | **Yes** | No |
| ESR | Normal | ↑ |
| Thoracic | **Right-sided** | Whole spine |
| Trauma neuro injury | Lower | **Higher** |

## How it's asked

**"50-yr, neck stiffness, vertigo — lateral C-spine flowing ossification?"** → **DISH** (TO)

**"Marked structures on X-ray/CT (bridging curved spurs)?"** → **claw osteophytes** (TO)

**"67-yr neck + back pain, anterior flowing ossification?"** → **DISH**\
✗ Wrong options: OPLL · RA · AS · OA

**"Neck stiffness + dysphagia, lateral X-ray?"** → **DISH**

**"DISH — NOT true?"** → **most common in African Americans**

**"Which image → elevated HbA1c?"** → **DISH**

**"AS vs DISH — true?"** → **AS has higher neuro injury rate with trauma**

**"Diabetic, dysphagia, stiff neck — next?"** → **X-rays of cervical, thoracic + lumbar spine**

**"Fracture in DISH — true?"** → **↑ morbidity + mortality**

**"80-yr, fall, DISH, mild neck pain?"** → **CT cervical spine**

**"C6 fracture into posterior elements with DISH?"** → **posterior fusion C3-T2**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Halo Orthosis Immobilization

*Spine · 27 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Biomechanics

**Principle** — fixes **skull to torso** → **most rigid external cervical immobilisation** (TO)

**Best controls** — **upper cervical** motion: **rotation at C1-2** / occipitocervical junction\
✗ Wrong options: subaxial flex-ext, rotation, lateral bend, CT junction

**Weakness** — **intercalated paradoxical motion** in subaxial spine; **lateral bending least controlled** → **"snaking"**: recumbent lateral shows mid-cervical **kyphosis**, upright shows lordosis → not ideal for **lower cervical** injuries (incl. facet fracture-dislocation — fails)

## 5 Management

### Indications

| Adult | Paediatric |
|---|---|
| **Occipital condyle fracture** | **Atlanto-occipital dissociation** |
| Occipitocervical dislocation | **Jefferson / atlas fractures** |
| **Stable Jefferson (type II atlas)** | **Unstable odontoid fractures** |
| **Type II odontoid in young** | **Persistent AARS** |
| **Type II / IIA hangman** | C1-2 dissociation; subaxial trauma |
| Post-op adjunct | **Pre-op traction** for deformity (halo-gravity) |

### Contraindications

| Absolute | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Cranial fracture** | Polytrauma, severe chest trauma, barrel chest, obesity |
| **Infection** | **Advanced age** — mortality **21% if > 79 yrs** (elderly → avoid halo) |
| Severe soft-tissue injury at pin sites | — |

**Pre-application CT** — suspected **cranial fracture** · **children < 10 yrs** (bone thickness, avoid **sutures**)\
✗ Wrong options: skull X-ray

### Pin technique

| | Adult | Child |
|---|---|---|
| **Pins** | **4** (2 anterior, 2 posterior diagonally opposite) | **6–8** (Orthobullets) · **8–12** (OB) — **more pins** |
| **Torque** | **8 in-lb** (Orthobullets, OB); 6–8 in-lb range | **2–4 in-lb** ("finger-tight") · **1–5 in-lb** (OB) — **lower torque** (✗ Wrong options: ft-lb · maximal hand tightening) |
| Vest | Standard | Custom vest > 2 yrs · **Minerva cast < 2 yrs** |
| Retighten | Day 2 | — |

**Anterior safe zone** — **1 cm above the orbital rim**, over the **lateral third** of the orbit (Orthobullets) · "lateral two-thirds", ≥ 4.5 cm from midline (OB) — **at/below skull equator** · **lateral to supraorbital nerve**, anterior + medial to **temporalis**

**Ring** — below widest part of skull, \~1 cm above ear helix · pins perpendicular to cortex

```
Anterior pin position
├─ Too MEDIAL → SUPRAORBITAL + SUPRATROCHLEAR NERVE injury, frontal sinus
│      (forehead / upper eyelid numbness)
├─ Correct (1 cm above lateral third, below equator) → NO NEURO COMPLICATION
└─ Too LATERAL (temporal fossa) → THIN BONE + TEMPORALIS → jaw pain, chewing pain, infection
```

![Halo anterior pin safe zone and nerves](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2019-safe-zones-showing-nerves-moved.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Overall** — **children \~70% (68%)** vs **adults \~35% (36%)**

| Complication | Rate / management |
|---|---|
| **Pin loosening** | **36% — commonest in adults**; retighten; persistent → exchange |
| **Pin-site infection** | **12–20%**; esp. posterior pin in **temporalis fossa**; **not loose → keep pin + oral antibiotics + local care** (✗ Wrong options: remove · tighten) · loose / deep → remove + new site, IV abx, drain abscess |
| Discomfort | 18%; release skin around pin |
| Dural puncture | 1%; meningitis if inner table breached |
| **Abducens (CN VI) palsy** | **Commonest cranial nerve** (✗ Wrong options: oculomotor · trochlear) — **traction injury**, not pin; diplopia + **loss of lateral gaze**; ↓ traction + **observe** (resolves) |
| Supraorbital / supratrochlear palsy | **Medial anterior pins** |
| Medical | Pneumonia, ARDS, arrhythmia; ↑ mortality elderly |

![Abducens palsy: loss of lateral gaze](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2019-abducens-clinical.jpg)

## X vs Y

| Nerve | Cause | Deficit |
|---|---|---|
| **Supraorbital** | Medial anterior pin | Upper eyelid / forehead / scalp numbness |
| **Supratrochlear** | More medial pin | Medial forehead + medial eyelid numbness |
| **Abducens (VI)** | **Traction** | Diplopia, ↓ lateral gaze |

## How it's asked

**"Anterior pins too medial — nerve at risk?"** → **supraorbital nerve**

**"Region for anterior pin?"** → **1 cm above lateral orbit, below equator**

**"Red (medial) zone — structures at risk?"** → **supraorbital + supratrochlear nerves**
