# Lumbar Spine Anatomy

*Spine · 37 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Alignment

**Lumbar lordosis** — average **60°** (range 20–80°) · apex **L3** · mostly from the **disc spaces**

### Osteology

**Vertebral bodies** — **largest** in the axial spine

| Part | Fact |
|---|---|
| Pedicles | Project posteriorly from posterolateral body corners |
| Laminae | Posteromedial from pedicles, join midline |
| **Mammillary processes** | **Separate ossification centres**; project posteriorly from the **superior articular facet** · pedicle screw landmark |
| **Pars interarticularis** | Bone between superior + inferior facets · **site of spondylolysis** |
| Facet (zygapophyseal) joint | From pedicle–lamina junction · **more coronal moving distally** |

![Facet orientation becomes coronal distally](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2071-facet-orientation.jpg)

### Ligamentum flavum

**Attachments (TO)** — origin **lower half of the anterior surface of the lamina above** · insertion **posterior surface + upper margin of the lamina below** · OB: origin ventral cranial lamina 60–70% of the way up (closer to superior edge), insertion superior edge of caudal lamina — read the options

**MRI** — **low signal on T2** · thickened flavum → stenosis\
✗ Wrong options: synovial cyst and epidural CSF are high signal

### Pedicle anatomy

**Landmarks** — superior-inferior midpoint = **midpoint of transverse process** · medial-lateral midpoint = **lateral border of pars**

**Pedicle screw entry (TO)** — **mammillary process, lateral to pars** · lumbar: intersection of line through lateral facet and line bisecting transverse process, \~1 mm below the facet joint · laterality: **mammillary > intersection > pars** start points

**Danger (TO)** — lumbar root lies in the **upper third** of the foramen, just **inferior to the medial pedicle wall** → **medial or inferior breach more dangerous** than lateral/superior

| Measure | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Medial angulation** | Increases distally: **L1 12° → L5 30° → S1 39°** · largest horizontal angle at L5 (TO) |
| **Smallest lumbar diameter** | **L1** (OB, Orthobullets) |
| Smallest overall | **T4** (Orthobullets) · **T5** horizontal (TO) — read the options |
| Widest | **L5** horizontal; **T11** sagittal · S1 \~19 mm |
| Sagittal angle | Caudal at L5, cephalad L3–T1 (TO) |
| Thoracic pedicle | 62–79% cancellous · **lateral wall thinner than medial** |

![Pedicle diameter by level](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2071-shows-pedicle-diameter.jpg)

### Cortical : cancellous ratio

| Highest → lowest |
|---|
| **Thoracic pedicles** |
| Lumbar pedicles |
| Vertebral bodies |
| **Sacrum** (lowest) |

### Cervical pedicles (TO)

**Size** — **C3 smallest**, C2 + C7 largest · C3–C5 need < 4.5 mm screws

**Angulation** — medial at all levels: **most C5**, least C2 + C7 · upward at C2-3, parallel C4-5, downward C6-7

**Vertebral artery** — at risk **C3–C6** (lateral cortex thinnest; medial cortex \~2× thicker) · less at risk C2 + C7-T1 (outside foramen at C7)

### Neural structures

**Root rule** — exits under the **same-numbered pedicle** · central/paracentral → traversing root · far lateral → exiting root

**Cervical vs lumbar** — cervical C6 under C5 pedicle (mismatch, extra C8 root) · lumbar L5 under L5 (match) · vertical lumbar roots → paracentral + foraminal discs hit **different** roots

**Cauda equina** — begins \~**L1**

### Pain innervation

**Disc** — **sinuvertebral nerve** → **outer annulus only** (nociception + proprioception) · discogenic pain: axial, worse sitting/bending, no radicular signs\
✗ Wrong options: nucleus · DRG direct

**Dura** — intradural communicating nerve (central herniation pain)

**Dorsal ramus branches** — **medial → facet capsule + multifidus** · intermediate → longissimus, spinalis · lateral → iliocostalis + skin

**Facet dual supply** — each facet from the **medial branch of the level above + same level** · **L4-L5 facet block = L3 + L4 medial branches**\
✗ Wrong options: ventral rami · lateral/intermediate branches

**Ventral rami** — anteromedial trunk

### Blood supply

**Segmental arteries** — dorsal branches → dura + posterior elements · tie off for corpectomy

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Lumbopelvic parameters

| Parameter | Definition | Formula |
|---|---|---|
| **Pelvic incidence (PI)** | Line S1 endplate centre → femoral head centre vs **perpendicular to S1 endplate** | **PI = PT + SS** · **fixed, independent of pelvic position** |
| **Pelvic tilt (PT)** | Same line vs **vertical** | PT = PI − SS |
| **Sacral slope (SS)** | S1 endplate vs **horizontal** | SS = PI − PT |

**PI** — correlates with spondylolisthesis severity (**Meyerding grade**)

![PI = PT + SS](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2071-combined-pi-ss-pt-moved.jpg)

### Image-guided interventions

| Procedure | Use |
|---|---|
| **Selective nerve root injection** | Unilateral radicular symptoms · diagnostic + therapeutic · **transforaminal (outside-in)** |
| **Facet injection** | Confirms facet as pain generator · may be therapeutic |
| **Epidural injection** | **Lumbar spinal stenosis** |
| **Discography** | Controversial · **concordant** vs discordant pain · shows annular tears |

**Needle** — 22–25 G, CT or fluoroscopy

## 5 Management

### Surgical approaches

| Approach | Facts |
|---|---|
| Posterior midline | PLIF / TLIF |
| **Wiltse paraspinal** | Far lateral pathology |
| **Retroperitoneal (anterolateral)** | **Aortic bifurcation L4-5** · **superior hypogastric plexus on L5 body → retrograde ejaculation** |
| **Lateral transpsoas** | **Left side** (aorta tolerates retraction) · ideal **L1-2, L2-3, L3-4** · **L4-5** = highest plexus risk (hip flexion + knee extension weakness) · T12-L1 needs rib + diaphragm takedown |

### Transpsoas safe zone

**Cranial → caudal** — **safe zone shrinks because the lumbar plexus moves ventral** · triggered EMG helps\
✗ Wrong options: vessels move; great vessels roughly constant

| Nerve injured | Result |
|---|---|
| **Ilioinguinal + iliohypogastric** | **Delayed (\~2 wks) abdominal wall paresis** — bulge mimics hernia (CT excludes) · groin paraesthesia · resolves with observation |
| Genitofemoral | Genital numbness, **lost cremasteric reflex** |
| Femoral | Knee extension weakness, anterior thigh numbness |
| LFCN | Lateral thigh (aggressive retroperitoneal fat dissection) |
| Obturator | Medial thigh numbness, **adduction weakness** |

**Risk factors** — multilevel, long retraction, excessive bed break, hips not flexed

![Nerves at risk in transpsoas approach](../../MCQ/attachments/q5918-illa-lateral-transpsoas-nerves-labeled.jpg)

### Retroperitoneal relations (TO)

**Anterior to L3 body** — answer not keyed by source (options aorta, ureter, right iliac artery, IVC) · Orthobullets: aorta bifurcates at **L4-5**, so the iliac arteries lie below L3

## X vs Y

| | Cervical root | Lumbar root |
|---|---|---|
| Course | Horizontal | Vertical |
| Pedicle | C6 under C5 (mismatch) | L5 under L5 (match) |
| Central vs foraminal disc | **Same root** | **Different roots** |

## How it's asked

**"Entry point for pedicle screw?"** → **mammillary process, lateral to pars** (TO)

**"T12–L4 fusion — smallest screw diameter?"** → **L1**

**"Highest cortical : cancellous ratio?"** → **thoracic pedicles**

**"Pelvic parameter independent of pelvic position?"** → **PT + SS (= pelvic incidence)**

**"Transpsoas safe zone cranial → caudal?"** → **decreases — lumbar plexus moves ventral**

**"Abdominal bulge 2 wks after L3-5 lateral fusion?"** → **ilioinguinal / iliohypogastric injury**

**"Ligamentum flavum origin and insertion?"** → **lower half anterior surface of lamina above → posterior surface + upper margin of lamina below** (TO)

**"Arrow on T2 axial — thick low-signal band?"** → **ligamentum flavum**

**"Discogenic pain pathway?"** → **sinuvertebral nerve to the annulus fibrosus**

**"Block for L4-L5 facet RFA?"** → **medial branches of L3 + L4 dorsal rami**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neck & Upper Extremity Spine Exam

*Spine · 38 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Root map

| Root | Motion | Muscle | Sensory | Reflex |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C4** | Scapular stabilisation (winging) | Upper serratus (variable) | Upper shoulder, over clavicle | — |
| **C5** | **Shoulder abduction**, elbow flexion (palm up) | **Deltoid**, **biceps** | **Lateral arm** below deltoid | **Biceps** |
| **C6** | Elbow flexion (thumb up), **wrist extension** | **Brachioradialis**, **ECRL** | **Thumb + radial hand/forearm** | **Brachioradialis** |
| **C7** | **Elbow extension**, **wrist flexion** | **Triceps**, **FCR** | **Fingers 2, 3, 4** | **Triceps** |
| **C8** | Finger flexion, **grip**, thumb extension | **FDS** | Finger 5 | — |
| **T1** | Finger abduction | Interossei | Medial elbow | — |

![Upper limb dermatomes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2001-dermatomes-arm-moved.jpg)

### Motor testing

| Motion | Muscle | Nerve | Root |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scapular stabilisation | Serratus | Long thoracic | C4 |
| Shoulder abduction | Deltoid | Axillary | C5 |
| Shoulder IR | Subscapularis | Subscapular | C5 |
| Shoulder ER | Infraspinatus | Suprascapular | C5 |
| Elbow flexion (palm up) | Biceps, brachialis | Musculocutaneous | C5 |
| Elbow flexion (thumb up) | Brachioradialis | Radial | C6 |
| Wrist extension | ECRL | Radial | C6 |
| Supination | Supinator | PIN | C6 |
| Elbow extension | Triceps | Radial | C7 |
| Wrist flexion | FCR, PL | Median | C7 |
| Pronation | PT, PQ | Median | C7 |
| MCP + PIP flexion | FDS | Median | C8 |
| DIP flexion | FDP | Ulnar + AIN | C8 |
| Thumb extension | EPL | PIN | C8 |
| Finger abduction | Interossei | Ulnar | T1 |

### Cervical root anatomy

**Mismatch** — **C7 root exits below C6 pedicle** (C6 under C5) · extra C8 root (no C8 pedicle)

**Horizontal root** — **central and foraminal disc at same level hit the same root** (C6-7 disc → **C7**)

**Commonest degenerative levels** — **C4-5 (C5)** and **C5-6 (C6)**

![Cervical vs lumbar root–pedicle relation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2001-illustration-cervical-vs-4-moved.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Inspection** — sagittal/coronal alignment (kyphosis), scars (ulnar transposition, carpal tunnel), **café-au-lait** (NF), atrophy (deltoid + biceps in palsy)

**Normal cervical ROM** — flexion **50°** · extension **60°** · rotation **80°** · lateral bend **45°**

**Motor / sensory grading** — ASIA 0–5 for one muscle per root C5–T1 · sensory: always **pain (pin) + light touch**; vibration, temperature, 2-point for focused exam

### Gait

| Gait | Cause |
|---|---|
| Antalgic | Hip/knee pathology, severe radicular pain |
| Trendelenburg | Painful hip arthritis, **gluteus medius weakness** |
| **Wide-based shuffling** | **Myelopathy** / neurologic |
| Steppage / lateral swing | **Foot drop** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Provocative tests

| Test | Technique | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|
| **Spurling** | Rotate toward affected side + extend + **axial load**, 30 s → ipsilateral arm pain | **Specific, not sensitive** — acute **radiculopathy** |
| **Hoffmann** | Flick distal phalanx of long finger into extension → **thumb IP flexes** | **Sensitive, not specific** — **myelopathy** |
| **Lhermitte** | Neck flexion/extension → electric shock down spine/limbs | **Specific, not sensitive** — cord compression |

## X vs Y

| Root | Distinguishing finding |
|---|---|
| C5 | Weak shoulder abduction + ER |
| C6 | **Absent brachioradialis reflex**, **thumb numbness** |
| **C7** | **Weak wrist flexion + elbow extension**, middle-finger numbness, ↓ triceps |
| C8 | **↓ Grip** |

## How it's asked

**"Left foraminal C6-7 disc — exam?"** → **weak wrist flexion** (C7)\
✗ Wrong options: absent brachioradialis = C6 · thumb numbness = C6 · shoulder abduction/ER = C5 · grip = C8

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neuromuscular Scoliosis

*Spine · 39 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — spinal curvature from disorders of **brain, spinal cord or muscle**

### Neuromuscular vs idiopathic curves

| Neuromuscular curves | |
|---|---|
| Progression | **More rapid**; **may progress after maturity** |
| Pelvis | **Pelvic obliquity** |
| Length | **Longer**, more vertebrae (may reach cervical) — long C-shaped |
| Surgery | **↑ Pulmonary complications** |

![Long C-shaped neuromuscular curve with pelvic obliquity](../../MCQ/attachments/q5291-illa-367f01-1-moved.gif)

### Paralytic scoliosis after childhood SCI (TO)

**Cervical complete SCI before age 10** — paralytic scoliosis in **virtually 100%** (TO answer: 6-yr with C4-5 fracture-dislocation → **100%**) · risk depends on level + age at injury

## 5 Management

**Outcome** — **spinal fusion is the only treatment with proven benefit on deformity** · carers report excellent QoL improvement

### Classification & treatment

| Category | Disease | Non-op | Operative |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Upper motor neuron** | **Cerebral palsy** | Boston underarm brace until puberty (10–12), wheelchair modification | Group I → **PSF** · Group II → PSF ± ASF + **fusion to pelvis (Luque-Galveston)** · indications: **curve > 50°**, worsening **pelvic obliquity with sitting imbalance** |
| | **Rett syndrome** | Brace C-shaped curves | PSF — thoracic curve affecting sitting/balance, C-curves failing brace |
| **Muscle weakness** | **Spinal muscular atrophy** | Boston brace until puberty | **PSF to pelvis** (wheelchair sitting) · release **hip contractures first** · may transiently lose UE function |
| | **Muscular dystrophy** | **Bracing contraindicated** | PSF ± pelvis (controversial) · **curve > 20°** or progressive · operate early (**20–30°**) **before pulmonary function declines** |
| **Paralytic** | **Spina bifida, SCI** | **Bracing contraindicated** | **ASF + PSF** with pelvic fixation (**anterior fusion needed — deficient posterior elements**) |
| | Polio | Boston brace until puberty | PSF |

## 6 Complications

### Wound complication risk factors

| Risk factor | Not a risk factor |
|---|---|
| **Serum albumin < 3.5 g/dL** | **Hemiplegia** |
| **WBC / lymphocytes < 1,500 cells/µL** | Number of levels fused |
| **VP shunt** | Blood loss, transfusion |
| **Severe spastic quadriplegia, non-ambulatory, seizures** | Preoperative curve magnitude |
| — | Age, length of surgery |

**Infection rate** — **4–14%** (> idiopathic)

## How it's asked

**"Wound complication risk factors in CP — EXCEPT?"** → **hemiplegia**

**"Known risk factor for wound infection after NM fusion?"** → **albumin < 3.5 g/dL**\
✗ Wrong options: levels · blood loss · curve size

**"Paralytic scoliosis after complete C4-5 SCI at 6 yrs?"** → **100%** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Occipitocervical Instability

*Spine · 40 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Traumatic** — 15–30% of cervical injuries at the occipitocervical junction · in **19% of fatal** cervical injuries

**Acquired** — commonest in **Down syndrome** · usually asymptomatic, found on screening (surgery, Special Olympics)

**Synonyms** — atlanto-occipital dissociation (AOD), occipitocervical dislocation

### Pathophysiology

**Traumatic** — high-energy **translation or distraction** · head usually displaces **anteriorly**

**Acquired** — bony dysplasia, ligament laxity · RA, Down syndrome

**Associations** — atlantoaxial instability, neuro deficit, **vertebral / carotid artery injury**, Down syndrome

### Anatomy

**Occipital condyles** — lateral foramen magnum · near vertebral arteries, medulla, **CN IX–XII**

**Joints (6)** — anterior + posterior median atlanto-odontoid, paired atlanto-occipital, paired atlantoaxial

| Ligament | Attachment | Role |
|---|---|---|
| **Transverse (TAL)** | Behind dens → C1 medial tubercles (horizontal limb of cruciate) | **Primary atlantoaxial stabiliser** · "strongest ligament of the spine" (TO) |
| **Alar** (paired) | Dens → occipital condyles (+ C1 lateral mass) | Strong · OC stability · **limits C1 rotation on C2** (TO) |
| Apical | Dens tip → foramen magnum | **Weak** |
| **Tectorial membrane** | Posterior C2 body → anterior foramen magnum | **Cephalad continuation of the PLL** · atlanto-occipital stability |
| Anterior AO membrane | Foramen magnum → C1 anterior arch | Continuation of ALL (TO) |
| Posterior AO membrane | Foramen magnum → C1 posterior arch | — |

**C1-2 joint (TO)** — near-horizontal facets → **60% of cervical rotation**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Measure | Normal / abnormal |
|---|---|
| X-ray (AP, lateral, odontoid) | **Low sensitivity (57%)** |
| **Powers ratio = BC / OA** (basion–posterior arch ÷ anterior arch–opisthion) | **\~1 normal** · **> 1 → anterior dislocation** · < 1 → posterior AOD, odontoid or atlas ring fracture |
| **Harris rule of 12** | **BDI or BAI > 12 mm** → dissociation |
| **Condyle–C1 interval (CCI)** | **Most sensitive + specific** · normal **< 2.5 mm** |
| **CT** | **Gold standard for bone** · midsagittal reconstruction |
| CT angiogram | Vertebral artery injury; anatomy before fusion |
| **MRI** | Ligament injury with preserved alignment, occult injury, deficits · AOD with normal CT measurements → **MRI** |

![Powers ratio](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2014-powers-ratio-ct-scan.jpg)

### Traynelis classification (direction)

| Type | Displacement |
|---|---|
| I | **Anterior** occiput |
| II | **Longitudinal** (distraction) |
| III | **Posterior** occiput |

### Harborview classification (instability)

| Stage | Injury | Stability |
|---|---|---|
| I | Minimal / undisplaced unilateral ligament injury | Stable |
| II | Minimally displaced, significant MRI soft-tissue injury (traction test) | Stable or unstable |
| III | **Gross malalignment (BAI or BDI > 2 mm beyond normal)** | **Unstable** |

### Atlantoaxial instability criteria (TO)

| Measure | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **ADI (flexion lateral)** | **> 3 mm adult**, **> 5 mm child** → TAL rupture |
| **PADI / SAC** | Normal 17–29 mm · **< 14 mm → cord compression** |
| **Rule of Spence** (open mouth) | C1 lateral mass overhang **> 6.9 mm** → TAL disruption |

**Causes of AAI (TO)** — trauma (hyperflexion), **Morquio**, **Down**, skeletal dysplasia, **os odontoideum**, **Grisel** (infection), **RA**, NF

## 5 Management

```
Occipitocervical instability
├─ Traumatic → PROVISIONAL HALO / TONGS (no traction or prolonged collar)
│      └─ Definitive → OCCIPITOCERVICAL FUSION
│      (traction only considered in Harborview stage 2 with preserved alignment)
└─ Acquired (Down, RA)
       ├─ Asymptomatic → OBSERVE
       └─ Myelopathy / significant pain / RA basilar invagination / tumour → OCCIPITOCERVICAL FUSION
```

**Traumatic OC dissociation (intubated polytrauma)** — **instrumented occipitocervical fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: Philadelphia collar · halo 12 wks as definitive · ACDF

### Occipitocervical fusion technique

**Set-up** — prone, **Mayfield**, set **O-C2 angle** on lateral fluoroscopy before draping · Occiput–**C3** instrumentation

**C1 dissection** — stay **< 1 cm lateral to midline** above the C1 posterior arch → protects **vertebral artery**

**Occipital screws** — **unicortical** (dural **venous sinuses** under the external occipital protuberance) · safe zone: **2 cm lateral + 1 cm inferior to EOP** along superior nuchal line (Orthobullets) · triangle **2 cm lateral, 2 cm inferior to EOP** (OB) — read the options

**Thickest occiput** — **5 cm lateral to EOP** → too lateral for screws; **Mayfield posterior pin** site

![Occipital screw safe zone](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2014-heyworth-modified.jpg)

**C1 anterior cortex breach** — **internal carotid artery** (< 3 mm from C1) · also bicortical C1 or C1-2 transarticular screws\
✗ Wrong options: vertebral · basilar · spinal arteries

**C2** — pars, pedicle, transarticular or translaminar · **C3** lateral mass aimed cephalad + lateral · complications: **nonunion**, bleeding (internal carotid, vertebral artery)

### Atlantoaxial subluxation – Fielding & Hawkins (TO)

| Type | Injury | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| I | Rotatory, **TAL intact** | Collar 6–8 wks |
| II | Anterior **3–5 mm**, one lateral mass pivot, TAL deficient | Collar / surgery |
| III | Anterior **> 5 mm** | **C1-2 fusion** (unstable) |
| IV | **Posterior** C1 on C2 (odontoid erosion) | **C1-2 fusion** (unstable) |

**Surgery (TO)** — adult: **ADI > 5 mm**, dynamic instability · child: deficit, **persistent ADI > 4 mm**, deformity > 3 months, recurrence despite 6 wks immobilisation

**AARS (TO)** — **"cock robin"** (20° tilt one way, 20° rotation other, 10° flexion) · Grisel syndrome · CT shows rotation · child, trivial fall, transient quadriparesis, recovered → **congenital atlantoaxial instability**\
✗ Wrong options: bifid atlas · ossified flavum · syrinx

## How it's asked

**"Down syndrome OC fusion — site for 8 mm unicortical screw?"** → **point B, within 2 cm lateral/inferior of the EOP**

**"C1 drill through anterior cortex endangers?"** → **internal carotid artery**

**"Tectorial membrane on MRI?"** → **posterior C2 body → anterior foramen magnum (PLL continuation)**

**"Intubated MVC, OC dissociation — definitive treatment?"** → **occipital-cervical fusion with instrumentation**

**"10-yr, trivial fall, transient quadriparesis?"** → **congenital atlantoaxial instability** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Odontoid Fracture

*Spine · 41 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **commonest axis fracture** · 10–15% of cervical fractures · **commonest cervical fracture in the elderly (> 65)**

**Bimodal** — young: high-energy head trauma (hyperflexion/extension) · elderly: **simple falls**, often missed, ↑ morbidity + mortality · children: rare, **almost always at the basilar synchondrosis**

**Displacement** — anterior (hyperflexion) → TAL failure + AAI · posterior (hyperextension) → C1 anterior arch strikes dens

### Anatomy

**Ossification** — **5 centres**: body, 2 neural arches, odontoid, **secondary (tip) centre** · **subdental (basilar) synchondrosis fuses \~6 yrs** (5–7) · secondary centre **appears \~3, fuses \~12 yrs** → fused synchondrosis + unfused tip = **8–10 yrs**

**Ligaments** — **transverse = primary atlantoaxial stabiliser** (limits anterior C1 translation) · **alar + apical limit rotation**

**Blood supply** — **watershed at the waist**: apex from **internal carotid** branches · base from **vertebral arteries** → **type II nonunion**

![Odontoid watershed blood supply](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2016-blood-supply-3.jpg)

### Cervical kinematics

| Joint | Flex/ext | Rotation | Lateral bend |
|---|---|---|---|
| Occiput–C1 | 50 | 4 | 8 |
| **C1-2** | **10** | **50** | **0** |
| C3–C7 | 50 | 50 | 60 |
| **Total** | **110** | **100** | **68** |

**C1-2 fusion** — **\~50% loss of rotation**

### Os odontoideum

**Cause** — likely **old trauma** (not failed fusion) · looks like a type II fracture · **observe** if no deficit/instability · **C1-2 fusion** if deficit or instability

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — neck pain worse with **rotation** · **dysphagia** (retropharyngeal haematoma)

**Neuro deficit** — **very rare** (large canal at C1-2)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| X-ray AP, lateral, **open-mouth** | Fracture best seen on **open-mouth view** |
| Flexion-extension | Type I (rule out **OC instability**) + os odontoideum · instability: **ADI > 10 mm**, **SAC < 13 mm** |
| **CT** | **Study of choice** — pattern + stability |
| **CT angiogram** | **Vertebral artery course before posterior screws** (aberrant VA up to 30%) |
| MRI | Only if neuro symptoms |

### Anderson & D'Alonzo

| Type | Fracture | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Oblique tip avulsion — alar ligament** | Rare; rule out **atlanto-occipital instability** (flexion-extension) |
| **II** | **Waist** | **Highest nonunion** (watershed) · 35% of C2 fractures |
| **III** | Into **cancellous C2 body** ± C1-2 joint | Good blood supply + large cancellous surface → heals |

![Anderson & D'Alonzo types](../../MCQ/attachments/q3389-illa-anderson-and-d-alonzo-classification.jpg)

### Grauer (type II)

| Subtype | Pattern | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **IIA** | Undisplaced / minimal, no comminution | **External immobilisation** |
| **IIB** | Displaced, line **anterosuperior → posteroinferior** ("anterior oblique") | **Anterior odontoid screw** (good bone) |
| **IIC** | **Anteroinferior → posterosuperior** or **comminuted** | **Posterior C1-2 stabilisation** |

![Grauer classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2016-grauer-classification.jpg)

### Nonunion risk factors (type II)

| Factor | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Posterior displacement** | **> 2 mm (strongest predictor)** (Orthobullets) · **> 5 mm** (TO, OB) — read the options |
| **Displacement magnitude** | **≥ 5 mm (> 50% nonunion)** (Orthobullets) · **> 6 mm = best predictor** (OB) |
| **Age** | **> 40** (Orthobullets) · **> 50** (OB) · > 65 (TO) · not age < 40 |
| **Delay** | **> 4 days** (✗ Wrong options: 36–48 h) |
| **Angulation** | **> 10°** (≥ 11° OB) · not 7° |
| Gap | **> 1 mm** |
| Others | **Smoking**, comminution, diabetes, osteoporosis |
| No | Gender |

**Overall nonunion** — 33% (up to 88%) · elderly non-op **60–71%**

## 5 Management

| Type / patient | Treatment (Orthobullets) |
|---|---|
| Type I | **Collar** (soft collar acceptable, OB) |
| **Type II < 40** (no risk factors) | **Halo vest** (or hard collar) |
| **Type II 40–80** | **Surgery** |
| **Type II > 80** | **Collar** |
| **Type III** | **Collar 6–12 wks** (✗ Wrong options: halo advantage) |

```
Type II odontoid
├─ Young, no nonunion risk → HALO VEST (or hard collar)
│      (halo if displacement < 6 mm, reduced within 1 wk, age < 60 → > 90% union)
├─ Risk factors, reducible, oblique IIB, good bone, normal habitus → ANTERIOR ODONTOID SCREW
├─ Risk factors otherwise / elderly surgical candidate → POSTERIOR C1-2 FUSION
└─ Elderly, comorbid / > 80 → RIGID COLLAR (fibrous union acceptable)
```

### Elderly type II

**Halo** — **highest morbidity + mortality** (aspiration, pneumonia, dysphagia, pin problems) → **contraindicated** in elderly, especially dementia, COPD

**Posterior C1-2 fusion (surgical candidate)** — ↑ union + **↓ mortality vs non-op** · ↑ SF-36 · less dysphagia than halo · **higher fusion than anterior screw**

**Not a surgical candidate** — **rigid collar 6–8 wks** · **fibrous union likely** and usually stable enough

**> 80 yrs** — **high morbidity + mortality regardless of treatment** · collar recommended

**Nonop vs op in elderly** — nonop → ↑ **nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: VA occlusion · myelopathy · radiculopathy

**Anterior screw in elderly** — avoid with **barrel chest (COPD)**, short neck, osteoporosis (cut-out), **dysphagia**, displaced fractures, fractures **> 4 wks old** · higher failure than posterior fusion

### Anterior odontoid screw

**Indications** — type II with nonunion risk **AND** acceptable/reduced alignment + **fracture line perpendicular to screw (anterior oblique, IIB)** + habitus allowing trajectory

**Pros** — **preserves C1-2 rotation**, fewer infections than posterior · single screw adequate

**Cons** — **higher failure than posterior C1-2 fusion** · not for transverse, posterior oblique, type I/III

![Anterior oblique fracture line suits odontoid screw](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2016-anterior-oblique-fracture-line-amenable-to-odontoid-screw.jpg)

### Posterior C1-2 fusion

**Indications** — type II with nonunion risk (50–80) · **type II/III nonunion** · os odontoideum with deficit/instability\
✗ Wrong options: PT · collar · halo · anterior screw

**C1 lateral mass screws** — **10° medial, 22° cephalad** · don't breach C1 anterior cortex (**internal carotid**)

**C2** — **pars (commonest)**, pedicle, laminar · Gallie/Brooks wiring needs halo (rare)

**Transarticular C1-2 screws** — **contraindicated with aberrant vertebral artery**

**Transoral odontoidectomy** — severe posterior dens displacement + cord compression + deficit · rarely; C1 laminectomy usually enough · combine with posterior fusion

### Halo

**Pin-site infection** — superficial → **tighten pins + oral antibiotics**

### Paediatric synchondrosis fracture

**Features** — **commonest C-spine fracture < 7 yrs** · through basilar synchondrosis · neuro deficit rare · nonunion rare

**Treatment** — **closed reduction (extension)** + **Minerva cast or halo** 6–8 wks\
✗ Wrong options: soft collar · screw · fusion initially

## 6 Complications

**Nonunion** — type II · symptomatic → **posterior C1-2 fusion**

**Mortality** — > 80 poor with any treatment, worst with halo

## X vs Y

| | Type II | Type III |
|---|---|---|
| Site | Waist (watershed) | C2 body |
| Union | Poor | Good |
| Default | Age/risk-based | Collar |

## How it's asked

**"Major nonunion risk in type 2?"** → **posterior displacement > 5 mm** (TO)

**"Nonunion risks EXCEPT?"** → **age < 40**

**"Greatest nonunion risk on images?"** → **type II with posterior displacement**

**"Best predictor of nonunion non-op?"** → **magnitude of displacement**

**"72-yr declines surgery — risk with non-op?"** → **fracture nonunion**

**"40-yr ladder fall, type II — consider?"** → **high nonunion from vascular watershed**

**"78-yr smoker, emphysema, delayed type II?"** → **high nonunion risk → C1-C2 posterior fusion**

**"Elderly type II — highest morbidity/mortality treatment?"** → **halo vest**

**"72-yr COPD, undisplaced type III?"** → **rigid cervical collar**

**"85-yr comorbid, displaced type II?"** → **cervical collar**

**"80-yr COPD + dementia, undisplaced type II?"** → **rigid orthosis 6–8 wks**

**"79-yr posterior C1-2 fusion — true?"** → **lower mortality than non-op**

**"75-yr non-ambulatory comorbid, < 50% displaced?"** → **rigid collar**

**"65-yr diabetic smoker COPD, type II?"** → **reduction + posterior C1-C2 fusion**

**"84-yr demented, undisplaced type II — true?"** → **fibrous union likely in hard orthosis**

**"71-yr posterior displaced horizontal type II?"** → **posterior C1-2 instrumented fusion**

**"81-yr, 5 mm posterior displacement — true?"** → **morbidity + mortality high regardless**

**"Young diabetic smoker, IIB, anterior screw — associated with?"** → **high union, few complications**

**"Aberrant vertebral artery — contraindicated?"** → **C1-C2 transarticular screws**

**"Symptomatic type II nonunion at 7 months?"** → **posterior C1-C2 fusion**

**"42-yr smoker, 5-day delay, favourable obliquity?"** → **anterior odontoid screw**

**"Single anterior screw suits?"** → **type II anterior oblique pattern**

**"37-yr type III?"** → **external orthosis 6–8 wks**

**"Rigid orthosis acceptable EXCEPT?"** → **Jefferson fracture with TAL disruption**

**"28-yr, 2 mm displacement, 7° angulation, 36 h?"** → **halo or hard orthosis (low nonunion risk)**

**"Fused synchondrosis, unfused tip centre — age?"** → **8–10 yrs**

**"Paediatric odontoid fracture?"** → **almost always at the basilar synchondrosis**

**"5-yr unstable synchondrosis fracture?"** → **closed reduction + Minerva cast or halo**

# Ossification of the Posterior Longitudinal Ligament

*Spine · 42 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Population** — **East Asian (Japanese)** commonest · Asian 2–3% (up to 4% of cervical patients) · Caucasian 0.2–0.7% (TO) · not European ancestry

**Sex / age** — **men \~2× women** (not prognostic) · peak 6th decade

**Level** — **C4-C6** (C4-5, C5-6) · **> 95% cervical** · then thoracic, lumbar

### Risk factors & labs

| Risk factor | Not a risk factor |
|---|---|
| **Hyperinsulinaemia** (even without diabetes) · **diabetes** | HLA-B27 (= AS) |
| **Obesity / BMI** (correlate with extent) | Traumatic brain injury (= HO) |
| **Fluoride intoxication (fluorosis)** — urinary fluoride ∝ severity (TO) | Female sex |
| Hyperparathyroidism, **hypophosphataemic rickets**, abnormal Ca metabolism | — |
| Growth hormone imbalance, recurrent minor trauma, infection (TO) | — |
| High-salt low-meat diet, poor Ca absorption, mechanical PLL stress | — |
| Genetic linkage | — |

**Pathology** — vascular infiltration → hypertrophy → **endochondral ossification** of PLL → lamellar bone

**Associations (TO)** — **DISH**, **ossified ligamentum flavum**, **ankylosing spondylitis**, fluorosis

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — often asymptomatic · **myelopathy** (hand clumsiness, gait instability, Hoffmann, hyperreflexia) · radiculopathy · **cord injury from minor trauma** (central canal stenosis)

**MRI clue** — compression at **mid-vertebral body levels**, not only discs

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Role |
|---|---|
| **Lateral X-ray** | **Diagnosis** + **sagittal alignment (lordosis)** · classification |
| **CT** | **Study of choice for extent + thickness** of ossification · screw planning, vertebral artery |
| **MRI** | **Study of choice for cord compression** |
| CT myelogram | MRI contraindicated / complex bony stenosis |

**Pre-op work-up (TO)** — **lateral + dynamic X-rays AND CT** (both) · laminectomy only if lordosis maintained

![OPLL on sagittal CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2046-ct-sagittal-moved.jpg)

### Classification (lateral X-ray) (TO)

| Type | Pattern |
|---|---|
| 1 **Continuous** | Long lesion over several bodies |
| 2 **Segmental** | Behind each body, interrupted at discs |
| 3 **Mixed** | Continuous + segmental |
| 4 **Localised** | Over disc space, 1–2 segments |

**Myelopathy risk** — **mixed + continuous** progress most · **occupancy ratio (OPLL ÷ canal AP) 30–60%** predicts myelopathy (TO)

![OPLL types and occupancy ratio](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17736767-2.png)

## 5 Management

```
OPLL
├─ Mild symptoms, not a surgical candidate → OBSERVE
└─ Significant myelopathy → DECOMPRESS + STABILISE
       ├─ Lordotic spine → POSTERIOR LAMINOPLASTY or LAMINECTOMY + FUSION
       ├─ Kyphotic spine → ANTERIOR CORPECTOMY (OPLL "floated")
       └─ Dynamic myelopathy → INTERBODY FUSION WITHOUT DECOMPRESSION
```

**Posterior (laminoplasty / laminectomy + fusion)** — **needs lordosis** (cord drifts back) · **safer, preferred** (avoids peeling OPLL off dura) · fuse after laminectomy (prevents **post-laminectomy kyphosis**) · risk **OPLL growth**

**Anterior corpectomy** — **kyphotic** spine · **"float" the OPLL** in the corpectomy site instead of excising → avoids **dural tear / CSF leak**

## 6 Complications

**Post-laminoplasty C5 palsy** — **commonest root palsy (\~6%)** · **deltoid / lateral arm (C5 dermatome)** · short C5 root at apex of lordosis, cord drifts back

**Recurrence / growth** — less with complete resection or floating

## X vs Y

| | OPLL | DISH | AS | Fluorosis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ossification | **Posterior** longitudinal ligament | **Anterior**, flowing, ≥ 4 levels | **Marginal syndesmophytes** | **Diffuse** — PLL, flavum, interosseous membranes |
| Marker | Hyperinsulinaemia | Diabetes | **HLA-B27** | Endemic fluoride (Punjab) |
| Anterior osteophytes | Small, non-syndesmotic | Large | — | — |

### Ossified ligamentum flavum (TO)

**Features** — rare cause of **thoracic myelopathy**, East Asians · **posterior** compression → **spastic gait, LL hyperreflexia, normal UL reflexes**

**Imaging** — **T2 sagittal MRI** · **whole-spine screen mandatory** (tandem lesions) · fine CT for planning

**Treatment** — **decompression only** (non-op fails) · better prognosis than other thoracic myelopathy · prognosis ← preop severity + time to surgery

![Thoracic ossified ligamentum flavum](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17134545-1.png)

## How it's asked

**"65-yr from Punjab, myelopathy, MRI sagittal — diagnosis?"** → **OPLL** (TO)

**"65-yr from Punjab, myelopathy, CT neck — diagnosis?"** → **fluorosis** (TO) — read the image and options

**"Mixed OPLL with myelopathy — pre-op tests?"** → **lateral + dynamic X-rays and CT** (TO)

**"Known risk factor for OPLL?"** → **hyperinsulinaemia**

**"Commonest OPLL level?"** → **C4-6**

**"42-yr myelopathy, cord compressed at mid-body levels, thick ossified PLL?"** → **OPLL**

**"Image suggestive of OPLL?"** → **ossified band behind the vertebral bodies on CT**

**"Commonest root palsy after laminoplasty?"** → **C5**

**"Spastic gait, LL hyperreflexia, normal UL reflexes?"** → **thoracic ossified ligamentum flavum** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteoporotic Vertebral Compression Fracture

*Spine · 43 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest fragility fracture** — ✓ VCF · 700,000/yr US · 70,000 admissions · $15 billion\
✗ Wrong options: distal radius · femoral neck · intertrochanteric · proximal humerus

**Prevalence** — **25% > 70 yrs** · **50% > 80 yrs** (up to 40% of women > 80, OB)

**Strongest predictor of further vertebral fractures** — **history of 2 VCFs** (postmenopausal women)

### Osteoporosis

**Definition** — **normal bone quality, decreased quantity** · thin cortices, ↓ trabecular continuity\
✗ Wrong options: abnormal quality = osteomalacia

**WHO** — **T-score < −2.5** · after a fragility fracture → **treat regardless of T-score**

**Peak lumbar BMD** — **females 33–40** · **males 19–33** (variable)

**Screening** — all women **≥ 65** + younger women with osteoporotic fractures

### Associations & mortality

**Pulmonary** — kyphosis → **each VCF ↓ FVC up to 9%** → **↑ death from pulmonary causes** (pneumonia) · ↓ chest/abdominal volume → **early satiety**

**Comorbid** — associated with hypertension, smoking, calcium/vitamin D use · frailty drives both fracture + death

| Mortality | Value |
|---|---|
| **1 yr** | **\~15%** (7–15%) — **less than hip** (hip \~25%) |
| **2 yr** | **\~20% — equivalent to hip** |
| Ranking (1 yr) | **Hip > VCF > distal radius** (distal radius: no ↑ mortality) |
| Medicare cohort | \~2× controls · **men > women** · **younger age at fracture → greater risk** · HR 1.8 · survival 54% / 31% / 11% at 3 / 5 / 7 yrs |

**Further fracture risk** — **20%** after first VCF (OB) · **\~40% within 2 yrs without osteoporosis treatment** (OB) · **5× more likely** (OB) — read the options

**Progressive kyphosis** — **load transfers to the superior adjacent vertebra** · deformity progresses even when pain-free · worse gait, satiety, lungs

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — only **25%** seek attention · localised; may wrap around ribs dermatomally · tenderness on deep spinous palpation

**Neuro** — **< 1%** (SCI very rare; lumbar foraminal root deficits) · no chronic pain in majority

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray whole spine** | Concomitant fractures **20%** · height loss **≥ 20% or ≥ 4 mm** |
| CT | Inadequate films, LE deficit, posterior wall |
| **MRI** | **Acuity (STIR/T2 oedema = acute)**, PLC, cord compression, **benign vs metastatic** · ✓ next study after X-ray |
| Bone scan (3-phase) | Acuity if MRI contraindicated |
| No | Flexion-extension, tagged WBC scan |

![L4 compression fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2021-450px-l4-compressionfracture2008.jpg)

### Benign vs malignant on MRI

| Osteoporotic (benign) | Metastatic (malignant) |
|---|---|
| **Fluid sign** (fluid cleft) | **Complete marrow replacement** (diffuse homogeneous low T1) |
| **Band-like low T1 / high T2 signal** | **Convex posterior border** |
| **Retropulsed fragment** | **Pedicle / posterior column involvement** |
| — | Paraspinal or epidural mass |

![MR fluid sign](../../MCQ/attachments/q5643-illb-illus-b-mr-fluid-sign-sagittal-stir.jpg)

**Suspect metastasis** — **above T5**, atypical films, constitutional symptoms, **young without a fall**

### Labs

**Work-up** — CBC, BMP · **ESR** (infection) · **SPEP + UPEP** (myeloma)

## 5 Management

```
Osteoporotic VCF, neuro intact
├─ Acute → ANALGESIA + EARLY MOBILISATION ± TLSO
│      (calcitonin 4 wks if < 5 days; no bed rest)
├─ Severe pain > 6 wks / Kümmell → KYPHOPLASTY
└─ Progressive deficit / PLL injury / unstable → DECOMPRESSION + LONG FIXATION
       (multiple fixation points ± anterior support)
```

### Non-operative

**First line** — **oral analgesia (opioid acceptable) + gradual activity, walking unrestricted** ± TLSO for comfort · even **> 30° kyphosis or > 50% height loss if PLL intact**\
✗ Wrong options: strict bed rest → DVT, PE, deconditioning, bone loss

**AAOS 2010 guideline** — only **moderate** recommendation: **calcitonin 4 wks if presenting within 5 days** · only **strong** recommendation: **against vertebroplasty** · kyphoplasty = limited/inconclusive option · brace, bed rest + opiates, exercise, ibandronate/strontium, L2 nerve block = inconclusive

**TLSO** — RCTs: same pain, disability, radiographs with or without brace · not strongly recommended

**Calcitonin** — **directly inhibits osteoclasts** (cell-surface receptor)

**Medical** — **bisphosphonates** prevent further fractures (atypical femur fracture risk) · **teriparatide ↑ BMD without atypical femur fracture risk** · exercise ↑ BMD

### Vertebral augmentation

**Indication** — **severe pain after 6 wks** of non-op (narcotic-dependent, sedentary, constipated) · not first line · not for **healed VCF with kyphosis** (TO)

**Evidence** — **double-blind sham-controlled RCTs: vertebroplasty no benefit at any time point** · surgery meta-analysis (OB): **greater pain improvement**

| | Vertebroplasty | Kyphoplasty |
|---|---|---|
| Cavity | None | **Balloon cavity** |
| Cement viscosity | **Less viscous** | More viscous |
| Injection pressure | **Higher** | **Lower** (TO: "kyphoplasty higher pressure" = false) |
| Extravasation | **Higher** | Lower |
| Height restoration | No | Goal; partial reduction |
| Cost / equipment | Less | **More** |
| Pain relief | **Comparable** (TO) | Comparable |
| Future VCF risk | — | Yes (TO) |

**TO trap** — vertebroplasty cement: **high viscosity + more radiopaque than arthroplasty cement** (true statement in TO stem)

**Mechanism** — **eliminates micromotion**

![Kyphoplasty balloon](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2021-kyphoplasty.jpg)

### Kümmell disease

**Definition** — **delayed post-traumatic vertebral body osteonecrosis** · pain-free interval (months–years) → new pain, collapse, **intravertebral cleft** (pathognomonic) · thoracolumbar junction

**Treatment** — **kyphoplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: continued non-op · vertebroplasty (leak, no correction) · fusion without deficit

![Intravertebral cleft of Kümmell disease](../../MCQ/attachments/q214130-illa-intravert-cleft.jpg)

### Operative stabilisation

**Indications** — **progressive neuro deficit**, **PLL injury / unstable** (very rare) · not stable 30% height loss, 15° kyphosis · retropulsion makes cement risky

**Technique** — **long constructs**, multiple fixation points, consider anterior support

## 6 Complications

**Cement extravasation** — into canal → **neuro injury** · vertebroplasty > kyphoplasty · check posterior cortex defects

**Kümmell disease** — avascular necrosis + nonunion

## How it's asked

**"71-yr L2 VCF, 30% height loss — management?"** → **analgesia, TLSO, progressive activity**

**"Ambulatory with oxycodone — first line?"** → **opioids + unrestricted walking**

**"78-yr T11 20% height loss, walker?"** → **analgesics + progressive rehabilitation**

**"AAOS-supported treatment within 5 days?"** → **activity modification + calcitonin**

**"Vertebroplasty vs kyphoplasty — false?"** → **kyphoplasty uses higher cement pressure** (TO)

**"Cementing techniques — EXCEPT?"** → **healed VCF with kyphosis can be cemented** (TO)

**"Kyphoplasty — EXCEPT?"** → **cement under high pressure** (TO)

**"Narcotic-dependent 6 wks after T11 VCF?"** → **percutaneous vertebral augmentation**

**"Vertebroplasty in sham-controlled RCTs?"** → **no benefit at any time point**

**"Old VCF with AVN + collapse, failed conservative care?"** → **kyphoplasty**

**"Surgery vs non-op meta-analysis?"** → **greater improvement in pain**

**"Old T12 VCF — compared with peers?"** → **greater risk of death from pulmonary conditions**

**"Acute T12 VCF — true?"** → **2-yr mortality ≈ hip fracture**

**"Mortality risk after VCF?"** → **greater in men than women**

**"Fragility fracture mortality, decreasing?"** → **hip > VCF > distal radius**

**"T-score −3.0 — commonest fragility fracture?"** → **vertebral compression fracture**

**"VCFs associated with?"** → **20% further fracture risk**

**"Progressive kyphosis after VCF due to?"** → **load transfer to the superior adjacent vertebra**

**"Spinal deformity of progressive osteoporotic collapse?"** → **deformity progression**

**"Untreated, risk of another fragility fracture in 2 yrs?"** → **\~40%**

**"MRI fluid sign?"** → **osteoporotic VCF**

**"75-yr L1 VCF 8 wks — next imaging?"** → **lumbar MRI**

**"Pain-free interval then pain 10 months after VCF?"** → **post-traumatic osteonecrosis (Kümmell)**

**"Breast cancer history — MRI finding favouring osteoporotic?"** → **band of high T2 signal**

**"Osteoporosis — true?"** → **decreased bone quantity**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pathologic Scoliosis

*Spine · 44 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — scoliosis from a **benign bone tumour**, usually in the **posterior elements** · any age; OO + osteoblastoma typically **2nd decade**

**Mechanism** — painful **paraspinal muscle spasm**

| | Osteoid osteoma | Osteoblastoma |
|---|---|---|
| Size | **< 2 cm** nidus | **> 2 cm** |
| Site | **Apex of the concavity** of the curve · posterior elements > body | Posterior elements |
| Curve | **Rigid** (✗ Wrong options: flexible) | — |
| Pain | Severe, **night**, **relieved by NSAIDs** | Less severe, **NSAIDs help less** |
| Neuro | — | **May cause deficits** |
| Histology | **Same as peripheral OO** (✗ Wrong options: more malignant) | Less ossified; ± secondary ABC |

**NSAID mechanism** — nidus makes **PGE₂ + PGI₂**; **COX-2** high in nidal osteoblasts

![Osteoid osteoma at the concave apex](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2059-osteoid-osteoma.jpg)

### Syndromic scoliosis (TO)

| Syndrome | Scoliosis incidence |
|---|---|
| **Marfan (FBN1, fibrillin-1)** | **Two-thirds — highest** |
| Rett (**MECP2**) | Up to half |
| Prader-Willi, Down, Loeys-Dietz | Fewer |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **painful scoliosis**, back pain **worst at night**, NSAID relief · curve usually **mild**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| Standing AP + lateral | Level + severity · cortical thickening with **radiolucent nidus** |
| **Fine-cut CT** | **Best for outlining the lesion + planning** · low-attenuation nidus with central mineralisation, perinidal sclerosis |
| MRI | Neurovascular proximity · may show only oedema, **miss the nidus** |
| **Bone scan / SPECT** | **Markedly ↑ uptake** |

![Nidus on axial CT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2059-scoli-axial.jpg)

### Differential of painful posterior-element lesions

| Lesion | Clue |
|---|---|
| **Osteoid osteoma** | Small sclerotic nidus, painful scoliosis |
| Osteoblastoma | Larger, less ossified, ± ABC |
| ABC | Purely lytic, no sclerotic nidus |
| **Eosinophilic granuloma** | **Vertebral body**, purely lytic, **vertebra plana** |
| Metastasis | Mixed blastic-lytic, no nidus |

## 5 Management

```
Pathologic scoliosis
├─ Minimal curve → NSAIDs + OBSERVE (up to 36 months to resolve)
│      (osteoblastoma rarely responds to NSAIDs)
└─ Painful, progressive scoliosis → EN BLOC RESECTION
       (RFA case by case — usually too near neural elements)
```

**Prognosis** — curve resolves after resection if done **within 15–18 months of curve onset** in a child **< 11 yrs**

## How it's asked

**"Spinal osteoid osteoma — true?"** → **occurs on the concave side of the curve**\
✗ Wrong options: flexible curve · never RFA · always body · more malignant

**"13-yr painful scoliosis, hot bone scan, sclerotic nidus in posterior element?"** → **osteoid osteoma**

**"Highest scoliosis incidence?"** → **Marfan syndrome** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pediatric Cervical Trauma Overview

*Spine · 45 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Share** — uncommon, but **60% of paediatric spinal injuries** · **33% present with neuro deficit** · other organs in \~40%

**Level** — **< 8 yrs: 87% at C3 or above** · > 8 yrs: lower cervical (adult pattern)

**Mechanism** — **MVA commonest** · falls (toddlers, school age) · sports (adolescents)

### Why under-8s are vulnerable

**Anatomy** — **large head : trunk**, **horizontal facets**, **ligamentous laxity**, weak muscles · column stretches **5 cm** without rupture

**SCI** — commoner + more lethal < 8 · recovery better > 8

**Mortality** — **C1 injury 17%** · C4 \~4% · higher at C3 and above

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — full exam (associated injuries) · suspect C-spine with **head + facial trauma** · document sensation incl. **sacral sparing**, motor, reflexes · **repeat — 20% with fractures examine normal** initially

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Mandatory X-rays** — **AP, open-mouth odontoid, cross-table lateral** · oblique (facets) optional · **flexion-extension only under physician supervision**

### Normal paediatric lateral (diagnostic criteria)

| Parameter | Normal |
|---|---|
| **Prevertebral soft tissue** | **< 2/3 of adjacent body width** |
| **Retropharyngeal space** | **< 6 mm at C2** · at C6: **< 22 mm** (Orthobullets) · **< 14 mm in children, < 22 mm adults** (TO) — read the options |
| **Retrotracheal space** | **< 14 mm** |
| **ADI** | **< 5 mm child**, **< 3 mm adolescent** |
| **Pseudosubluxation C2-3 / C3-4** | **< 4 mm** if **posterior laminar line contiguous** |
| Contour lines | Smooth anterior + posterior body, spinolaminar, spinous tips · parallel facets |
| **C3 wedging** | Normal in **7%** |
| **Loss of lordosis** | Normal in **14%** |

![Normal paediatric lateral C-spine](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2047-lateralcspine.jpg)

**CT** — upper cervical fractures, **atlantoaxial rotatory subluxation**, canal compromise · not as screening study

**MRI** — **obtunded / closed head injury**, canal compromise

## 5 Management

### Transport immobilisation

**Board** — **standard adult backboard → cervical flexion (kyphosis)** — avoid · use **occipital recess (head cut-out)** or **thoracic elevation (torso pad)** for < 8 yrs

**Alignment** — in-line, **external auditory meatus in line with the shoulders** (relative extension)\
✗ Wrong options: EAM anterior/posterior · adult board + sandbag under head

**Collars** — commercial collars often misfit → sandbags · paediatric collars acceptable

![Backboard with occipital recess vs standard](../../MCQ/attachments/q210134-illa-backboard.jpg)

### Treatment by injury

| Treatment | Indications |
|---|---|
| **Observation** | **C2-3 pseudosubluxation** |
| **Collar** | Stable odontoid fracture, AAI, **acute AARD**, stable subaxial injury |
| **Halo** (thin calvaria — many low-torque pins) | Unstable odontoid, OC instability, AAI, **subacute AARD**, **Jefferson**, unstable subaxial |
| **Surgery** | Unstable spine + SCI, AAI, **chronic AARD** |

### Car seat & airbag safety

| Stage | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| **Rear-facing**, rear centre | Until **≥ 1 yr and ≥ 20 lb** |
| **Forward-facing seat** | Until **≥ 4 yrs or ≥ 40 lb** |
| **Booster** | Until **60–80 lb and 8 yrs** |
| Seat belt, rear middle | 8–12 yrs |
| **Front seat** | From **13 yrs** · **airbag OFF if < 80 lb** |

## How it's asked

**"8-yr unconscious after fall — acute stabilisation?"** → **in-line, external auditory meatus in line with shoulders**

**"Avoid in young children after trauma?"** → **standard backboard**

**"Consistent car-safety recommendation?"** → **75 lb 13-yr in front seat — airbag OFF**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pediatric Spinal Cord Injury

*Spine · 46 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2–5% of all SCI** · **83% cervical** · M:F 1.5:1

**Age pattern** — **< 8 yrs: cervical** (**large head-to-body ratio**) · **> 8 yrs: thoracolumbar** · upper cervical (C1-4) > lower (C5-7)

**Mechanism** — **MVA / RTA commonest** · falls, sport, **child abuse** · sledding + ATV → thoracolumbar

**Associations** — **head injury > 30%**, intrathoracic, intra-abdominal

### Injury patterns

| Region | Patterns |
|---|---|
| Cervical | OC / atlantoaxial dislocation, **atlantoaxial rotatory fixation**, odontoid (synchondrosis), subaxial ligamentous, **SCIWORA** |
| Thoracolumbar | **Compression (commonest)**, burst, flexion-distraction, fracture-dislocation |

### Anatomy & biomechanics

**Cord** — ends at **L3 at birth** → **L1-2** · adult size by **10 yrs**

**Flexibility** — ligamentous laxity, immature supports, **shallow horizontal facets**, nucleus with ↑ water + ↓ collagen cross-links · column stretches **5 cm** without rupture · forces pass through weak **physeal cartilage**

## 3 Presentation

### Primary survey (paediatric ATLS)

**C** — **Broselow tape** estimates weight

**D** — log-roll + immobilisation on **paediatric board** or adult board with **torso pad / head cut-out** (avoid C-spine flexion) · **Paediatric GCS**

![Board with occipital recess](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2068-f1-large-2.jpg)

**Secondary survey** — myotomes + dermatomes, **rectal + genital exam**, bulbocavernosus reflex

### Paediatric GCS

| Score | Motor | Verbal | Eyes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | Normal spontaneous movement | — | — |
| 5 | Withdraws to touch | Smiles, orients to sound, follows, interacts | — |
| 4 | Withdraws to pain | Cries but consolable, confused | Spontaneous |
| 3 | Abnormal flexion | Inconsistently consolable, moaning | To voice / touch |
| 2 | Extension | Inconsolable, agitated | To pain |
| 1 | None (flaccid) | None | None |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| X-ray AP + cross-table lateral of whole spine | **Multilevel injury common** · ± swimmer's, open-mouth, flexion-extension |
| CT | Polytrauma, high energy, altered mental state, head/face injury · **radiation risk** · **not a screening tool** |
| **MRI** | **Deficit with normal X-rays (SCIWORA)** · soft tissue, cord · no radiation |

**Retropharyngeal space (TO)** — **< 6 mm at C2** (adults + children) · at C6 **< 14 mm children**, **< 22 mm adults**

### SCIWORA (TO)

**Definition** — **spinal cord injury without radiographic abnormality** · deficit with **normal X-ray + CT** · often recovers (TO stem: quadriplegia, normal imaging, complete recovery in 3 months — answer not keyed by source; SCIWORA is the option matching the definition) · MRI is the study

## 5 Management

| Treatment | Indications |
|---|---|
| Analgesia + activity | **Apophyseal, spinous process, transverse process** fractures |
| **Collar 8–12 wks** | Stable odontoid, AAI, acute AARD, stable subaxial |
| **TLSO 8–12 wks** | **Compression < 50% height loss**, **burst < 50% retropulsion, no deficit**, **purely osseous flexion-distraction (Chance)** |
| Halo | Unstable cervical (**thin calvaria → skull penetration**) |
| **Cervical fusion** | OC, atlantoaxial, subaxial instability (OC fusion, transarticular screws, pedicle screws) |
| **Thoracolumbar fixation** | **Unstable burst, cord compression, irreducible fracture-dislocation, ligamentous flexion-distraction** · pedicle screws 1–2 levels above + below |

## 6 Complications

**List** — complete deficit, **progressive spinal deformity**, poor wound healing, CES

**Post-injury scoliosis** — cervical complete SCI **before age 10** → paralytic scoliosis in **virtually 100%** · follow all to maturity

**Prognosis** — most paediatric SCI **incomplete** · SCI commoner + more lethal < 8 · recovery better > 8

## How it's asked

**"9-yr complete C5-6 SCI — scoliosis risk?"** → **100%**

**"Paediatric SCI — EXCEPT?"** → **"cervical injury in < 8 from small head-to-body ratio"** (it's large) (TO)

**"Child quadriplegic, normal X-ray + CT, recovers in 3 months?"** → **SCIWORA** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pediatric Spondylolysis & Spondylolisthesis

*Spine · 47 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Prevalence** — **4–6%** of population (defects **not present at birth**) · **6–7% of adolescent athletes** · up to **47% of adolescent LBP** · up to **47% in gymnasts, weightlifters, football linemen, divers**

**Ethnicity** — ↑ **Native Americans** · possible **autosomal dominant** inheritance

**Level** — **L5 pars (85–95%)**, L5 on S1 · then L4, L3 (TO)

**Laterality (TO)** — **80% bilateral**, 20% unilateral · unilateral defect stresses the contralateral pars

**Risk factors** — **repetitive hyperextension** (+ rotation) · ↑ **pelvic incidence**, sacral slope, sacral table index · ↓ sacral table angle

### Continuum

| Stage | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Pars stress reaction** | Sclerosis, no complete break |
| **Spondylolysis** | Complete pars fracture |
| **Isthmic spondylolisthesis** | Forward slip through the pars defect |
| **Spondyloptosis** | **100%** translation |

**Morita stages (TO)** — early (hairline) → progressive (wider, fragments) → **terminal (sclerotic, pseudarthrosis)** · healing predictors: **unilateral**, earlier stage, **level other than L5**

### Progression

**Lysis → listhesis** — **\~15%** (all bilateral defects) · most pars defects **never need surgery**

| Progression risk ↑ |
|---|
| **Higher grade at presentation (> Meyerding 2, > 50%)** |
| **Dysplastic (Wiltse I)** slips |
| **Slip angle > 45–50°** — best predictor of progression (OB), instability, pseudarthrosis, pain |
| Young age at presentation |

**Severity of current slip** — correlates most with **pelvic incidence** (PI = PT + SS; constant, \~57° adult; high PI → ↑ lumbosacral shear)\
✗ Wrong options: SS · PT · slip grade

## 3 Presentation

**Classic** — active adolescent, **activity-related LBP**, relieved by rest · many asymptomatic · **grade ≠ symptoms**

**Leg** — **hamstring tightness (commonest)**, buttock pain, L5 radicular pain

**Commonest exam finding** — **pain with lumbar extension**, especially **single-leg-stance extension**\
✗ Wrong options: pain on flexion · SLR · lost Achilles reflex

**High-grade / dysplastic** — **heart-shaped buttocks**, flat lumbar lordosis, palpable **step-off**, ↑ **popliteal angle**

**Gait (Phalen-Dickson)** — **short stride, hips + knees flexed (crouched)**

**Listhetic crisis** — severe pain worse with extension, neuro deficit, hamstring spasm · CES rare

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **AP + lateral X-ray** | **Best initial**; lateral shows **80%** of defects · obliques add 15% (OB) but **no extra diagnostic value + more radiation** (Orthobullets, OB) · TO: AP, lateral + **bilateral obliques** |
| Oblique | **Scotty dog collar** (neck = pars, nose = TP, eye = pedicle, ear = SAP, front leg = IAP) |
| **SPECT** | **Most sensitive** after normal X-rays (OB) · "gold standard" (TO) · now rarely used (radiation) |
| **MRI** | **As sensitive + specific as SPECT, no radiation** → **next test** after negative X-rays · STIR oedema = acute · neuro deficit, stenosis |
| **CT (thin cut)** | **Best to delineate the defect** · age + healing |
| Bone scan | Most sensitive screen (lesion may be cold) |
| Flexion-extension | Instability (**> 3 mm** translation abnormal, TO) |

![Scotty dog collar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2058-scott-dog.jpg)

### Wiltse-Newman

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **I Dysplastic** | Congenital lumbosacral facets/sacrum, **pars intact** · **more neuro symptoms, more progression** |
| **II-A Isthmic** | **Pars fatigue (stress) fracture** — lytic |
| **II-B Isthmic** | **Pars elongation** (healed microfractures) |
| **II-C Isthmic** | **Acute pars fracture** |
| III | Degenerative (commonest overall) |
| IV | Traumatic |
| V | Neoplastic / pathologic |
| VI | Iatrogenic (TO) |

**Marchetti-Bartolozzi** — developmental (Wiltse I + II) vs acquired (traumatic, post-surgical, pathologic, degenerative)

### Meyerding

| Grade | Slip |
|---|---|
| I | < 25% |
| II | 25–50% |
| III | 50–75% |
| IV | 75–100% |
| V | **Spondyloptosis** (> 100%) |

**Low grade** I–II · **high grade** III–V

![Meyerding grades](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2058-meyerding-classification.jpg)

**Slip angle** — lumbosacral kyphosis

![Slip angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2058-slip-anlge-2.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Spondylolysis / spondylolisthesis
├─ Asymptomatic (any grade) → OBSERVE, NO ACTIVITY RESTRICTION
├─ Symptomatic lysis / low grade → ACTIVITY MODIFICATION + PT 6 MONTHS
│      └─ Acute stress reaction or failed PT → BRACE (TLSO / LSO) 6–12 WKS
└─ Failed non-op
       ├─ L1–L4 defect, multiple defects → PARS REPAIR
       ├─ L5 lysis / low grade (I–II) → L5-S1 POSTEROLATERAL FUSION
       └─ High grade (III–V) → L4-S1 POSTEROLATERAL FUSION ± reduction
```

### Non-operative

**PT** — 6 months: **hamstring stretching, pelvic tilts, abdominal strengthening** · most improve

**Bracing** — **acute pars stress reaction** (brace > activity restriction alone; prevents defect formation) · failed PT · chronic L5 lysis failing 3 months PT (no STIR signal) → **rigid LSO 6–12 wks** · **antilordotic (hypolordotic) brace**\
✗ Wrong options: hyperextension brace

**TO stepwise protocol** — avoid hyperextension → **non-rigid corset** → after 2–4 wks PT (core) → still painful at 4 wks → **TLSO / Boston antilordotic brace** · healing 3–6 months · return to play from **8 wks** if pain-free

**TO answers** — SPECT-proven bilateral stress fracture → **activity modification + rest** first · **2-month-old bilateral pars defect → antilordotic brace**

**Sport** — stopping sport ≥ 3 months improves outcome · return controversial

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Direct pars repair** | **L1–L4 isthmic defect** failing non-op, **multiple defects** · preserves motion · screw, tension wiring, screw + hook · not with **disc degeneration** (MRI first), facet arthropathy, listhesis |
| **L5-S1 posterolateral fusion** ± ALIF | **L5 spondylolysis** failing non-op · **low grade (I–II)** failing non-op, progressive, deficit or **dysplastic** · in situ, ± instrumentation, TLSO |
| **L4-S1 posterolateral fusion** ± reduction | **High grade (III–V)** · L5-S1 alone fails for > 50% slip |
| Gaines L5 vertebrectomy | **Spondyloptosis** (grade V) |
| No | Isolated ASF, pars repair with listhesis |

**TO surgical indications** — pain > **6–12 months** of conservative care, neuro deficit, worsening symptomatic listhesis, **grade ≥ III**, progression > 1 grade, instability > 3 mm · fusion avoided in pro athletes (adjacent segment disease: **36%** reoperation at 10 yrs after posterior lumbar fusion)

**TO acute grade 3 in a gymnast, no deficit** — **observation** initially (then elective fusion) — controversial

**Return to sport after fusion** — non-contact 3–6 months, contact 6–12 months · ALIF may help · 90% success

### Reduction

**Pros** — restores sagittal alignment, ↓ lumbosacral kyphosis · TO: partial slip-angle reduction favoured for high grade

**Cons** — complications **8–30%** · **L5 root commonest injury** (**weak EHL / great-toe extension**, hip abduction, dorsal foot numbness) · sexual dysfunction · catastrophic deficit → neuromonitoring

**360° fusion + decompression** — more in-hospital complications

## 6 Complications

**List** — **L5 root injury (commonest neuro)**, pseudarthrosis, slip progression, hardware failure

## X vs Y

| | Dysplastic (I) | Isthmic (II) |
|---|---|---|
| Pars | Intact (elongated) | Defect |
| Neuro | **More** (intact arch traps cauda) | Less |
| Progression | **Higher** | Lower |

## How it's asked

**"Most sensitive test when AP + lateral normal?"** → **SPECT**

**"13-yr, 6 months back pain, SPECT + CT?"** → **spondylolysis**

**"Lineman with insidious LBP — exam?"** → **pain with lumbar extension in single-leg stance**

**"Commonest exam finding in chronic spondylolysis?"** → **pain with lumbar extension**

**"14-yr, extension pain, negative X-rays — next?"** → **MRI**

**"15-yr lineman, extension pain — true?"** → **best initial studies are AP + lateral alone**

**"Gymnast, MRI finding fitting rest + PT?"** → **pars fracture (Figure A)**

**"Gymnast, SPECT bilateral pars stress fracture — next?"** → **activity modification + rest** (TO)

**"2-month-old bilateral pars defect?"** → **antilordotic brace** (TO)

**"13-yr gymnast, acute pain, SPECT hot L5 pars?"** → **molded lumbosacral orthosis**

**"Asymptomatic low-grade isthmic slip in soccer player?"** → **observe, no activity restriction**

**"Chronic L5 lysis, failed 3 months PT, no STIR signal?"** → **LSO brace**

**"7-yr gymnast with L5 pars defect — true?"** → **likely never needs surgery**

**"Spondylolysis, extension pain, hamstring tightness — initial?"** → **activity modification ± brace + PT**

**"Grade I isthmic slip in 13-yr gymnast?"** → **short activity modification + structured PT**

**"Parameter correlating with severity?"** → **pelvic incidence**

**"Lines measuring the angle best correlating with severity?"** → **pelvic incidence lines (C + D)**

**"Grade III — predicts progression?"** → **slip angle**

**"Factor associated with progression?"** → **> Meyerding 2 at presentation**

**"Gymnast acute grade 3 L5-S1, no deficit?"** → **observation** (TO)

**"12-yr, 80% slip, failed non-op?"** → **posterior instrumented fusion L4-S1**

**"Reduction of high-grade L5-S1 slip — complication?"** → **weak great-toe extension (L5)**

**"14-yr, grade 4, crouched gait — surgery?"** → **posterior fusion L4–S1 ± instrumentation**

**"Gait pattern in spondylolisthesis?"** → **short stride, hips + knees flexed**

**"Same lesion at L3 — surgical difference?"** → **pars interarticularis repair indicated**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pseudosubluxation of the Cervical Spine

*Spine · 48 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **physiologic** anterior translation (up to **4 mm / 40%**) in young children

**Incidence** — **\~20%** of children admitted with polytrauma · **no association with gender**, trauma, intubation or injury severity\
✗ Wrong options: commoner in girls

**Age** — **< 8 yrs**

**Level** — **C2 on C3 commonest** · C3 on C4 second

**Cause** — **horizontal facet joints** → become vertical with age

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — lateral + **flexion-extension**

**Swischuk's line** — spinolaminar line **C1 → C3** · C2 spinolaminar point within **1.5 mm** = pseudosubluxation

![Swischuk's line](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2048-lateral-xray-pseudosubluxation.jpg)

### Pseudosubluxation vs true subluxation

| Pseudosubluxation | True traumatic subluxation |
|---|---|
| **Reduces on extension** | Persists |
| Spinolaminar line **within 1.5 mm** of C2 | > 1.5 mm |
| **No significant trauma** history/exam | Trauma (e.g. **Hangman's fracture**) |
| **No anterior soft-tissue swelling** | Prevertebral swelling |

![True subluxation — Hangman's fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2048-hangman-fracture.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Observation** — no ↑ morbidity or mortality

## How it's asked

**"Pseudosubluxation — EXCEPT?"** → **more common in female children** (TO)

**"5-yr, no trauma, C2-3 step reduces on extension?"** → **pseudosubluxation of the cervical spine**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rheumatoid Cervical Spondylitis

*Spine · 49 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Prevalence** — cervical involvement in **90% of RA** (often missed) · cervical spine = **2nd commonest RA site** after peripheral joints (TO) · **C1-C2 (synovial joint) = commonest segment** (TO)

**Risk factors (TO)** — older age at onset, active synovitis, **↑ CRP**, rapidly erosive peripheral disease, early joint subluxation

**DMARDs** — have **↓ cervical surgery** rates

### Three patterns

| Pattern | Frequency (Orthobullets) | Frequency (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **Atlantoaxial subluxation (commonest)** | **50–80%** | **19–70%** |
| **Basilar invagination / cranial settling** | 40% | 38% |
| **Subaxial subluxation** | 20% | 7–29% |

**Others (TO)** — inflammatory discitis, **atraumatic odontoid erosion / fracture**

### Genes & lab markers

**Anti-CCP** — **most sensitive + specific RA marker** (✓ for C2 pannus)\
✗ Wrong options: CRP/WBC (non-specific) · HLA-B27 (AS) · M-spike (myeloma)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **neck pain** (craniocervical, 69%), stiffness · **occipital headache** (**lesser / greater occipital nerve, C2**), mastoid pain · **myelopathy** (hand clumsiness, gait instability, hyperreflexia) · vertebrobasilar: tinnitus, vertigo, visual change, dysphagia (TO)

**C1-2 facet arthrosis / O-C1 ankylosis** — **axial neck pain + ↓ rotation** with occipital headache\
✗ Wrong options: Spurling · Hoffmann · Lhermitte

### Ranawat classification (neurologic)

| Class | Findings |
|---|---|
| I | Pain, no deficit |
| II | **Subjective** weakness, hyperreflexia, dysaesthesia |
| IIIA | **Objective** weakness + UMN signs, **ambulatory** |
| **IIIB** | Objective weakness + UMN, **non-ambulatory** → **do NOT operate** (poor results) |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Flexion-extension X-rays** — **always before elective surgery** (anaesthesia)

**CT** — bony anatomy, **vertebral artery** course before transarticular screws

**MRI** — **cord compression + myelomalacia** (study of choice) · **cervicomedullary angle**

### Atlantoaxial subluxation – diagnostic criteria

| Measure | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **AADI normal** | **< 3 mm** adult (< 5 mm child) |
| **Instability** | **> 3.5 mm** change flexion↔extension (Orthobullets) · AADI > 3 mm not fixed (TO) — **not alone an indication for surgery** |
| > 7 mm motion | Alar ligament disruption |
| **AADI surgery** | **> 10 mm** (Orthobullets, OB) · 6–10 mm or > 8 mm cited (TO) |
| **PADI / SAC surgery** | **< 14 mm** (Orthobullets, OB) · **< 13–14 mm** (TO) · PADI < 14 mm **97% sensitive** for deficit |
| **PADI predicting recovery** | **> 13 mm** (Orthobullets, OB, TO) · **> 14 mm** (OB) — read the options |

**Best predictor of neuro deficit** — **PADI** · cord 10 mm + 1 mm dura + 1 mm CSF each side → PADI ≥ 14 mm (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: AADI

**Mechanism** — **pannus** between dens and C1 ring → destroys **transverse ligament + dens** · usually **anterior** C1 on C2

![Dynamic atlantoaxial subluxation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2043-dynamic-xray-atlantoaxial-subluxation-small-moved.jpg)

### Basilar invagination – lines

| Line | Landmarks | Abnormal |
|---|---|---|
| **Ranawat C1-2 index** | **Centre of C2 pedicle → C1 arch line** | Normal **17 mm men / 15 mm women** · **< 13 mm = impaction** (Orthobullets) · **< 15 mm in men** (TO) · **most reproducible** |
| **McGregor** | **Posterior hard palate → most caudal occiput** | Dens tip **> 4.5 mm above** · **routine screening line** (TO) |
| **Chamberlain** | Dorsal hard palate → **posterior foramen magnum (opisthion)** | Dens **> 5 mm above** · hard to see |
| **McRae** | **Foramen magnum** opening (basion → opisthion) | Dens below = no impaction (asymptomatic) |
| Fischgold-Metzger | **AP view** — digastric grooves | Normally 10.7 mm above dens tip (TO) |

**Best combination for cranial settling (TO)** — **Clark station + Ranawat + Redlund-Johnell**

**MRI** — **cervicomedullary angle < 135°** → impending deficit → surgery

![Ranawat C1-2 index](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2043-ranawat-c1-2.jpg)

![McGregor's line](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2043-mcgreggor.jpg)

### Subaxial subluxation

**Features** — multilevel, with upper instability · ↑ with **steroids, males, seropositive, nodules, severe RA** · pannus + facet/Luschka laxity

**Cord compression** — slip **> 4 mm or > 20%** · **cervical height index (body height ÷ width) < 2.0** → \~100% sensitive + specific for deficit

## 5 Management

**Goal** — prevent progression; **may not reverse existing deficit** · pharmacotherapy first

### Operative criteria

| Pattern | Indication | Operation |
|---|---|---|
| **AAS** | **AADI > 10 mm** or **PADI < 14 mm** (even without deficit), **progressive myelopathy** · not 3.5 mm change alone | **Posterior C1-2 fusion** (reducible; **transarticular screws** avoid halo; CT for VA) |
| AAS + **basilar invagination** / irreducible AAS | — | **Occiput–C2 fusion ± C1 posterior arch resection** |
| **Basilar invagination** | **Progressive migration > 5 mm**, deficit, **CMA < 135°** | **Occiput–C2 fusion** · transoral / retropharyngeal odontoid resection for **brainstem compromise** |
| **Subaxial** | **> 4 mm / > 20% slip + intractable pain + neuro** | Posterior fusion + wiring |

**Pannus** — **resolves after posterior fusion** (stability) → **posterior fusion ± decompression** for periodontoid pannus myelopathy · **odontoidectomy** only for residual anterior compression after fusion\
✗ Wrong options: transoral resection first (CSF leak, wound, more morbidity)

**TO basilar impression** — surgery by **symptoms, not degree** · anterior impingement → **occiput-C1-2 fusion in extension** ± later anterior odontoid excision · posterior impingement → suboccipital craniectomy + C1 (± C2) laminectomy

## 6 Complications

**Failure to improve** — **Ranawat IIIB**

**Pseudarthrosis** — **10–20%** · ↓ by **extending to occiput**

**Adjacent level degeneration**

## How it's asked

**"Commonest RA cervical complication?"** → **atlantoaxial instability** (TO)

**"Commonest instability in RA?"** → **atlanto-axial** (TO)

**"RA cervical — FALSE?"** → **"PADI more than 13–14 mm is a surgical indication"** (it's less than) (TO)

**"Most useful measure predicting deficit?"** → **PADI** (TO)

**"RA cervical involvement occurs as?"** → **C1-2 instability** (TO)

**"Most frequently involved segment?"** → **C1-C2** (TO)

**"Spastic quadriplegia — good prognostic parameter?"** → **PADI > 13 mm** (TO)

**"RA myelopathy + AAS on flexion-extension?"** → **posterior C1-C2 instrumented fusion**

**"Predicts neural recovery after decompression?"** → **PADI > 13 mm**

**"Surgical indications EXCEPT?"** → **3.5 mm ADI change on flexion-extension**

**"Predicts full recovery with stabilisation?"** → **PADI > 14 mm**

**"RA periodontoid pannus myelopathy — treatment?"** → **posterior fusion ± decompression**

**"Hard palate → most caudal occiput?"** → **McGregor line** (TO)

**"RA, occipital headaches, C1-2 arthrosis — exam?"** → **axial neck pain + ↓ rotation**

**"Radiographic surgical indication?"** → **cervicomedullary angle 120°**

**"C2 pannus — lab?"** → **positive anti-CCP**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sacroiliac Joint Dysfunction

*Spine · 50 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — overlooked; **15–30% of outpatient LBP**

**Risk factors** — **previous lumbar fusion, especially > 3 levels** (≈ adjacent segment disease) · **pregnancy + vaginal delivery** (3rd-trimester oestrogen/relaxin → hypermobility) · pelvic trauma · **iliac crest graft harvest** \
✗ Wrong options: age > 65 · male sex · THA · prior SI injection

### Causes

| Mechanism | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Idiopathic (commonest)** | **Repetitive trauma** → capsule/ligament tension, shear, hypo/hypermobility |
| Arthritis | **\~100% of spondyloarthropathies** (AS, Reiter's) · HLA-B27 |
| Infection | Haematogenous, **unilateral** · S. aureus, Pseudomonas, Cryptococcus, TB · IVDU, endocarditis, immunosuppression |
| Metabolic | CPPD, gout, ochronosis, hyperparathyroidism, renal osteodystrophy, acromegaly |
| Tumour | Metastasis commonest (pelvis 40% of bony mets, 2nd to spine) · primary rare: GCT, PVNS, chondrosarcoma |
| Extra-articular | Enthesopathy (posterior ligaments), **insufficiency fractures**, post-traumatic (**lateral compression** pelvic injuries) |

### Anatomy & biomechanics

**Joint** — **largest axial joint**, \~17.5 cm² · called synovial though **superior 75% non-synovial** · flat until puberty → ridges by 30 → erodes by 50 · ankylosis common in men by 50

**Ligaments** — thin anterior capsule · **posterior interosseous ligament** (posterior capsule rudimentary) · sacrotuberous (→ ischial tuberosity), sacrospinous (→ ischial spine)

**Innervation** — anterior **L2–S2 ventral rami** + sacral plexus · posterior **L4–S4 dorsal rami** (RFA targets **L5–S3 lateral branches**)

**Biomechanics** — **triplanar shock absorber** · "keystone in arch" · takes 6× medial load of lumbar spine but fails at 1/20 of its axial load · motion **< 4° rotation, 1.6 mm translation** · **nutation (nodding) = commonest motion** (backward rotation of ilium on sacrum)

![SI joint anatomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422812-si-joint.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **just inferior to the PSIS** · only 4% above L5 · may radiate past knee · **relieved by a tight belt**

**Tenderness** — **sacral sulcus (most)** > **PSIS (2nd)**

**Neuro** — intact (pain-inhibited weakness only)

### Provocative tests

**Rule** — no single test 100% · **> 3 positive = highly suggestive**\
✗ Wrong options: 2

| Test | Technique |
|---|---|
| **FABER (Patrick)** | Flexion, abduction, ER → SI pain (groin pain = hip/iliopsoas) |
| **Fortin finger** | Patient points **twice** to area **inferomedial to PSIS** |
| **Gaenslen** | **Affected hip extended off the table**, contralateral hip + knee flexed → shear |
| SI compression | Lateral decubitus, medial force on iliac crest |
| Anterior sacral thrust | Prone, anterior force on sacrum |
| SI distraction | Supine, posterior force on ASIS |
| SLR | Usually negative; may hurt **> 60°** (SI motion) |

![Gaenslen's test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422812-gaenslen-test.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – SI injection

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **> 75% pain relief after a single injection** | **Confirms diagnosis** |
| > 50% relief after **two** injections | Diagnostic |
| Injection response | Does **not** predict pain relief after fusion |

**Guidance** — fluoroscopy/US (blind injection reaches joint only **22%**) · max **3 in 6 months / 4 per year**

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray: pelvis AP, inlet, outlet, obliques, **SI views**, lumbar flexion-extension | Narrowing, sclerosis, cysts, osteophytes, ankylosis · **25% of asymptomatic > 50 abnormal** |
| **Flamingo views** | Single-leg standing → **pelvic instability** |
| CT | Surgical planning · poorer than injection diagnostically |
| MRI | Exclude tumour, infection |
| **Bone scan** | **Specificity 90%**, sensitivity 12%, PPV 86%, NPV 72% |

**Differential (top 5)** — lumbar stenosis, DDD, hip OA, hip labral tear, disc herniation

## 5 Management

```
SI joint dysfunction
├─ 1st line → NSAIDs, PT (core), PELVIC BELT, ± prolotherapy (≥ 4 wks)
├─ 2nd line → SI CORTICOSTEROID INJECTION (60% relief at 6 months)
├─ 3rd line → RADIOFREQUENCY ABLATION (lateral sacral branches)
└─ Confirmed generator, failed non-op → SI ARTHRODESIS
       ├─ Normal anatomy → MINIMALLY INVASIVE (triangular titanium implants)
       └─ Dysmorphic sacrum / revision / aberrant anatomy → OPEN POSTERIOR
```

**Pelvic belt** — 4–8 inch belt around **greater trochanters** (medial compression) · best **after pregnancy**

**Prolotherapy** — phenol/glucose at ligament bases → scarring · best for **ligamentous laxity**

**Injection** — **lower success after lumbar fusion** · not first line

**RFA** — limited (can't denervate anterior joint)

**MIS vs open arthrodesis** — MIS: shorter stay, smaller incision, less blood loss, **earlier full weight bearing**, fewer SSIs (theory) · "fusion" by **ongrowth onto implants** · open: posterior approach, protected WB **12 wks**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Risk |
|---|---|
| SSI | Immunocompromise, smoking, diabetes |
| Wound | **Open** (dependent wound) |
| **Nerve injury (L5, S1, S2)** | **MIS**, **sacral dysmorphism** |
| Pseudarthrosis | **Up to 5%** → open revision |

**QoL** — worse than COPD + mild heart failure; ≈ hip/knee OA
