## How it's asked

**"Buttock pain after spine fusion, >75% relief with injection — risk factor?"** → **spine fusion**

**"Post-fusion SI pain, positive provocative tests — confirm with?"** → **diagnostic SI joint injection**

**"SI dysfunction after 3-level fusion — true?"** → **injection less successful after lumbar fusion**

**"SI joint pathology — exam finding?"** → **pain extending hip off table with contralateral hip/knee flexed (Gaenslen)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Sacroiliitis

*Spine · 51 / 69*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **inflammation** of the SI joint → LBP + posterior pelvic pain

**Demographics** — teens to middle age · **M > F**

**Causes** — chronic inflammation (**AS, Reiter's**), infection, trauma, overuse, **pregnancy** · may end in fibrosis + ossification

**Septic** — **IVDU**, haematogenous · gram-positive cocci (Staph ± Strep)

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Fact |
|---|---|
| **HLA-B27** | Ankylosing spondylitis · **1–2% of all people** carry it |
| RF | Usually **negative** in AS |
| **ESR / CRP** | **Usually ↑** |
| WBC | Usually normal; ↑ in infection |
| **Blood cultures** | Positive in **\~50%** of septic cases |

**Reiter's** — **oligoarthritis + conjunctivitis + urethritis**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain with prolonged standing, **difficulty climbing stairs**, generalised LBP, ipsilateral hip weakness, **morning stiffness**

**Exam** — **FABER → posterior (SI) pain** with otherwise painless hip ROM · AS: thoracolumbar flexion deformity

**Septic** — fever, leukocytosis, limp; may mimic hip sepsis (hip US/exam normal) · L5 root irritation possible

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray | **Non-specific erosions**, sclerosis, calcification |
| **MRI with gadolinium** | **Study of choice** — T2 **fluid / inflammation** in SI joint, **abscess** |
| Nuclear medicine | Historical |

![Septic sacroiliitis on axial T2 MRI](../../MCQ/attachments/q214855-c-ax-t2-pelvis.jpg)

## 5 Management

| Cause | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Infection** | **IV antibiotics** (until symptoms + CRP settle) → oral · **surgery only if antibiotics fail or large abscess** |
| IVDU septic shock (GPC clusters + chains) | **Vancomycin + clindamycin** (✗ Wrong options: 1st-gen cephalosporin alone · immediate I&D) |
| Trauma / overuse | Rest, activity modification, NSAIDs, steroid injection |
| **Pregnancy** | **Observe** — resolves after delivery |
| Spondyloarthropathy | Aggressive PT, NSAIDs, **TNF inhibitors** / biologics |

```
Septic sacroiliitis
├─ No abscess → EMPIRIC BROAD-SPECTRUM IV ANTIBIOTICS, narrow on cultures
└─ Failed antibiotics / large abscess → SURGICAL DEBRIDEMENT
```

**Prognosis** — most resolve

## X vs Y

| | Septic sacroiliitis | Septic hip | Transient synovitis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hip ROM | **Painless** | Painful | Painful |
| FABER | **Posterior pain** | Groin pain | — |
| Hip US | Normal | Effusion | Effusion |
| Treatment | **IV antibiotics** | I&D | NSAIDs |

## How it's asked

**"IVDU, septic shock, SI fluid without abscess, GPC clusters + chains?"** → **vancomycin + clindamycin**

**"12-yr febrile, painful FABER, normal hip, SI fluid on MRI?"** → **broad-spectrum IV antibiotics + monitor**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scheuermann's Kyphosis

*Spine · 52 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 0.4–8.3% · **commonest structural kyphosis in adolescents** · 2nd commonest adolescent deformity after idiopathic scoliosis (TO)

**Age / sex** — onset **10–12 yrs** (diagnosed 12–17) · **M:F 2:1 to 7:1**

**Location** — **thoracic** (commonest) · thoracolumbar/lumbar less common

### Genes & pathology

**Inheritance** — **autosomal dominant**

**Theory (most accepted)** — **developmental error in collagen aggregation → abnormal endplate** (defective endochondral ossification) · others: ring apophysis osteonecrosis, disc herniation, relative osteoporosis, biomechanical growth arrest

**Histology (TO)** — abnormal endplate cartilage, irregular mineralisation, **↓ collagen : proteoglycan ratio**

**HLA-B27** — association stated in one OB explanation

### Associations

| Association | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Lumbar hyperlordosis** | Compensatory |
| **Lumbar spondylolysis** | **33%** (Orthobullets) · **50–54%** (TO) — read the options |
| **Scoliosis** | **33%** |
| Dural cysts | — |
| Tight hamstrings, iliopsoas, anterior shoulder | — |
| Pulmonary compromise | Curves **> 100°** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — cosmesis, dull thoracic/lumbar pain (worse with activity, better with rest) · neuro deficit **rare**

**Exam** — **rigid kyphosis, sharper angulation on forward bending** · doesn't correct prone/extension · compensatory cervical/lumbar lordosis

**Normal thoracic kyphosis** — **20–45°** (Orthobullets) · **20–50°** T5-T12 (OB) — 38° in a 13-yr = normal · any thoracolumbar kyphosis abnormal

![Clinical Scheuermann kyphosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2061-clinical-scheuermann.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria (Sorensen)

| Criterion |
|---|
| **Anterior wedging > 5° of 3 consecutive vertebrae** (at apex) |
| Irregular endplates / apophyseal lines |
| **Schmorl's nodes** (disc into endplate) |
| **Disc space narrowing** |
| TO: + thoracic kyphosis **> 50°** |

✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — posterior wedging, lumbar hypolordosis, preserved disc height, **marginal syndesmophytes (AS)**

![Anterior wedging over three vertebrae](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2061-f1-1-moved.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| AP + lateral standing | Wedging, endplates, Schmorl's, scoliosis · **C7 plumb line** (sagittal balance) |
| **Hyperextension lateral (supine over bolster)** | **Separates postural kyphosis** · Scheuermann's is **inflexible** |
| MRI | Pre-op (controversial) — disc herniation, epidural cyst, cord, stenosis · **any neuro deficit** |
| Lumbar films | Spondylolysis if LBP |

### Types

| | Thoracic (typical, type 1) | Thoracolumbar / lumbar (atypical, type 2) |
|---|---|---|
| Curve | T1/2–T12/L1, **apex T6–T8** | T4/5–L2/3, apex TL junction |
| Frequency | Commonest | Less common |
| Radiograph | Wedging | **Endplate irregularity, Schmorl's, narrow discs — less wedging** |
| Course | **Better prognosis** | **More pain, more progressive** |
| Surgical threshold | 70–75° | **25–30°** (OB) |

## 5 Management

```
Scheuermann's kyphosis
├─ < 60° (TO: < 50°), mild symptoms → OBSERVE + PT (extension, hamstring stretch)
├─ 60–80° with growth left → EXTENSION BRACE (Jewitt / Milwaukee)
└─ > 75°, neuro deficit, cord compression, severe adult pain → POSTERIOR SPINAL FUSION ± PONTE / SPO
```

### Bracing

**Thresholds** — **60–80° with growth remaining** (Orthobullets) · **50–74°** skeletally immature (OB) · **> 50° with ≥ 1 yr growth + 40–50% flexibility** (TO) — read the options

**Brace** — **extension orthosis (Jewitt, high chest pad)** · **Milwaukee** (TO) full time 12–18 months then part time · TLSO if apex **T7–T9 or lower** · Boston for < 70°

**Outcome** — stops progression; best if **< 65°** and **> 15° correction in brace** · compliance poor \
✗ Wrong options: Charleston night brace · electrical stimulation

**TO answer** — 12-yr, 55°, pain, no deficit → **Milwaukee brace**

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Source |
|---|---|
| **Kyphosis > 75°** | Orthobullets, OB |
| **> 70–75° + pain not relieved + unacceptable cosmesis — all** | TO |
| **> 60° + persistent pain** | OB |
| Neuro deficit, cord compression | All |
| Severe pain in adults | Orthobullets |
| Not if painless < 75° | OB |

**Technique** — **posterior-only PSF, dual rods**, pedicle screws ± hooks · **cantilever** or **compression across posterior anchors** · **Ponte (posterior shortening)** for stiff curves: remove spinous processes, flavum, facets · anterior release rarely now · **MEP + SSEP monitoring**\
✗ Wrong options: intracanal hooks at apex

**Osteotomy choice** — **Smith-Petersen** for **long, sweeping kyphosis** (\~10°/level, less in rigid Scheuermann) \
✗ Wrong options: PSO (AS, 30–40°/level) · VCR (sharp angular kyphosis)

**Goal** — final kyphosis **40–50°** · **correct ≤ 50%** of original

### Fusion levels

**Proximal** — whole kyphotic Cobb

**Distal (LIV)** — **sagittal stable vertebra (SSV)**: most proximal body **bisected by / ≥ 50% anterior to the posterior sacral vertical line** · old **first lordotic disc** method → more DJK

**Too long (e.g. L5 when SSV is L3)** — protects against DJK but **excessively stiff** → early disc degeneration

![Posterior sacral vertical line selecting LIV](../../MCQ/attachments/q218926-b-screenshot-2023-05-02-at-11-46-17-am.jpg)

**Outcomes** — 60–90% pain improvement (not correlated with correction) · residual > 75° → worse function

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Neurologic** | **0.6–0.8%** (> AIS surgery) · cord stretch → ensure posterior shortening · reverse correction on signal change |
| **Distal junctional kyphosis** | **20–30%** · avoid: fuse to **SSV**, **don't overcorrect > 50%** |
| **Proximal junctional kyphosis** | **Overcorrection + negative sagittal balance** (less common) |
| Others | Pseudarthrosis, hardware failure, loss of correction, **SMA syndrome** |

**Junctional kyphosis** — Cobb **> 10°** between fused and unfused segments

**Prognosis** — at least some progression in **80%** · adult pain rarely limits activity (mean 71°) · > 75° → severe thoracic pain

## X vs Y

| | Scheuermann's | Postural kyphosis |
|---|---|---|
| Rigidity | **Rigid** | Corrects on hyperextension |
| Wedging | > 5° × 3 | None |
| Forward bend | **Sharp angulation** | Smooth |

## How it's asked

**"Not true regarding Scheuermann's?"** → **"cannot be corrected by postural control"** (TO key — read carefully; Orthobullets calls it rigid)

**"13-yr, 38° thoracic kyphosis?"** → **normal range**

**"16-yr chronic back pain, wedged vertebrae — expected finding?"** → **Schmorl's nodes**

**"15-yr athlete, mid-back pain — underlying condition?"** → **anterior wedging > 5° across three consecutive vertebrae**

**"12-yr, 55° kyphosis with pain, no deficit?"** → **Milwaukee brace** (TO)

**"Risser 2, kyphosis corrects to 50°?"** → **extension-type spinal orthosis**

**"Surgical indication?"** → **all — > 70° curve, pain + rigidity, unacceptable cosmesis** (TO)

**"Fixed 80° kyphosis, no correction on hyperextension?"** → **Smith-Petersen osteotomies + posterior arthrodesis**

**"Fusion stopped at L5 beyond SSV (L3)?"** → **protects against DJK but excessively stiff**

**"14-yr Risser 4, rigid 90° kyphosis?"** → **posterior spinal fusion + instrumentation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Spinal Cord Anatomy

*Spine · 53 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Cord overview

**Length** — \~45 cm adult · foramen magnum (continuous with medulla) → **lower border of L1** (TO)

**Conus medullaris** — **L3 at birth → L1 at maturity** · lower L1 in women, L1 pedicle in men (OB)

**Filum terminale** — **modification of pia mater** (TO) · conus → adheres to dura → inserts on **first coccygeal segment** (TO)

**Thecal sac** — dura containing CSF, roots, cauda equina

**Meninges** — dura (outer) → arachnoid → pia (inner) · **CSF in subarachnoid space** (arachnoid–pia)

### Embryology

| Structure | Becomes |
|---|---|
| **Neural tube** | Spinal cord · failed closure cranially → anencephaly · distally → spina bifida occulta, meningocele, myelomeningocele |
| **Neural crest** (dorsal to tube) | PNS, **pia mater**, spinal ganglia, sympathetic trunk |
| **Notochord** (ventral to tube) | Vertebral bodies, **nucleus pulposus** (annulus from sclerotome) |

### Autonomic

**Sympathetic chain** — **22 ganglia** (3 cervical, 11 thoracic, 4 lumbar, 4 sacral) · cervical: superior, **middle**, stellate · **middle ganglion at C6**, medial edge of **longus colli** → injury → **Horner's**

**Hypogastric plexus** — S2–S4 parasympathetic + lumbar sympathetic splanchnics

### Functional tracts

| Tract | Function |
|---|---|
| **Dorsal columns** | **Deep touch, proprioception, vibration** |
| **Lateral spinothalamic** | **Pain + temperature** · cordotomy site |
| Ventral spinothalamic | Light touch |
| **Lateral corticospinal** | Main voluntary motor · **UE fibres medial (central)** → **central cord: arms > legs** |
| Ventral corticospinal | Voluntary motor |

![Ascending tracts](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2004-ascending-tracts.jpg)

### CSF

**Production** — **choroid plexus** (3rd, 4th, lateral ventricles) · ultrafiltrate of plasma · **\~500 mL/day** · total **\~150 mL** → turned over **3–4×/day** · mechanical + immunological protection

### Blood supply

| Vessel | Supply |
|---|---|
| **Anterior spinal artery (single)** | **Anterior 2/3** incl. lateral + ventral corticospinal tracts — **predominant supply** |
| **Posterior spinal arteries (paired)** | Posterior 1/3 — **dorsal columns** |
| **Artery of Adamkiewicz** | **Largest anterior segmental (radicular) artery** · from **left posterior intercostal** · **lower 2/3 of cord** via ASA |

**Adamkiewicz level** — **left T8–L1 in 75%** (Orthobullets) · **left T9–L1** (OB, TO) · **T9–T11 in 80%** (TO, Dommisse) · range T5–L4, either side · "anywhere between T8 and T12" (TO true statement) — read the options

**Adamkiewicz injury** — **paraplegia NOT common** (TO EXCEPT) · thoracic watershed ischaemia with **sacral sparing** · ASA infarct → **anterior cord syndrome** (motor loss, preserved sensation/proprioception) · e.g. after wide **left anterolateral** corpectomy approach

**Critical vascular zone (TO)** — **T4–T9**: narrowest canal + poorest supply → paraplegia risk

**Dommisse principles (TO)** — 3 longitudinal channels (1 anterior, 2 posterolateral) · largest at **cervical + lumbar enlargements** (grey matter demand) · 2–17 anterior and 6–25 posterior medullary feeders · **vertebral arteries supply 80% of neck radicular arteries** · reversible flow like circle of Willis

![Artery of Adamkiewicz](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2004-artery-of-adamkiewicz.jpg)

### Nerve root anatomy

| Region | Exit |
|---|---|
| **Cervical** | **Above** same-numbered pedicle (C5 above C5) · horizontal · **extra C8 root** |
| Thoracic | **Below** same-numbered pedicle (T4 between T4-5) |
| Lumbar | **Below** pedicle (L5 below L5) · descend vertically |
| Intrathecal lumbar | **Exiting roots ventral**; **motor fibres ventral** to sensory |

**Why thoracic/lumbar roots lie below their vertebrae (TO)** — **7 cervical vertebrae, 8 cervical nerves** · 31 pairs total

**Vertebral artery (TO)** — through transverse foramina **C1–C6**\
✗ Wrong options: C7 (foramen present but artery usually outside)

**Saddle anaesthesia, no other LL deficit (TO)** — lesion **below S2** (S3: inner thighs, buttocks, perianal)

### Neurogenic bladder (TO)

| Lesion | Bladder |
|---|---|
| **Above pons** (micturition centre in pons, **locus coeruleus**) | **Loss of voluntary inhibition**, mild detrusor overactivity, **no DSD**, normal volume + pressure, no hypertrophy |
| **Above S2 (suprasacral cord)** | **Detrusor-sphincter dyssynergia**, high pressures → functional outlet obstruction |
| **Below S2 / pelvic nerves** | **Detrusor areflexia**, no DSD, **high volume, low pressure** |

### Neuroanatomy – brain & cranial nerves (TO)

| Topic | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Trigeminal-associated ganglia** | **Ciliary (V1), otic + pterygopalatine (V2 per TO), submandibular (V3) — all four** · trigeminal ganglion is sensory |
| Brodmann | **4 primary motor** · 6 premotor/SMA · 3,1,2 somatosensory · **17 visual** · 41,42 auditory · **44 Broca** · 8 frontal eye field · **Wernicke = 39 + 40** |
| **Internal acoustic meatus** | Transverse crest (horizontal) + **Bill's bar** (vertical, deeper) · **"7 up, Coke down"** — facial + nervus intermedius anterosuperior, **cochlear anteroinferior** (✗ Wrong options: superior), vestibular nerves posterior |
| **Vernet (jugular foramen)** | **IX, X, XI** — taste posterior 1/3, palate/pharynx/larynx, trapezius + SCM · not **tongue deviation (XII, hypoglossal canal)** |
| **Cavernous sinus** | **Inside: VI (abducens) + ICA + sympathetic plexus** · lateral wall: III, IV, V1, V2 · "OH TOM CAT" |
| Annulus of Zinn | Through: III (sup + inf), **VI**, **nasociliary**, optic nerve + ophthalmic artery · **outside: frontal, lacrimal, trochlear**, superior ophthalmic vein |
| **Facial nerve branches** | **1st = greater superficial petrosal** (geniculate) → stapedius → chorda tympani → posterior auricular, digastric, stylohyoid → 5 terminal |
| Facial muscles | Orbicularis oris/oculi, buccinator · not **lateral pterygoid (V3)** |
| Facial nerve blood | **External carotid** branches |
| Olfaction | Unmyelinated, cribriform plate, entorhinal cortex · **only sense not relaying in thalamus** (✗ Wrong options: VPL) |
| **Left optic tract** | **Right homonymous hemianopia** (temporal right + nasal left) |
| **Posterior pituitary** | **Neuroectoderm** (3rd ventricle floor) · anterior = Rathke's pouch |
| **Asterion** | Lambdoid + occipitomastoid + parietomastoid sutures |
| **Kocher's point** | **1 cm anterior to coronal suture**, 2–3 cm from midline (mid-pupillary) — EVD into frontal horn |
| **Vein of Labbé** | **Sylvian fissure (superficial middle cerebral vein) → transverse sinus** |
| **Pineal infratentorial supracerebellar approach** | Sacrifice **superior vermian + precentral cerebellar veins** · not basal vein of Rosenthal |

## X vs Y

| | Cervical | Thoracic / lumbar |
|---|---|---|
| Root vs pedicle | Above | Below |
| Reason | 7 vertebrae, 8 nerves | — |

## How it's asked

**"Spinal anatomy — EXCEPT?"** → **vertebral artery through C1–C7 foramina** (it's C1–C6) (TO)

**"Artery of Adamkiewicz — EXCEPT?"** → **paraplegia after its injury is common** (TO)

**"Critical vascular zone?"** → **T4–T9** (TO)

**"Predominant cord blood supply?"** → **anterior spinal artery (A)**

**"Motor loss with preserved sensation after left anterolateral corpectomy?"** → **left-sided artery T9–L1 (Adamkiewicz)**

**"Filum extends to?"** → **first coccygeal segment** (TO)

**"Saddle anaesthesia only?"** → **below S2** (TO)

**"Why thoracic/lumbar nerves exit below their vertebrae?"** → **7 cervical vertebrae, 8 cervical nerves** (TO)

**"Nerve between T4-5?"** → **T4** (TO)

**"Correct root–foramen grouping?"** → **cervical roots above, lumbar below their pedicle (Group D)**

**"Conus level in adults?"** → **L1**

**"Ganglia associated with trigeminal?"** → **all of the above** (TO)

**"Veins sacrificed in infratentorial supracerebellar approach?"** → **superior vermian + precentral cerebellar** (TO)

**"Brodmann 4?"** → **primary motor cortex** (TO)

**"IAM — NOT true?"** → **cochlear nerve in superior-medial compartment** (TO)

**"Vernet syndrome — FALSE?"** → **tongue deviation** (TO)

**"Wernicke's area?"** → **Brodmann 39 + 40** (TO)

**"Direct content of cavernous sinus?"** → **6th cranial nerve** (TO)

**"Posterior pituitary develops from?"** → **neuroectoderm** (TO)

**"Junction of lambdoid, occipitomastoid, parietomastoid?"** → **asterion** (TO)

**"Not through annulus of Zinn?"** → **frontal nerve** (TO)

**"Cranial nerve supplied by ECA?"** → **VII** (TO)

**"Olfaction — false?"** → **relays to VPL of thalamus** (TO)

**"Facial nerve supplies all EXCEPT?"** → **lateral pterygoid** (TO)

**"First branch of facial nerve?"** → **greater superficial petrosal nerve** (TO)

**"Left optic tract lesion?"** → **temporal right + nasal left field loss** (TO)

**"Kocher's point?"** → **1 cm anterior to coronal suture** (TO)

**"Vein of Labbé connects?"** → **Sylvian fissure to transverse sinus** (TO)

**"Urgency, detrusor overactivity, no DSD, normal pressures?"** → **lesion above the pons** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Spinal Cord Injuries

*Spine · 54 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — 11,000/yr US · **incomplete tetraplegia 34% (central cord commonest)** · complete paraplegia 25% · complete tetraplegia 22% · incomplete paraplegia 17%

**Distribution** — bimodal: young high-energy · elderly minor trauma + spondylotic stenosis · **50% cervical** · **MVA 50%**, falls, GSW · **3–25% occur after the initial trauma** (poor immobilisation/transport)

### Pathophysiology

**Primary injury** — direct, **irreversible**

**Secondary injury** — ↓ perfusion, **lipid peroxidation**, free radicals/cytokines, apoptosis → target of methylprednisolone

**Associated** — head injury, **non-contiguous spine fractures**, **vertebral artery injury** (atlas fractures, facet dislocations; usually asymptomatic unilateral; **MRA** least invasive; **stent only if basilar insufficiency symptoms**)

## 3 Presentation

### Spinal vs neurogenic vs hypovolaemic shock

| | Spinal shock | Neurogenic shock | Hypovolaemic |
|---|---|---|---|
| BP | ↓ | ↓ | ↓ |
| Pulse | **Bradycardia** | **Bradycardia** | **Tachycardia** |
| **Bulbocavernosus reflex** | **Absent** | Variable / **can be intact** | Variable |
| Motor | **Flaccid areflexic** | Variable | Variable |
| Mechanism | Neurons **hyperpolarised**, unresponsive | **Loss of sympathetic tone** (injury above **T6**), ↓ SVR, pooling, unopposed vagus | ↓ preload |
| Skin / urine | — | **Warm extremities**, urine output preserved (✗ Wrong options: **absent urine output**) | Cold |

**Bulbocavernosus reflex** — anal contraction on squeezing glans/clitoris or **tugging the Foley** · **its return = end of spinal shock** · conus/cauda equina injury → **may never return**\
✗ Wrong options: "returns in all cauda equina injuries" (TO)

**Spinal shock ruled out** — BCR present **or** 48 h elapsed

### Spinal shock phases

| Phase | Orthobullets | TO (Ditunno) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **0–48 h** areflexia | **0–24 h** areflexia; **delayed plantar** first, then BCR, abdominal, cremasteric |
| 2 | 1–2 days: **polysynaptic (BCR) return**, monosynaptic (patellar) absent | Day 1–3: cutaneous reflexes, DTRs absent |
| 3 | 1–4 wks hyperreflexia | Day 4–1 month: DTRs return, Babinski |
| 4 | 1–12 months spasticity | 1–12 months: hyperreflexia, **autonomic dysreflexia** |

**First reflex to return** — **delayed plantar** (TO) · classical teaching **bulbocavernosus** — read the options · order: delayed plantar → BCR → cremasteric → ankle jerk → Babinski → knee jerk

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### ASIA impairment scale

| Grade | Motor | Sensory |
|---|---|---|
| **A** Complete | None | None (incl. **S4-5**) |
| **B** Incomplete | **None** (none > 3 levels below NLI) | **Preserved** (incl. sacral) |
| **C** Incomplete | Preserved; **> half key muscles < grade 3** | Preserved |
| **D** Incomplete | Preserved; **≥ half key muscles ≥ grade 3** | Preserved |
| E | Normal | Normal |

![ASIA classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2006-2006-asia-classification.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria – classifying an injury

**Step 1 – neurologic level** — **lowest segment with normal motor + sensory both sides** (e.g. intact wrist extension, absent triceps → **C6**)

**Step 2 – complete?** — **no voluntary anal contraction + 0/5 distal motor + no perianal sensation + BCR intact** → complete

**Step 3 – grade** — any **perianal sensation = at least B** (sacral sparing → incomplete)

**BCR absent** → **cannot assign ASIA grade** (spinal shock) · prognosis by **repeated examinations over 48–72 h**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · SSEP · EMG

**Descriptive** — **tetraplegia** (cervical: arms, trunk, legs, pelvis) · **paraplegia** (arms spared)

**ASIA B at C6 (OB)** — deltoid 5/5, long-finger + medial forearm sensation may be present, intrinsics 0/5 · no **triceps power** (2/5 OB; 3/5 in the unkeyed TO variant)

### Incomplete cord syndromes

| Syndrome | Mechanism | Deficit | Prognosis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Central cord (commonest)** | **Hyperextension in elderly spondylotic neck** (cord pinched between body + buckled flavum) | **UE > LE**, distal > proximal (UE fibres medial), sacral sparing | Good |
| **Brown-Séquard** | Hemisection, **penetrating** | **Ipsilateral motor + proprioception/vibration**, **contralateral pain + temp** (2–3 levels below) | **Best** |
| **Anterior cord** | Hyperflexion / compression, **anterior spinal artery** (e.g. **aortic dissection**) | Motor + pain/temp lost, **proprioception preserved** | **Worst** (\~90% no recovery) |
| Posterior cord | Rare (tabes dorsalis atraumatic mimic) | Proprioception lost | — |
| Conus / cauda equina | — | — | Conus recovers better than proximal lesions |

**Rear-end collision → quadriparesis (TO)** — **extension–distraction** (whiplash mechanism)

## 5 Management

### Field & initial care

**Immobilisation** — rigid collar + spine board (transport only) · **decubiti after 30–60 min on board** · log roll · athletes: **leave helmet + shoulder pads on** until controlled simultaneous removal

**Airway** — injury **above C5** → likely intubation

**Exam** — remove collar, inspect face/scalp, rotational head posture (unilateral facet dislocation), **posterior midline tenderness** (absent in alert patient → low probability of injury), log roll, neuro exam · **seat-belt sign → flexion-distraction**

**Prevention** — DVT prophylaxis (LMWH, rotating bed, pneumatic compression) · avoid hypotension · pulmonary toilet · skin

### Neurogenic shock treatment

**TO** — IV fluids first → **vasopressors/inotropes** if persists · phenylephrine (pure α1 → reflex bradycardia) · bradycardia: **atropine**, glycopyrrolate (before suction), isoproterenol, theophylline/aminophylline

**OB** — hypotension unresponsive to initial fluids → **pressors** · Swan-Ganz-guided fluids\
✗ Wrong options: more rapid fluid → overload, CHF

### Methylprednisolone

**Current** — **not recommended**: no clear benefit, **↑ complications** (infection, GI bleed, ventilator days, LOS) · no effect on cord oedema · OB variant: "**controversial, may be given in appropriate circumstances**" — read the options

**Historical NASCIS III** — non-penetrating SCI **within 8 h** · **30 mg/kg bolus** + **5.4 mg/kg/h** for **23 h (< 3 h)** or **47 h (3–8 h)** · CI: **GSW, pregnancy, < 13 yrs, > 8 h**, peripheral nerve injury

**Presenting at 10 h** — **don't start methylprednisolone**

**GM-1** — controversial; no long-term benefit · **hypothermia** — not recommended (coagulopathy, sepsis, pneumonia, arrhythmia)

### Reduction & surgery

**Closed reduction** — **awake, alert** patient with deficit from fracture-dislocation (commonest: **bilateral facet dislocation**) · add 10 lb every 10 min with exams · **abort**: overdistraction, **neuro worsening (→ remove traction)**, failure

**TO (stable C4-5 fracture-dislocation with weakness)** — **MRI first** (cord, compression, disc)\
✗ Wrong options: immediate traction

**Decompression + stabilisation** — **most incomplete and complete SCI (except GSW)** · **early (< 24 h) improves recovery even in complete injury** · may recover 1–2 root levels · stabilise for rehab

**GSW** — **most → brace / treat extraspinal injury** (stable lateral masses, no CSF leak) · surgery for **progressive deficit with canal bullet**, **cauda equina**, **bullet in thecal sac (lead poisoning)**

**C6 SCI + spondylotic C6 fracture** — **early decompression + stabilisation alone**\
✗ Wrong options: steroids · hypothermia

### Functional level

| Level | Function |
|---|---|
| C1-3 | Ventilator; electric chair head/chin control |
| C3-4 | Ventilator → may wean; head/chin (**C4 puffer**) |
| **C5** | Deltoid, biceps · **electric wheelchair with hand control** · can't feed (no wrist extension/supination) |
| **C6** | Wrist extension + supination → feeds self · **manual wheelchair + sliding-board transfers** · drives with hand controls |
| **C7** | Triceps · **manual wheelchair + independent transfers** |
| C8-T1 | Hand dexterity · fully independent transfers |
| T2-6 | Normal UE, wheelchair |
| T7-12 | Abdominal control; bracing walk possible |
| L1-5 | Variable; aids |
| S1-5 | Walk with minimal assistance |

**Posterior deltoid → triceps transfer** — **complete C5/C6** with **5/5 deltoid + biceps, 0/5 triceps**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary nerve injury · weak deltoid

![Posterior deltoid-to-triceps transfer](../../MCQ/attachments/q1259-illa-trans.jpg)

**FES** — stimulates **skeletal muscle**

**TENS** — **presynaptic inhibition in the dorsal horn** (small myelinated afferents block C-fibre input) + endorphins

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| Pressure injury | Prevention: **frequent repositioning**, off board early · NPUAP 2016: "**injury**", **Arabic stages** (TO keys: "Grade II pressure injury" FNB 2022 · "Grade 2 pressure injury" — read the options) |
| VTE | Immediate prophylaxis |
| **Urosepsis** | **Common cause of death** |
| **Sinus bradycardia** | **Commonest arrhythmia** acutely |
| Orthostatic / supine hypotension, arrhythmia | Lost sympathetic control (**T6 or above**) · not **spasticity** (not sympathetic) |
| **Autonomic dysreflexia** | **Above T5-6** · headache, agitation, **hypertension** (SBP ↑ ≥ 20%), bradycardia, sweating · **unchecked visceral stimulus** → check **Foley**, disimpact, X-ray for **occult fracture** (swollen thigh) |
| **Major depression** | **\~11% (5–20%)** · suicidal ideation |
| Shoulder | Long-term paraplegia → **4× rotator cuff tears** |
| Post-traumatic instability | Missed adjacent ligamentous slip below construct → **revise + extend fusion** |

### Prognosis

**Complete** — **80% gain 1 root level**, 20% gain 2 · **1% full recovery** · **ASIA A: least recovery, highest lifetime cost** (ASIA B 46.6% marked recovery)

**Incomplete** — more sparing + faster early recovery = better · plateau rarely resumes

**MRI predicting complete injury** — **intramedullary cord haematoma**\
✗ Wrong options: oedema · ALL disruption · prevertebral oedema

**SCIWORA** — commoner < 10 yrs · **severity of initial deficit = best predictor**

### Dural tears (TO)

**Commonest site** — **lateral**, then root sheath, axilla · **suture whenever possible** · sealants (Tachosil, fibrin glue) if not · small unsuturable → tight fascia + skin\
✗ Wrong options: posterior

### SIADH vs cerebral salt wasting (TO)

| | SIADH | Cerebral salt wasting |
|---|---|---|
| **Volume** | **Euvolaemic / hypervolaemic** | **Hypovolaemic** |
| Serum osm | < 275 | ↓ |
| Urine osm | > 100 | — |
| Urine Na | **> 40 mEq/L** | High |
| Criteria exclude | Diuretics, thyroid/adrenal disease (✗ Wrong options: **renal insufficiency is not a criterion**) | — |
| Supplementary | Uric acid < 4, corrected by fluid restriction, ↑ ADH | — |
| Treatment | Na < 125 acute/severe → **3% saline** + furosemide · correct **≤ 8 mEq/L per 24 h** (osmotic demyelination) · chronic → **fluid restriction**, demeclocycline, conivaptan | Saline |

### Whiplash (TO)

**Mechanism** — rear impact → lower cervical **hyperextension** + upper flexion (S-shape) · commonest MVA injury (up to 83%) · "WAD"

**Poor outcome predictors** — **rapid-onset pain**, **severe early pain**, hospital admission, **radiation to arm**, **headache** · **least predictive: pain isolated to neck** (TO key)

**Management** — MRI not initially (false positives) · **early mobilisation** > soft collar

## How it's asked

**"Spinal shock — EXCEPT?"** → **BCR returns in all cauda equina injuries** (TO)

**"Cervical fracture-dislocation, no motor/sensation/anal tone, BCR intact?"** → **complete SCI** (TO)

**"Spinal shock — HR, BP, BCR?"** → **bradycardia, hypotension, absent BCR** (TO)

**"First reflex to return after spinal shock?"** → **delayed plantar** (TO)

**"ASIA B at C6 — NOT seen?"** → **triceps power** (TO unkeyed; OB: **2/5 triceps**)

**"Thoracic trauma, bradycardia, no catheter-tug response — ASIA?"** → **cannot be determined** (TO unkeyed; OB same scenario keyed)

**"Wrist extension 5/5, triceps 0/5, no sacral function, BCR intact?"** → **complete C6 (ASIA A)**

**"Grade 3 in most muscles, sensation intact, BCR intact?"** → **ASIA D**

**"No motor, diminished perianal + LE sensation?"** → **ASIA B**

**"ASIA B motor definition?"** → **no motor > 3 levels below NLI**

**"Grade 2 power in 80% of LE key muscles, sacral sensation intact?"** → **ASIA C**

**"Complete injury — early decompression?"** → **improves chance of neurologic recovery**

**"Biceps 4/5 left, deltoid 5/5, nothing below, no sacral function?"** → **complete C5**

**"No recovery at 48 h with BCR back?"** → **complete cord syndrome**

**"BCR absent — best prognostic tool?"** → **repeat physical examinations**

**"Diver, HR 57, BP 92/70, absent BCR?"** → **unable to determine ASIA**

**"Neurogenic shock — NOT seen?"** → **absent urine output** (TO)

**"Hypotension + relative bradycardia, BCR present?"** → **neurogenic shock**

**"Expected vitals in neurogenic shock?"** → **HR 55, BP 65/40**

**"Hypotension unresponsive to fluids after cervical SCI?"** → **pressors**

**"Methylprednisolone 10 h after injury?"** → **don't start**

**"Methylprednisolone in SCI — evidence?"** → **not supported; more complications, no clear benefit**

**"C6 fracture SCI — treatment?"** → **early decompression + stabilisation**

**"ASIA A C5-6 facet dislocation — steroids?"** → **controversial, may be given in appropriate circumstances**

**"Best prognosis syndrome?"** → **Brown-Séquard** (TO)

**"Central cord mechanism?"** → **hyperextension in elderly with hypertrophied facets + flavum** (TO)

**"Worst prognosis syndrome?"** → **anterior cord** (TO)

**"Rear-end crash, quadriparesis?"** → **extension–distraction** (TO)

**"82-yr hyperextension, UE weakness, LE spared?"** → **central cord syndrome**

**"Left neck GSW, Brown-Séquard?"** → **left limb weakness, right pain/temp loss**

**"LE weakness + pain/temp loss after aortic dissection, proprioception kept?"** → **anterior cord syndrome**

**"Brown-Séquard deficits?"** → **ipsilateral motor, contralateral pain + temp**

**"Right C7 pain/temp loss, left C5 motor + vibration loss?"** → **left hemisection at C5**

**"Complete C5 function?"** → **electric wheelchair with hand control**

**"Deltoid-to-triceps transfer candidate?"** → **C6 ASIA A, 5/5 biceps**

**"C6 SCI maximal function?"** → **manual wheelchair + sliding-board transfers**

**"SCIWORA — best predictor?"** → **severity of initial neurologic injury**

**"MRI predicting complete SCI?"** → **6-mm cord haematoma**

**"Lowest recovery + highest cost?"** → **ASIA A**

**"Quadriplegic sacral sore — description?"** → **Grade II / Grade 2 pressure injury** (TO — keys differ)

**"T6 lesion — NOT sympathetic loss?"** → **spasticity**

**"FES acts on?"** → **skeletal muscle**

**"MDD in SCI?"** → **5–20%**

**"Paraplegic since 14 — shoulder MRI?"** → **rotator cuff tear**

**"Slip below thoracolumbar construct on sitting films?"** → **revise + extend posterior fusion**

**"T3 paraplegic, dysreflexia, swollen thigh?"** → **occult fracture**

**"ASIA C → B during traction?"** → **remove traction**

**"Low-velocity GSW, stable, no CSF leak?"** → **treat extraspinal injuries + orthosis**

**"TENS mechanism?"** → **presynaptic inhibition in the dorsal horn**

**"C4-5 fracture-dislocation with weakness, stable — next?"** → **MRI** (TO)

**"Not an SIADH criterion?"** → **renal insufficiency** (TO)

**"SIADH vs CSW?"** → **euvolaemia** (TO)

**"Dural tears — true?"** → **suture whenever possible** (TO)

**"Least predictive of poor whiplash outcome?"** → **pain isolated to neck** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Spinal Cord Monitoring

*Spine · 55 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Modalities

| Modality | Monitors | Stimulate → record | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SSEP** | **Dorsal columns** (afferent; gracilis LE, cuneatus UE) | **Posterior tibial** (ankle) / peroneal; **ulnar/median** → **transcranial recording of somatosensory cortex** (or neck) | **25% sensitive, 100% specific** |
| **tcMEP** | **Lateral + ventral corticospinal tracts** (efferent; anterior 2/3, ASA territory) | **Transcranial motor cortex** → muscle (gastroc, soleus, EHL) | **100% sensitive, 100% specific** · **most sensitive early indicator** |
| **Spontaneous (mechanical) EMG** | Specific **nerve roots** | Surgical manipulation → muscle · **burst = contact (insignificant)** · **sustained train = significant traction/injury** | Over-sensitive |
| **Triggered (electrical) EMG** | **Pedicle breach** (medial/inferior wall → exiting root) | Stimulate screw → muscle · bone insulates | — |
| Stagnara wake-up test | Gross motor (move feet) | Intraoperative waking (amnestic) | Not continuous; delays |

![Monitored pathways and blood supply](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9023-pathways.jpg)

| | SSEP | MEP |
|---|---|---|
| Anaesthetic effect | **Unaffected / reliable** | **Highly affected** (TO trap: "MEPs not affected" = false) |
| Anterior cord ischaemia | **Unreliable** (paralysis with normal SSEP reported) | **Detects** |
| Existing motor deficit | — | **Still measurable** (TO) |

![Anterior infarct missed by SSEP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9023-senory-with-infarct.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Alert criteria

| Modality | Significant change |
|---|---|
| **SSEP** | **≥ 50% ↓ amplitude and/or ≥ 10% ↑ latency** (or loss) · not 10% amplitude / 50% latency |
| **MEP** | **> 50% ↓ amplitude** · **> 100 V ↑ threshold** (early injury) |
| **Triggered EMG** | **< 8 mA → breach** (Orthobullets, OB) · Stecker: < 10 mA inspect, **< 5 mA likely malpositioned** · **> 8 mA (e.g. 11, 12, 14 mA) = intact pedicle** |

**Lumbar (L4-5) surgery (TO)** — **triggered EMG**\
✗ Wrong options: SSEP · MEP · D-waves (cord monitors)

**Patient with existing motor deficit (TO)** — **transcranial MEPs**

## 5 Management

### Responding to signal loss

```
Significant signal change
├─ Check technical → LEADS, EQUIPMENT, interference (irrigation, cautery, fluoroscopy, warming blanket)
├─ Check anaesthesia → NO PARALYTIC / INHALATIONAL bolus, steady state
├─ Optimise physiology → RAISE MAP (80–90 TO · > 90 OB), Hb > 8, O2, reverse hypocapnia + hypothermia
├─ Surgical → REVERSE LAST MANOEUVRE (distraction/correction), REMOVE IMPLANT, widen decompression
│      (signals may take ≥ 10 min to return; close 3-column osteotomy)
└─ Still absent → STAGNARA WAKE-UP TEST
```

**TO EXCEPT** — ✗ Wrong options: **lower BP to reduce blood loss** (raising MAP recovers signals in \~50%)

**Corpectomy cage placed → 75% SSEP ↓ + MEP loss** — **remove the cage** and monitor

**Deformity correction → sustained 80% SSEP ↓, technical + anaesthetic causes excluded** — **reverse correction ± wake-up test**\
✗ Wrong options: steroids · remove all instrumentation · continue

**DMD child, diffuse 80% MEP ↓, MAP 55 on propofol** — **restore MAP > 90 and retest**\
✗ Wrong options: remove one screw (diffuse change) · switch to halogenated gas

**Most concerning in AIS correction** — **65% drop in LE MEPs**\
✗ Wrong options: 30% SSEP ↓ · 5% latency ↑ · 14 mA screw

**Most concerning in PSO** — **> 100 V MEP threshold rise**

### Reversible causes

**Commonest non-anaesthetic reversible cause** — **patient positioning** (nerve compression, plexus traction, neck alignment)\
✗ Wrong options: screw · ischaemia · retractor

**Unilateral cortical SSEP loss only during ACDF (subcortical + Erb's normal)** — **cerebral ischaemia from carotid retraction** (incomplete circle of Willis)

**Hypothermia SSEP drop (TO)** — **flood the field with warm saline** (fastest — exposed pathway)\
✗ Wrong options: warmer air, room, blankets

### Physiological effects on evoked potentials (TO)

| Variable | Effect |
|---|---|
| Cerebral blood flow | Normal to 20 · deteriorate 18–13 · **absent < 10–12 mL/100 g/min** |
| Hypotension | ↑ latency |
| ↑ ICP | ↑ latency, ↓ amplitude |
| PaO₂ | Deteriorate **< 40 mmHg** |
| Haematocrit | Latency ↑ at 10–15%; amplitude ↓ < 10% |
| PaCO₂ | Extreme hypocapnia **< 25 mmHg** |
| **Hypothermia** | **↑ latency**; no recording < 18 °C |
| Inhalational agents | Depress: **N₂O > isoflurane > halothane** |
| IV agents | **Propofol** no spinal effect (cortical depression recovers fast) · opioids, ketamine none · benzodiazepines mild |

### EMG myotomes (TO)

| Root | Muscle |
|---|---|
| C5 | Deltoid, biceps |
| C6 | Biceps, triceps, brachioradialis, PT, FCR |
| C7 | Triceps, PT, FCR, forearm extensors |
| C8 / T1 | Hand intrinsics (APB, FDI, ADM), FCU |
| L2–L4 | Iliopsoas, adductors, quadriceps |
| **L5** | **Tibialis anterior, peroneus longus** |
| **S1-2** | **Gastrocnemius, abductor hallucis** |
| S2–S5 | Anal, urethral sphincter |
| CN VII / XI / XII | Facial muscles / SCM + trapezius / tongue |

## How it's asked

**"Neuromonitoring — EXCEPT?"** → **"tcMEP unaffected by anaesthetics vs SSEP"** (TO)

**"Monitoring in a patient with existing motor deficit?"** → **transcranial MEPs** (TO)

**"AIS fusion monitoring — true?"** → **SSEPs monitor the dorsal columns**

**"SSEPs — true?"** → **unreliable for anterior cord pathways**

**"Best detector of evolving motor tract injury in cervical surgery?"** → **tcMEP**

**"Most sensitive early indicator of cord injury?"** → **transcranial MEPs**

**"SSEP alert criteria?"** → **50% ↓ amplitude, 10% ↑ latency**

**"Most concerning change during AIS correction?"** → **65% LE MEP drop**

**"Cage placed, SSEP + MEP loss?"** → **remove strut cage, monitor**

**"80% SSEP drop during correction, causes excluded?"** → **reverse correction ± wake-up test**

**"DMD, diffuse MEP drop, MAP 55?"** → **restore MAP > 90, retest**

**"PSO — most concerning change?"** → **> 100 V MEP threshold increase**

**"Isolated right cortical SSEP loss during ACDF?"** → **cerebral ischaemia from retraction**

**"Commonest non-anaesthetic reversible cause?"** → **patient positioning**

**"Signal loss — steps EXCEPT?"** → **lower BP to reduce blood loss** (TO)

**"SEP drop from hypothermia — fastest fix?"** → **warm saline into the field** (TO)

**"L3 screw stimulates at 11 mA?"** → **intact pedicle**

**"L4-5 surgery — which monitoring?"** → **triggered EMG** (TO)

# Spinal Cord Tumors

*Spine · 56 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Primary CNS tumours** — mostly intracranial · only **2–4% spinal** (850–1,700/yr US)

**Compartments** — **intradural extramedullary** (60–70%) · **intradural intramedullary** (20–30% adults, **50% children**) · extradural

![Spinal tumour compartments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2072-spinal-tumor.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

| Compartment | Symptoms |
|---|---|
| Intradural extramedullary | Cord compression, **radicular pain**, deep **back pain worst at night**, cauda equina-like |
| Intramedullary | Local/radicular pain, motor (65%), sensory (60%), **sphincter dysfunction (38%) — often first symptom** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Intradural extramedullary

| | Schwannoma | Meningioma |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Commonest intradural tumour** (with meningioma) | **25%** of primary spinal tumours |
| Demographics | **40s–50s, M = F** | **Women 80%**, 5th–6th decades |
| Site | **Dorsal nerve root** · cervical 31%, cauda equina 24%, thoracic 22% | **Thoracic** |
| Risk | **NF2** | **NF2**, prior radiation |
| MRI | T1 iso, **T2 hyperintense** | **Dura-based**, T1 iso/hypo, T2 slightly hyper, homogeneous enhancement |
| Histology | **Antoni A (hypercellular) + B (hypocellular)**, **S-100+** | Lobulated, **meningothelial whorls** |
| Treatment | Resection ± RT if malignant | Resection (curative if complete); RT for recurrence |

![Schwannoma from dorsal root](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2072-schwannoma.jpg)

### Intramedullary

| | Ependymoma | Astrocytoma |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Commonest intramedullary (adults)** (Orthobullets) | **Commonest in children (80–90%)** · **commonest overall** (TO) |
| Age | Adults | Peak **3rd decade** |
| Site | **Cervical** commonest spinal site (TO) · myxopapillary in **filum terminale** | **Cervicothoracic** junction (children) |
| Types | Myxopapillary 40–50%, cellular 42% (benign) · anaplastic | 30% benign |
| MRI | T2 hyper, heterogeneous · myxopapillary: **scalloping, foraminal enlargement, scoliosis, sacral seeding** · cellular: **polar cysts**, haemorrhage | **Fusiform, irregular margins**, infiltrative |
| Histology | **Rosettes + pseudorosettes** | Hypercellular, mitoses, **eosinophilic granules** |
| Treatment | **Gross total resection** — good prognosis (clean plane) | GTR difficult → **RT** ± chemo |

**TO** — paediatric ependymoma intracranial (**4th ventricle**); adult spinal

![Ependymoma polar cyst](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2072-polar-cyst.jpg)

### Extradural & metastatic

**Metastasis** — extramedullary = advancing disease · **drop metastases** via CSF · intramedullary rare (< 1%) from **lung > breast**, cervical + conus · **intramedullary mets → life expectancy < 3 months** · RT/chemo

**GBM drop metastasis (TO)** — **intradural extramedullary** · 1–2% of GBM · CSF spread (debulking ↑ seeding) · lag \~8.5 months · steroids for rapid cord compression

**Lymphoma** — image whole neuraxis · T2 ill-defined hyperintense, marked homogeneous enhancement, little cord enlargement · **cervical** · **methotrexate**

## 5 Management

### Metastatic cord compression

**Frameworks** — **SINS** (instability) + **NOMS** (neurologic, oncologic/radiosensitivity, mechanical, systemic) · **Tokuhashi 9–11 → 6–12 months**

**Renal cell metastasis with anterior compression, myelopathy** — **pre-op embolisation → corpectomy + instrumented fusion → post-op RT**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior decompression alone · surgery without embolisation · pre-op RT

**Unkeyed TO item** — structure spared and cut last during intramedullary tumour removal (denticulate ligament / midline myelotomy / functional root / DRG) — answer not provided by source

### Brain tumour facts (TO)

| Fact | Answer |
|---|---|
| Commonest brain tumour | **Metastases** (> 50%) |
| Commonest primary (adult CNS) | **GBM** |
| Commonest primary non-glial | **Meningioma** |
| Commonest paediatric CNS tumour | **Astrocytoma** (\~50%), then medulloblastoma |
| Commonest paediatric brain malignancy / vermian tumour | **Medulloblastoma** |
| **Brainstem glioma** presentation | **Cranial neuropathies first** (then weakness/ataxia; headache, vomiting late) · 77% < 20 yrs |
| **Petroclival meningioma** | Feeders from **ICA dural branches (meningohypophyseal trunk)** · PVA particles not more complications · liquid agents (NBCA, Onyx) infrequent |
| **Arachnoid cyst** | Commonest **Sylvian fissure** (middle fossa) · M:F 4:1, left · EMA+, CEA− · middle fossa cysts bleed (bridging veins) · suprasellar → hydrocephalus, precocious puberty, **bobble-head doll** · incidental adult → 1 repeat scan at 6–8 months |

## How it's asked

**"Renal cell met with cord compression, 6–12 months expected?"** → **embolisation, corpectomy + fusion, post-op RT**

**"Commonest site for spinal ependymoma?"** → **cervical** (TO)

**"Commonest intramedullary tumour?"** → **astrocytoma** (TO — overall; ependymoma in adults)

**"GBM drop metastases?"** → **intradural extramedullary** (TO)

**"Petroclival meningioma — true?"** → **feeders from internal carotid artery** (TO)

**"Brainstem glioma first presents with?"** → **cranial neuropathies** (TO)

**"Commonest arachnoid cyst location?"** → **Sylvian fissure** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Spinal Epidural Abscess

*Spine · 57 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — pus / granulation tissue between **dura and epidural fat**

**Patients** — usually **> 60 yrs** · 2–10 per 10,000 admissions (TO) · location usually **dorsal thoracolumbar** · cervical SEA commoner in **IVDU injecting arms/neck** (TO)

**Risk factors** — **IVDU**, immunodeficiency, HIV, malignancy, immunosuppressants, **recent spinal procedure**, diabetes, endocarditis

### Pathogenesis

**Routes** — **haematogenous \~50%** (commonest non-iatrogenic) · contiguous discitis \~33% · direct inoculation

**Nidus (TO)** — **skin** commonest\
✗ Wrong options: respiratory

**Organisms** — **Staph aureus 50–65%** · gram-negatives (E. coli 18%) · **Pseudomonas in IVDU**

**Association** — **spondylodiscitis** (SEA in \~18% of spondylodiscitis)

**Neurologic** — deficit in **33%** · permanent paralysis **4–22%** (compression or cord infarction) · risk highest cervical, lowest lumbar (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **severe insidious pain (87%)** · **fever only \~50%** · sicker than osteomyelitis · **rapid neuro progression (days)** (TO) · meningism, radicular pain · sequence: back ache → root pain → weakness → paralysis

**Exam** — radiculopathy or myelopathy (clonus, Hoffmann → look above)

### Labs

| Marker | Fact |
|---|---|
| WBC | Mean 22,000; raised in only **\~42%** |
| **ESR** | Raised **> 90%** |
| **CRP** | Raised **90%** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| X-ray | Usually normal |
| CT | Poor sensitivity |
| CT myelogram | 90% sensitive, invasive |
| **MRI + gadolinium** | **Study of choice** · **ring-enhancing lesion = pathognomonic**, T2 bright · pus vs CSF · extent, osteomyelitis, compression |

![Cervical epidural abscess on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2026-cervical-moved.jpg)

### Whole-spine MRI (skip lesions)

| Indication |
|---|
| Concomitant infection outside the spine |
| **Delayed presentation > 7 days** (strongest predictor among listed options ; ✗ Wrong options: IVDU · diabetes · CRP · fever) |
| **ESR > 95 mm/h** |
| **Neuro signs not explained by the lesion found** (e.g. Hoffmann with lumbar SEA; clonus with L2-3 discitis) |

## 5 Management

```
Spinal epidural abscess
├─ Neuro intact, small abscess, minimal compression, can be observed → ADMIT, IV ANTIBIOTICS ± BRACE
│      (also non-surgical candidates)
└─ Deficit / cord compression / failure risk / persistent infection / instability → SURGICAL DECOMPRESSION ± FUSION
       ├─ Posterior abscess, no discitis → LAMINECTOMY (avoid wide facetectomy)
       └─ Anterior abscess / spondylodiscitis → ANTERIOR DEBRIDEMENT + STRUT ± POSTERIOR FIXATION
```

### Failure of medical treatment

| Predictor | Note |
|---|---|
| **Neurologic deficit** | **Strongest predictor** |
| **Diabetes** | — |
| **CRP > 115 mg/L** | — |
| **WBC > 12–12.5 k** | — |
| **Positive blood cultures** | — |
| **Age > 65** | — |
| **MRSA** | — |
| No | Smoking, IVDU |

**> 40%** of medically managed patients fail (pain, deficit)

**Neuro-intact 53-yr, MSSA bacteraemia** — **admit, brace, IV cefazolin 6 wks** · serial exams\
✗ Wrong options: discharge on oral linezolid · laminectomy

**Most likely to need surgery** — **67-yr diabetic, leg weakness, MRSA bacteraemia** (all risk factors)

### Operative

**Indications** — **neuro deficit**, cord compression on imaging, failure risk factors (> 65, diabetes, new deficit, MRSA), persistent infection, deformity/instability

**Antibiotics after surgery** — **2–4 wks if no bone involvement** · **6 wks if bony involvement**

**Cervical anterior abscess + deficit (IVDU)** — **anterior decompression + fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior · steroids · antibiotics alone

**Cervical spondylodiscitis C5-7 + kyphosis + deficit + sepsis** — **anterior debridement, corpectomy, fusion + posterior instrumentation** (instrument despite infection)

**Lumbar 3-level destruction + abscess after endocarditis** — **I&D, corpectomy, fusion**

**Antibiotic timing** — hold until intraoperative cultures if stable (OB 213125)

### Prognosis

**Best predictor** — **pre-operative neuro deficit** · mortality \~5% · early diagnosis key · only 18% with frank abscess / 23% with paralysis fully recover

### Intracranial infections (TO)

**Brain abscess** — **congenital cyanotic heart disease**, endocarditis, lung, sinusitis (**frontal/ethmoid → frontal lobe**), otitis, trauma · overall **Streptococcus**; trauma S. aureus; transplant fungi; post-neurosurgery S. epidermidis/aureus · stages: early cerebritis 1–3 d → late cerebritis 4–9 d → early capsule 10–13 d → late capsule > 14 d · IV antibiotics 6–8 wks then oral · medical alone if **< 3 cm, cerebritis stage, < 2 wks** symptoms

**Neurocysticercosis** — **Taenia solium** larvae (pork / contaminated vegetables) · **commonest CNS parasite**, commonest acquired epilepsy in low-income countries · C. cellulosae: 5–15 mm transparent cyst with **scolex** · stages: vesicular → colloidal → granular nodular → calcific · **seizures** commonest · **EITB serology** \~100% specific · **MRI choice** (scolex pathognomonic) · treatment: **albendazole** + steroids (start before) + antiepileptics ± surgery/shunt

### Pneumorrachis (TO)

**Definition** — **air in the spinal canal** (traumatic, iatrogenic, spontaneous) · mostly cervical · from pneumothorax/pneumomediastinum tracking via retropharynx

**Gas from necrotising fasciitis / emphysematous osteomyelitis** — hypointense epidural lesion · CT best (can't separate air from gas) · usually resolves conservatively

## How it's asked

**"53-yr neuro-intact SEA, MSSA?"** → **admission, bracing, IV cefazolin 6 wks**

**"Most likely to need surgery?"** → **67-yr diabetic, leg weakness, MRSA bacteraemia**

**"Strongest predictor of failure in IVDU with weakness?"** → **lower extremity weakness**

**"Lumbar SEA + positive Hoffmann — next?"** → **MRI cervical + thoracic spine**

**"IVDU, diffuse weakness, anterior cervical abscess?"** → **anterior decompression + fusion**

**"Post-endocarditis, 3-level lumbar destruction, weakness?"** → **I&D, corpectomy, fusion**

**"C5-7 spondylodiscitis, kyphosis, deficit, sepsis?"** → **anterior debridement, corpectomy, fusion + posterior instrumentation**

**"Cervical anterior abscess, 1 month fevers, weakness?"** → **anterior decompression + fusion**

**"SEA — EXCEPT?"** → **commonest nidus is respiratory infection** (TO — it's skin)

**"Post-endocarditis back pain, clonus — imaging?"** → **thoracic ring-enhancing epidural collection (Figure B)**

**"L2-3 discitis, neuro signs above — next?"** → **MRI of the entire spine**

**"Factor predicting skip lesions?"** → **duration of symptoms (≥ 7 days)**

**"Large fronto-parietal lesion + congenital heart disease?"** → **brain abscess** (TO)

**"Multiple white transparent cysts?"** → **neurocysticercosis** (TO)

**"Necrotising fasciitis, hypointense epidural lesion?"** → **pneumorrachis** (TO)

# Spinal Tuberculosis

*Spine · 58 / 69*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Pott's disease** — M. tuberculosis spondylitis · ↑ with immunocompromise (**HIV, CD4 50–200**)

**Frequency** — 15% of TB extrapulmonary · **spine = commonest extrapulmonary site** (thoracic) · 5% of all TB

**Level (TO)** — **lower thoracic** > lumbar > upper thoracic > cervical > sacral · children: **anterior lower thoracic** (OB)

### Pathoanatomy

**Seeding** — haematogenous to end-arterioles in the **anterior vertebral body next to the endplate (metaphysis)** · **commonest pattern: anterior** (TO key) · spreads **under the ALL** → multilevel, **skip lesions (15%)**, **paraspinal abscess (50%)** — large, anterior (≫ pyogenic)

**Disc** — **spared early** (vs pyogenic disc-first) → may mimic tumour

**Aneurysmal phenomenon (TO)** — **long-standing abscess** → concave anterior scalloping of bodies · thoracic abscess = **bird-nest shadow**

**End result in spine (TO)** — **bony ankylosis** (weight-bearing + cancellous ends) vs fibrous in limb joints

### Kyphosis

**Adults** — static after healing · mean 15° non-op; > 60° in 5%

**Children** — **progresses in 40%** after healing (growth) · **anterior + posterior element involvement = best predictor of progression**\
✗ Wrong options: age · level count · bracing timing; thoracic-only does better than thoracolumbar

| Rajasekaran progression | Course |
|---|---|
| **Type I** | ↑ until growth stops → **surgery** |
| Type II | Decreasing progression with growth |
| Type III | Minimal change |

## 3 Presentation

**Onset** — insidious · malaise, **night sweats**, weight loss · back pain often late

**Earliest (TO)** — symptom **back pain** · sign **paraspinal muscle spasm** · first neuro sign **ankle clonus** (motor before sensory — anterior disease near motor tracts), then extensor plantar

**Neuro deficit** — **10–47%** · abscess, granulation, debris, caseation, instability, **ossified flavum** near severe kyphosis · upper thoracic commonest (narrow canal) · Pott's paraplegia 10–30% (TO)

### Pott's paraplegia (TO)

| | Early onset | Late onset |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | **Active disease, < 2 yrs** | **> 2 yrs**, healed disease |
| Cause | Oedema, granulation, abscess, caseation, ischaemia | **Deformity** (internal gibbus), debris, sequestra, stenosis |
| Prognosis | **Better** | Worse |
| Treatment | ATT ± decompression | Deformity correction + stabilisation |

**Commonest cause of late-onset paraplegia** — **deformity** (TO)

**Prognosis by cause (Orthobullets)** — abscess / bony sequestra better than **meningomyelitis**

| Modified Tuli stage | Findings |
|---|---|
| 1 | Subtle — clonus, brisk reflexes, Babinski |
| 2 | UMN deficit + spasticity, **ambulatory** |
| 3 | **Bedridden**, spastic, lateral column sensory loss |
| 4 | Bedridden, severe sensory loss (posterior + lateral), sores |
| 5 | Stage 4 + **bladder/bowel**, flexor spasms, flaccidity |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| **CXR** | Abnormal in **66%** — always obtain |
| X-ray early | Anterior body involvement, **disc preserved** |
| X-ray late | Collapse, disc destruction, kyphosis |
| **MRI + gadolinium** | **Preferred** · T1 low, T2 high · **septated smooth-walled para/prevertebral abscess with subligamentous spread** · multilevel 16–70% · endplate disruption (sens 100%, spec 81%), paravertebral shadow (97/85) · cord oedema, myelomalacia, syrinx |
| CT | Lesions < 1.5 cm; fragmentary, osteolytic, subperiosteal, sclerotic destruction · poor for epidural extent |
| Tc + gallium scan | Highest sensitivity for infection |

![Subligamentous TB abscess on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2027-abscess.jpg)

**Disc-confined lumbar abscess** — pyogenic, not TB (TO)

### Spine-at-risk signs (Rajasekaran)

| ✓ Sign |
|---|
| **Facet subluxation / separation** at apex |
| **Retropulsion** (posterior displacement) |
| **Lateral translation** |
| **Toppling** |
| ✗ Wrong options: kyphosis angle (> 50°, > 55°) · abscess · neuro worsening |

![Spine-at-risk signs](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob2027-at-risk.jpg)

### Labs & tissue

| Test | Fact |
|---|---|
| CBC | Relative lymphocytosis, low Hb |
| ESR | Usually ↑ (normal in 25%) |
| **PPD** | **+ in \~80%** |
| **CT-guided biopsy** | **Most specific test (TO)** · **AFB** stain (smear + 52%), culture + 83% (**up to 10 wks**), **PCR** 95% sensitive |
| No | **Empirical antibiotics without biopsy** (TO) |

**Differential** — Actinomyces, Nocardia, **Brucella**, fungi (Coccidioides, Blastomyces, Cryptococcus, Aspergillus), syphilis

### Pyogenic vs TB (TO)

| Treat as pyogenic | Treat as TB |
|---|---|
| Acute, septic, severe pain | Subacute / chronic |
| **Lumbar, disc-predominant**, no big abscess | **Skip / multiple lesions** |
| Older, immunocompromised | **Paraspinal abscess** |
| — | **Thoracic / thoracolumbar** |

## 5 Management

### Medical

**Regimen** — **HRZE 2 months → HR 9–18 months** (Orthobullets) · **HRZE 2 months → HRE ≥ 10 months, total 12–16 months, daily** (TO, India) · with surgery: continue H, R, Z

**HIV + cervical Pott's with Ziehl-Neelsen +** — **isoniazid, rifampin, pyrazinamide, streptomycin**

**Orthosis** — pain + deformity prevention · **steroids only for arachnoiditis/meningitis** (TO)

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Neuro deficit** | Worsening deficit, acute severe paraplegia, **not responding to ≥ 3 wks ATT** (TO) |
| Instability | Panvertebral disease ± subluxation · **Ganga score > 3**, **≥ 2 spine-at-risk signs** (TO) |
| **Kyphosis** | **> 60° adults** (Orthobullets) · **thoracic > 40°, lumbar > 30°** (TO) · **progressive in a child** · ≥ 3 vertebrae with loss of ≥ 1.5 bodies (thoracic) · child ≤ 7 with ≥ 3 bodies + ≥ 2 at-risk signs |
| Late-onset paraplegia | Kyphosis correction |
| Others | **Caseation** blocking drugs, failure after 3–6 months, uncertain diagnosis, > 4 levels (TO), large abscess |

**Benefits** — less kyphosis, faster healing, fewer sinuses, better neuro recovery · implant colonisation rarer than pyogenic · autograft or allograft struts fine

### Approach

| Situation | Approach |
|---|---|
| Classic | **Anterior decompression / corpectomy + strut ± posterior instrumentation ± posterior shortening** (single-stage transpedicular, two-stage, extrapleural anterolateral) |
| **TO modern** | **Posterior / transpedicular** preferred (lung already involved; diseased body poor for screws) · **posterior abscess (e.g. D6-7) → posterior laminectomy** · lumbar: anterior abscess drainable via laminectomy (dura retractable) |
| **TO progressive deficit, T7-9, no paravertebral collection** | **Posterior decompression + stabilisation** |
| **TO worsening paraplegia + bladder after 3 wks ATT** | **Decompression + evaluate for MDR-TB** (✗ Wrong options: add streptomycin · steroids · repeat MRI) |
| Cervical kyphosis | Halo traction → anterior decompression, graft, plate |
| Lumbar kyphosis | **Pedicle subtraction osteotomy** |
| Healed thoracic/TL kyphosis | **Internal kyphectomy** (cord moved anteriorly) |
| Transthoracic | **Left side** (aorta easier than IVC) |
| Atypical TB (granuloma) | Laminectomy (extradural/subdural) · myelotomy (intramedullary) |

**Rajasekaran (TO)** — active disease: posterior stabilisation then anterior debridement + graft · healed rigid deformity: anterior first then posterior

### Drug-resistant TB (TO)

**Rif-resistant on Xpert** — next **second-line LPA / DST for fluoroquinolone resistance** (MDR vs pre-XDR) · XDR work-up: SL-LPA + **gene sequencing** (Bdq/Lzd)

| WHO 2022 group | Drugs |
|---|---|
| **A (all three)** | **Levofloxacin/moxifloxacin, bedaquiline, linezolid** |
| B | Clofazimine, cycloserine/terizidone |
| C | Ethambutol, delamanid, pyrazinamide, amikacin/streptomycin, ethionamide |

**Regimens** — shorter all-oral **9–12 months** (FQ-sensitive) · longer **18–20 months** · **BPaLM** 6 months · injectables last resort · never add a single drug to a failing regimen

**XDR (2021)** — MDR + FQ resistance + bedaquiline or linezolid resistance

**Monitoring** — **bedaquiline → QTc (ECG)** · **linezolid → myelosuppression (CBC)**

## 6 Complications

**Deformity** — highest after **anterior graft alone** (graft slips/breaks, ≥ 2 levels) · lowest with **anterior + posterior fusion**

**Others** — retropharyngeal abscess (dysphagia, hoarseness), TB arteritis/pseudoaneurysm, costopelvic impingement, **sinus formation**, Pott's paraplegia

**Atypical spinal TB** — compressive myelopathy **without visible deformity** · intraspinal granuloma, neural arch disease, concentric collapse, sclerotic bridging

**Spina ventosa (TO)** — **tubercular dactylitis**: phalanges of the hand, destruction + new bone, **spindle-shaped finger swelling**

## How it's asked

**"Aneurysmal phenomenon in dorsal Pott's?"** → **long-standing abscess** (TO)

**"MRI NOT suggestive of TB?"** → **abscess confined to lumbar disc space** (TO)

**"Commonest TB spine pattern?"** → **anterior** (TO)

**"End result of TB spine?"** → **bony ankylosis** (TO)

**"9-yr gibbus — true?"** → **vertebral body involved, disc spared**

**"Commonest site in children?"** → **anterior lower thoracic**

**"Early finding in Pott's?"** → **preserved disc space**

**"Late-onset paraplegia cause?"** → **deformity** (TO)

**"T7-9 lesion, progressive deficit, no collection?"** → **posterior decompression + stabilisation** (TO)

**"Worsening paraplegia + bladder after 3 wks ATT?"** → **decompression + evaluate for MDR TB** (TO)

**"D6-7 posterior abscess failing ATT — approach?"** → **posterior laminectomy** (TO)

**"HIV, cervical Pott's, ZN positive — drugs?"** → **INH, rifampin, PZA, streptomycin**

**"Predicts kyphosis progression after healing in child?"** → **anterior + posterior element involvement**

**"Xpert: rifampicin resistance — next?"** → **second-line LPA / DST for fluoroquinolones** (TO)

**"Group A drugs?"** → **bedaquiline, linezolid, fluoroquinolone** (TO)

**"Monitoring on bedaquiline + linezolid?"** → **ECG QTc + weekly CBC** (TO)

**"Tool for FQ + Group A resistance?"** → **SL-LPA + targeted gene sequencing** (TO)

**"Spine-at-risk EXCEPT?"** → **kyphosis > 55° / > 50°** (TO)

**"Spine-at-risk sign?"** → **facet subluxation** (TO)

**"First neurological sign?"** → **ankle clonus** (TO)

**"Earliest sign of Pott's?"** → **paraspinal muscle spasm** (TO)

**"Most specific test for spinal infection?"** → **biopsy** (TO)

**"Spina ventosa?"** → **tubercular dactylitis** (TO)
